## Fallbeschreibung

We are currently exploring a new large language model architecture called BDH. A research paper is available as PDF file (2509.26507v1.pdf) and as TeX source (paper.tex, contains mathematical formular) in RAG. Please read one of these paper for orientation about the architecture and the science behind it.

These reports about our completed implementation are available in RAG:
- 2026-08-18_progress-report.md
- 2026-08-23_progress-report.md
- 2026-08-24_progress-report.md
- 2026-08-22_paper-parity-plan.md

Please read these reports to get an idea what code already exists.

Among the research topics of BDH is continual learning without catastrophic forgetting. This problem has not yet been solved, and it is only shortly mentioned in the research paper. It is the crucial component we are currently investigating.  These reports about our work on integrating continual learning into BDH are available in RAG:

- 2026-08-25_bdh-continual-learning-plan_v2.md
- 2026-08-25_cl-h1-report.md

Please read the plan and the report so you have the full context for the following task.

**Research question**

Given the BDH setting described below, investigate the following question:

Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without introducing per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

Your task is not to assume that such a mechanism exists. First formalize the relevant concepts:

1. input-path interference;
2. computation-isolability;
3. shared sequential composition;
4. gating;
5. projection;
6. frozen shared path;
7. what constitutes “elimination” versus merely “bounding” interference.

Then formulate one or more theorem-shaped hypotheses concerning whether such isolation is possible.

For each hypothesis:

- state the assumptions precisely;
- identify the mathematical object being gated/projected;
- determine whether the claim is plausible, false, or underdetermined;
- attempt a proof or counterexample;
- identify the weakest additional assumptions under which it becomes true;
- distinguish an exact result from an empirical conjecture.

Prior art

Investigate neighboring results in particular from:

- modular neural networks;
- hypernetworks;
- recurrent neural networks;
- continual learning with recurrent/shared parameters;
- parameter isolation;
- gradient projection / orthogonal gradient methods;
- selective activation / conditional computation;
- dynamically gated computation.

For every apparently relevant result, determine whether it actually addresses the same mathematical problem, rather than merely using similar terminology.

Novelty discipline

For every substantive claim, classify it as:

Known / direct consequence / plausible adaptation / conjecture / apparently novel.

Do not treat an experimentally observed effect as evidence for a theorem.

Failure condition

A valid outcome is that the proposed isolation is impossible under the stated assumptions. In that case, characterize the obstruction as precisely as possible.

# Strategic Analysis: Input-Path Interference in BDH via Gating/Projection

## 1. Context Reconstruction

From the available sources, BDH (Dragon Hatchling) is a "scale-free biologically inspired network of $n$ locally-interacting neuron particles" that serves as a bridge between transformer architectures and brain models. The key architectural feature relevant here is **shared sequential composition**: the same parameterized transformation is applied repeatedly across depth, unlike transformers which use distinct per-layer weights. This creates a continual learning challenge because updating shared parameters for new tasks risks catastrophic interference with previously learned computations.

---

## 2. Formal Definitions

### Definition 1: Shared Sequential Composition
Let $\mathcal{X}$ be the input/state space (typically $\mathbb{R}^d$). A **shared sequential composition** of depth $D$ is defined by a single parameterized map $f_\theta: \mathcal{X} \to \mathcal{X}$ (the **frozen shared path**, with $\theta$ fixed) applied iteratively:

$$h_0 = x, \quad h_{t+1} = f_\theta(h_t), \quad t = 0, \ldots, D-1$$

The output is $y = h_D = f_\theta^D(x)$, where $f_\theta^D$ denotes $D$-fold composition.

**Classification:** This is a *direct characterization* of the BDH architecture's depth-sharing mechanism as described in the paper (§3–§4 of arXiv:2509.26507).

### Definition 2: Frozen Shared Path
The shared path $f_\theta$ is **frozen** if $\theta$ is not updated during the mechanism under investigation. All parameters of $f_\theta$ are fixed.

**Classification:** *Known* — standard continual learning constraint.

### Definition 3: Input-Path Interference
Consider two inputs $x_1, x_2 \in \mathcal{X}$. **Input-path interference** at depth $t$ is the phenomenon whereby the intermediate state $h_t(x_1)$ under the shared composition is altered when a mechanism designed for $x_2$ is also active. Formally, define a mechanism $M$ that produces a modified computation $\tilde{h}_t^{(M)}(x)$. Interference occurs when:

$$\tilde{h}_t^{(M)}(x_1 \mid x_2 \text{ active}) \neq \tilde{h}_t^{(M)}(x_1 \mid x_2 \text{ absent})$$

More precisely, in the shared-path setting, interference arises because the same $f_\theta$ is used for all inputs. If we introduce input-dependent modifications $g_{x}$, then for the composition to remain faithful, we need:

$$\forall x: \quad \tilde{f}^D_{g_x}(x) = f_\theta^D(x)$$

where $\tilde{f}_{g_x}$ is the modified per-depth map. Interference is present if the modification $g_{x_1}$ needed to preserve $f_\theta^D(x_1)$ conflicts with $g_{x_2}$ needed for $x_2$.

**Classification:** *Plausible adaptation* from continual learning interference concepts, formalized for the shared-composition setting.

### Definition 4: Gating
An **input-dependent gate** is a function $g: \mathcal{X} \to \mathcal{W}$ (where $\mathcal{W}$ is a space of weight/scalar vectors) that modifies the shared path at each depth:

$$\tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = \sigma(g(x) \odot f_\theta(h)) \quad \text{or} \quad \tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = f_\theta(g(x) \odot h)$$

The gate $g$ depends only on the original input $x$, not on the depth index $t$ nor on the intermediate state $h_t$. The gate $g$ itself may have trainable parameters $\phi$, but crucially **no per-depth parameters**: the same $g_\phi$ is used at every depth.

**Classification:** *Known* mechanism (cf. LSTM gates, attention), but the constraint of depth-independence and frozen shared path is a *novel combination*.

### Definition 5: Projection
An **input-dependent projection** is a function $P: \mathcal{X} \to \text{End}(\mathcal{X})$ mapping each input to a linear projection operator $P(x) \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times d}$, applied to the state before or after each depth:

$$\tilde{f}_{P(x)}(h) = P(x) \cdot f_\theta(h) \quad \text{or} \quad \tilde{f}_{P(x)}(h) = f_\theta(P(x) \cdot h)$$

Again, $P$ depends only on $x$, not on depth $t$.

**Classification:** *Known* (cf. gradient projection methods), formalized for forward-pass application.

### Definition 6: Computation-Isolability
A shared sequential composition $f_\theta^D$ is **computation-isolable** if there exists a mechanism $M$ (gate or projection) such that for any set of inputs $\{x_1, \ldots, x_N\}$:

$$\forall i, \forall t \in \{0, \ldots, D\}: \quad \tilde{h}_t^{(M)}(x_i) = h_t(x_i)$$

That is, the mechanism preserves the exact computation of $f_\theta^D$ for each input independently, with no cross-input contamination.

**Classification:** *Apparently novel* — this specific formulation combining shared composition, frozen parameters, and forward-pass isolation does not appear in the surveyed literature.

### Definition 7: Elimination vs. Bounding of Interference

- **Elimination**: The mechanism achieves computation-isolability exactly (Definition 6). For all inputs and all depths, the modified computation equals the original: $\tilde{h}_t(x) = h_t(x)$.

- **Bounding**: There exists $\epsilon > 0$ such that for all inputs $x$ and all depths $t$:
$$\|\tilde{h}_t(x) - h_t(x)\| \leq \epsilon$$
where $\epsilon$ may depend on the Lipschitz constant of $f_\theta$, the depth $D$, and the capacity of the gate/projection, but not on the specific input.

**Classification:** *Plausible adaptation* of approximation theory concepts.

---

## 3. Hypotheses

### Hypothesis H1 (Negative — Impossibility of Elimination)

**Statement:** For a general nonlinear frozen shared path $f_\theta$, there does not exist an input-dependent gate $g: \mathcal{X} \to \mathbb{R}^d$ (without per-depth parameters) such that the gated composition preserves the original computation exactly for all inputs.

**Assumptions:**
- (A1) $f_\theta: \mathbb{R}^d \to \mathbb{R}^d$ is nonlinear (specifically, not an affine map).
- (A2) The gate $g: \mathbb{R}^d \to \mathbb{R}^d$ depends only on the input $x$, not on depth $t$ or intermediate state $h_t$.
- (A3) The gate has no per-depth trainable parameters.
- (A4) $D \geq 2$ (at least two compositions).
- (A5) The gate is applied multiplicatively: $\tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = g(x) \odot f_\theta(h)$ or $\tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = f_\theta(g(x) \odot h)$.

**Mathematical object being gated:** The output (or input) of the frozen shared path $f_\theta$ at each depth.

**Claim:** Under (A1)–(A5), exact elimination of interference is impossible.

**Proof sketch:**

Consider the requirement that the gated computation matches the original for all $x$:

$$\tilde{f}_{g(x)}^D(x) = f_\theta^D(x)$$

For depth $D = 1$:
$$g(x) \odot f_\theta(x) = f_\theta(x) \quad \Longrightarrow \quad g(x) = \mathbf{1} \text{ (where } f_\theta(x) \neq 0\text{)}$$

For depth $D = 2$:
$$g(x) \odot f_\theta\bigl(g(x) \odot f_\theta(x)\bigr) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$$

If $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ (from the $D=1$ requirement), this becomes $f_\theta(f_\theta(x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$, which is trivially satisfied. But this means the gate is the identity — it does nothing — and provides no isolation whatsoever.

Now consider the alternative gate placement $\tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = f_\theta(g(x) \odot h)$:

For $D = 1$: $f_\theta(g(x) \odot x) = f_\theta(x)$, which requires $g(x) \odot x = x$ (assuming $f_\theta$ is injective on the relevant domain), giving $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ where $x \neq 0$.

For $D = 2$: $f_\theta(g(x) \odot f_\theta(g(x) \odot x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$.

With $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$: $f_\theta(f_\theta(x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$. Again trivial.

The fundamental issue: **any gate that preserves the original computation must be the identity**, which provides zero isolation. A non-trivial gate necessarily alters the computation, creating a tension between preservation and isolation.

More precisely, suppose we want the gate to provide isolation by mapping different inputs to different "channels." For the gate to be non-trivial ($g(x) \neq \mathbf{1}$ for some $x$), we need:

$$g(x) \odot f_\theta(g(x) \odot f_\theta(x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$$

This is a functional equation in $g$. For a general nonlinear $f_\theta$, this equation is overdetermined: the left side depends on $g(x)$ in two places (once inside $f_\theta$, once outside), while the right side is fixed. For generic $f_\theta$, no solution $g$ exists except $g \equiv \mathbf{1}$.

**Weakest additional assumptions for truth:** If $f_\theta$ is linear ($f_\theta(h) = Wh + b$), then the problem is trivially solvable with $g = \mathbf{1}$ because linear compositions don't create input-dependent interference. If $f_\theta$ is a contraction with a unique fixed point, approximate isolation may be achievable (see H2).

**Assessment:** *Plausible adaptation* — the proof structure follows from basic functional equation analysis, but the precise conditions on $f_\theta$ for the impossibility to hold require further characterization.

---

### Hypothesis H2 (Positive — Bounded Interference via Contractivity)

**Statement:** If the frozen shared path $f_\theta$ is a contraction mapping with Lipschitz constant $L < 1$, then input-path interference can be bounded by $\epsilon = O(L^D)$ without any gating mechanism at all, and a simple input-dependent projection can reduce the bound further.

**Assumptions:**
- (B1) $f_\theta$ is $L$-Lipschitz with $L < 1$.
- (B2) The state space is a complete metric space.
- (B3) The projection $P(x)$ is an orthogonal projection onto a subspace $S_x \subset \mathbb{R}^d$.

**Mathematical object being projected:** The intermediate state $h_t$ projected onto $S_x$ before each application of $f_\theta$.

**Claim:** Under (B1)–(B3), the interference between inputs $x_1$ and $x_2$ at depth $t$ satisfies:

$$\|h_t(x_1) - h_t(x_2)\| \leq L^t \|x_1 - x_2\|$$

This is a direct consequence of the Banach fixed-point theorem and the contraction property.

**Proof:** By induction. Base case: $\|h_0(x_1) - h_0(x_2)\| = \|x_1 - x_2\|$. Inductive step:

$$\|h_{t+1}(x_1) - h_{t+1}(x_2)\| = \|f_\theta(h_t(x_1)) - f_\theta(h_t(x_2))\| \leq L \|h_t(x_1) - h_t(x_2)\| \leq L^{t+1} \|x_1 - x_2\|$$

For the projection-enhanced version, if $P(x)$ projects onto a subspace where $f_\theta$ has a smaller Lipschitz constant $L' < L$, then:

$$\|P(x) \cdot h_t(x_1) - P(x) \cdot h_t(x_2)\| \leq L'^t \|x_1 - x_2\|$$

**Assessment:** *Known/direct consequence* — the contraction bound is standard. The projection enhancement is a *plausible adaptation* of spectral methods.

**Caveat:** This bounds the *separation* between different inputs' trajectories, not the *interference* from shared modifications. It shows that different inputs naturally diverge under contraction, but doesn't address the core problem of how to apply input-specific modifications without cross-contamination.

---

### Hypothesis H3 (Negative — Depth-Agnostic Gate Cannot Adapt to Nonlinear Dynamics)

**Statement:** For a generic nonlinear $f_\theta$, an input-dependent gate $g(x)$ that is depth-agnostic cannot simultaneously (i) preserve the computation $f_\theta^D(x)$ and (ii) provide non-trivial isolation between different inputs' computational paths.

**Assumptions:**
- (C1) $f_\theta$ is smooth and nonlinear.
- (C2) $D \geq 3$.
- (C3) The gate $g(x)$ is a continuous function.
- (C4) The gate is applied as $\tilde{f}_{g(x)}(h) = g(x) \odot f_\theta(h)$.

**Mathematical object being gated:** The output of $f_\theta$ at each depth, modulated by $g(x)$.

**Claim:** Under (C1)–(C4), the only gate that preserves the computation for all inputs is $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$, which provides no isolation.

**Proof sketch:**

The preservation condition at depth $D$ requires:

$$\underbrace{g(x) \odot f_\theta\bigl(g(x) \odot f_\theta\bigl(\cdots g(x) \odot f_\theta(x)\bigr)\bigr)}_{D \text{ applications}} = f_\theta^D(x)$$

Define $\phi_x(h) = g(x) \odot f_\theta(h)$. The condition is $\phi_x^D(x) = f_\theta^D(x)$.

At depth 1: $g(x) \odot f_\theta(x) = f_\theta(x)$, so $g(x)_i = 1$ whenever $[f_\theta(x)]_i \neq 0$.

At depth 2: $g(x) \odot f_\theta(g(x) \odot f_\theta(x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$.

Since $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ (from depth 1, on the relevant domain), this becomes $f_\theta(f_\theta(x)) = f_\theta(f_\theta(x))$. ✓

But $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ means the gate is trivial. Any perturbation $g(x) = \mathbf{1} + \delta(x)$ with $\delta(x) \neq 0$ will, at some depth, cause:

$$\tilde{h}_t = (\mathbf{1} + \delta(x)) \odot f_\theta(\tilde{h}_{t-1}) \neq f_\theta(\tilde{h}_{t-1})$$

and the error compounds through subsequent compositions.

**Assessment:** *Plausible adaptation* — the argument is structurally sound but relies on the gate being applied at every depth. If the gate is applied selectively (e.g., only at certain depths), the analysis changes.

**Weakest additional assumptions for truth:** The result holds for any nonlinear $f_\theta$ that is injective on the relevant domain. If $f_\theta$ has symmetries (e.g., $f_\theta(h) = f_\theta(-h)$), non-trivial gates may exist.

---

### Hypothesis H4 (Conditional Positive — Depth-Conditioned Gate)

**Statement:** If the gate is allowed to depend on both the input $x$ and the depth index $t$ (but with shared parameters — a single network $g_\phi(x, t)$), then computation-isolability can be achieved for a broad class of shared paths $f_\theta$.

**Assumptions:**
- (D1) The gate $g_\phi: \mathcal{X} \times \{0, \ldots, D-1\} \to \mathbb{R}^d$ is a single neural network with shared parameters $\phi$.
- (D2) $f_\theta$ is smooth and its Jacobian $J_f(h) = \partial f_\theta / \partial h$ has full rank on the relevant domain.
- (D3) The gate is applied as $\tilde{f}_{g_\phi(x,t)}(h) = g_\phi(x,t) \odot f_\theta(h)$.

**Mathematical object being gated:** The output of $f_\theta$ at depth $t$, modulated by $g_\phi(x, t)$.

**Claim:** Under (D1)–(D3), for any finite $D$ and any smooth $f_\theta$, there exists a gate $g_\phi$ (with $O(d \cdot D)$ parameters) such that the gated computation preserves the original: $\tilde{f}^D(x) = f_\theta^D(x)$ for all $x$.

**Proof sketch:** At each depth $t$, the preservation condition is:

$$g_\phi(x, t) \odot f_\theta(h_t) = f_\theta(h_t)$$

where $h_t = f_\theta^t(x)$. This requires $g_\phi(x, t)_i = 1$ whenever $[f_\theta(h_t)]_i \neq 0$.

A gate that simply outputs $\mathbf{1}$ for all $(x, t)$ trivially satisfies this. But for isolation, we want $g_\phi(x, t)$ to also project away components that would interfere with other inputs' computations.

The key insight: if we define $g_\phi(x, t) = \mathbf{1} + P_t(x)$ where $P_t(x)$ is a projection onto the null space of $f_\theta$'s Jacobian at $h_t$, then:

- Preservation: $g_\phi(x, t) \odot f_\theta(h_t) = f_\theta(h_t) + P_t(x) \odot f_\theta(h_t) = f_\theta(h_t)$ if $P_t(x) \odot f_\theta(h_t) = 0$.
- Isolation: $P_t(x)$ projects away components that are "shared" between different inputs' intermediate states.

However, this requires $P_t$ to depend on $h_t = f_\theta^t(x)$, which in turn depends on $x$ and $t$. A single network $g_\phi(x, t)$ can learn this mapping if given sufficient capacity.

**Assessment:** *Conjecture* — the existence of such a gate is plausible but not proven. The construction requires the gate to implicitly compute $f_\theta^t(x)$, which may require the gate to be as complex as the shared path itself.

**Weakest additional assumptions for truth:** The gate network $g_\phi$ must have sufficient capacity to represent the function $(x, t) \mapsto$ appropriate depth-dependent modulation. This may require $g_\phi$ to be at least as expressive as $f_\theta^D$.

---

## 4. Prior Art Analysis

### 4.1 Orthogonal Gradient Methods (OGM)
**Source:** Lopez-Paz & Ranzato (2017), "Gradient Episodic Memory for Continual Learning"; Zeng et al. (2019).

**Relevance:** OGM projects gradients onto the orthogonal complement of previously used gradient directions to prevent interference during *training*.

**Does it address the same problem?** **No.** OGM operates in parameter space during backpropagation. Our problem concerns forward-pass computation isolation with frozen parameters. The mathematical objects are different: OGM modifies $\nabla_\theta \mathcal{L}$, while we seek to modify $f_\theta(h)$.

**Classification:** *Known technique, not directly applicable.*

### 4.2 Progressive Neural Networks
**Source:** Rusu et al. (2016).

**Relevance:** Adds new columns (with new parameters) for each task, with lateral connections to previous columns.

**Does it address the same problem?** **No.** Progressive networks introduce per-task parameters, violating our constraint of no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters. They also require architectural modification, not just gating.

**Classification:** *Known technique, violates constraints.*

### 4.3 Hypernetworks
**Source:** Ha et al. (2016).

**Relevance:** A hypernetwork generates weights for a main network conditioned on some input.

**Does it address the same problem?** **Partially.** A hypernetwork $H_\phi(x)$ could generate per-depth weights $W_t = H_\phi(x, t)$, effectively creating input-dependent per-depth parameters. However:
- This requires the hypernetwork to have per-depth output dimensions.
- The generated weights replace $f_\theta$ rather than gating it.
- The hypernetwork itself has trainable parameters $\phi$.

**Classification:** *Known technique, relevant but violates the "no per-depth trainable parameters" constraint in spirit (the hypernetwork's output is per-depth).*

### 4.4 Conditional Computation / Mixture of Experts
**Source:** Shazeer et al. (2017), "Outrageously Large Neural Networks."

**Relevance:** Routes inputs to different expert subnetworks based on input-dependent gating.

**Does it address the same problem?** **Partially.** MoE uses input-dependent routing to select which parameters to use, which is a form of input-dependent computation. However:
- MoE typically has per-layer gating (depth-dependent routing).
- The experts are distinct networks, not a single shared path.
- MoE doesn't address the problem of preserving a specific shared-path computation.

**Classification:** *Known technique, structurally related but solves a different problem.*

### 4.5 Parameter Isolation / Masking
**Source:** Mallya & Lazebnik (2018), "PackNet"; Wortsman et al. (2020), "Supermasks."

**Relevance:** Uses binary masks to select which parameters are active for each task.

**Does it address the same problem?** **Partially.** Masking can be input-dependent (as in supermasks), but:
- Masks are typically per-layer, not shared across depth.
- Masking modifies which parameters are used, not how a shared path is composed.
- The masks are usually learned per-task, not per-input.

**Classification:** *Known technique, relevant analogy but different mathematical structure.*

### 4.6 Recurrent Neural Networks with Gating (LSTM/GRU)
**Source:** Hochreiter & Schmidhuber (1997); Cho et al. (2014).

**Relevance:** LSTMs use input-dependent gates (forget, input, output) to control information flow through a recurrent shared computation.

**Does it address the same problem?** **Yes, most closely.** LSTM gates are:
- Input-dependent (they depend on $x_t$ and $h_{t-1}$).
- Applied at each depth (time step).
- Used to control what information persists through shared recurrence.

However, LSTM gates depend on the *current state* $h_{t-1}$, not just the original input $x$. This is a crucial difference: our constraint requires the gate to depend only on $x$, not on intermediate states.

**Classification:** *Known technique, directly relevant but violates the "input-only" constraint.*

### 4.7 Attention as Gating
**Source:** Vaswani et al (2017).

**Relevance:** Attention mechanisms compute input-dependent weights to modulate information flow.

**Does it address the same problem?** **No.** Attention operates within a single layer and depends on the current representation, not on a fixed shared path. It also requires per-layer parameters (Q, K, V projections).

**Classification:** *Known technique, different problem.*

---

## 5. Novelty Classification

| Claim | Classification |
|-------|---------------|
| Shared sequential composition creates interference | **Known** (standard in RNN theory) |
| Input-dependent gating can control information flow | **Known** (LSTM, MoE) |
| Depth-agnostic gate cannot achieve exact isolation for nonlinear shared paths | **Plausible adaptation** (novel combination of known results) |
| Contractivity bounds interference without gating | **Direct consequence** (Banach fixed-point theorem) |
| Depth-conditioned gate with shared parameters may achieve isolation | **Conjecture** (no direct precedent) |
| The specific formulation of computation-isolability for frozen shared paths | **Apparently novel** |
| The impossibility result for depth-agnostic gates | **Apparently novel** (though structurally similar to results in dynamical systems theory) |

---

## 6. Obstruction Analysis

### The Core Obstruction

The fundamental obstruction to input-path isolation via depth-agnostic gating is a **temporal information deficit**: the gate cannot distinguish which depth it is operating at, and therefore cannot adapt its behavior to the depth-specific state of the computation.

More precisely:

1. **The gate sees only $x$, not $h_t$.** The intermediate state $h_t = f_\theta^t(x)$ encodes the cumulative effect of $t$ applications of the shared path. Different inputs $x_1, x_2$ may have similar $h_t$ values at some depths but divergent values at others. A depth-agnostic gate cannot detect this.

2. **Nonlinearity creates depth-dependent dynamics.** For a nonlinear $f_\theta$, the Jacobian $J_f(h_t)$ varies with $t$ (since $h_t$ changes). The "directions of interference" at depth $t$ depend on $J_f(h_t)$, which the gate cannot access.

3. **The preservation-isolation tradeoff.** Any gate that preserves the original computation must satisfy $g(x) \odot f_\theta(h) = f_\theta(h)$ (or equivalent), which forces $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ on the relevant domain. A non-trivial gate necessarily alters the computation, creating a tradeoff between fidelity and isolation.

### Characterization of the Obstruction

The obstruction can be formalized as a **rank deficiency** in the gate's information:

Let $\mathcal{I}_t(x) = (x, h_1, \ldots, h_t)$ be the full information available at depth $t$. The gate has access only to $\mathcal{I}_0(x) = \{x\}$. The information gap is:

$$\Delta_t(x) = \dim(\mathcal{I}_t(x)) - \dim(\mathcal{I}_0(x)) = t \cdot d$$

For $t \geq 1$, the gate is missing $t \cdot d$ dimensions of information about the current state. This gap grows linearly with depth, making isolation increasingly difficult for deeper compositions.

### Conditions Under Which the Obstruction Vanishes

1. **Linearity:** If $f_\theta(h) = Wh + b$, then $h_t = W^t x + (W^{t-1} + \cdots + I)b$. The state is a deterministic function of $x$ and $t$, and a depth-conditioned gate $g(x, t)$ can perfectly track the computation. Moreover, linear compositions don't create nonlinear interference, so $g = \mathbf{1}$ suffices.

2. **Contractivity with small $L$:** If $L \ll 1$, then $h_t \to h^*$ (the fixed point) exponentially fast. After a few depths, all inputs converge to the same state, and interference becomes negligible. A gate is unnecessary.

3. **Depth-conditioned gate:** If the gate can depend on $t$ (via a shared network $g_\phi(x, t)$), the information gap $\Delta_t$ is closed, and isolation becomes feasible (subject to the gate's capacity).

4. **State-conditioned gate:** If the gate can depend on $h_t$ (as in LSTMs), the information gap is fully closed, and standard gating techniques apply.

---

## 7. Summary and Recommendations

### Key Findings

1. **Exact elimination of interference is impossible** under the stated constraints (depth-agnostic gate, frozen shared path, nonlinear $f_\theta$). This is an *apparently novel* result, though structurally analogous to known limitations in dynamical systems.

2. **Bounded interference is achievable** if the shared path is contractive, but this is a property of $f_\theta$, not of the gate. The gate contributes nothing in this regime.

3. **Depth-conditioned gating** (with shared parameters) is the most promising direction, but requires the gate to implicitly compute $f_\theta^t(x)$, which may necessitate gate capacity comparable to the shared path itself.

4. **The LSTM approach** (state-conditioned gating) is the closest prior art, but violates the "input-only" constraint. Relaxing this constraint to allow state-conditioning would make the problem tractable using known techniques.

### Strategic Recommendation

The investigation should pivot from "can we achieve isolation with depth-agnostic gating?" to "what is the minimal additional information the gate needs?" The answer is likely: **the gate needs access to the intermediate state $h_t$** (or equivalently, the depth index $t$ with sufficient capacity to simulate $f_\theta^t$). This suggests that:

- Either accept state-conditioned gating (LSTM-style), which is well-understood but adds complexity.
- Or accept approximate isolation via contractivity, which requires architectural constraints on $f_\theta$.
- Or accept that the shared path must be retrained (or extended with per-depth parameters), which violates the original constraints but may be necessary.

The "failure condition" is met: **the proposed isolation is impossible under the stated assumptions.** The obstruction is the gate's inability to access depth-specific state information, which is a fundamental information-theoretic limitation.

# Revised Analysis: Input-Path Interference in BDH and the Limits of Input-Dependent Gating/Projection

I treat the BDH shared core abstractly as follows: a frozen map \(F\) is applied sequentially for \(D\) steps. The questions below are independent of the particular particle dynamics of BDH, except where I explicitly use properties of \(F\). If the implementation reports define \(F\) concretely, the theorems should be instantiated with that \(F\); the structural obstructions remain.

---

## 1. Formalization

### 1.1 Shared sequential composition

Let \(V=\mathbb{R}^d\) be the state space and \(X\subseteq V\) the input space. A **depth-\(D\) shared sequential composition** is defined by a fixed map

\[
F:V\to V
\]

and the recurrence

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)),\qquad t=0,\dots,D-1.
\]

Write

\[
F^D=F\circ F\circ\cdots\circ F
\]

for the \(D\)-fold composition. The **unperturbed trajectory** of \(x\) is

\[
\tau_F(x)=(s_0(x),s_1(x),\dots,s_D(x)).
\]

This is the relevant BDH abstraction: the same parameterized transformation is applied at every depth, and the same transformation is shared by all inputs.

### 1.2 Frozen shared path

The map \(F\) is **frozen** if its parameters are not updated during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection mechanism may be trained. Formally:

\[
\frac{\partial F}{\partial \theta}=0
\]

during any adaptation step. No theorem below relies on retraining \(F\).

### 1.3 Gating

An **input-dependent gate** is a map

\[
g:X\to \mathbb{R}^d
\]

which is applied identically at every depth. The two canonical placements are:

\[
\text{output gating:}\quad s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

\[
\text{input gating:}\quad s_{t+1}=F\bigl(g(x)\odot s_t\bigr).
\]

The gate may be trained, but it has **no per-depth parameters**: the same \(g(x)\) is used at every \(t\). I will also consider the relaxed variant

\[
g:X\times \{0,\dots,D-1\}\to \mathbb{R}^d,
\]

where the gate receives the depth index \(t\) as input but still has only shared parameters. This is not a per-depth parameter vector; it is a single function of both \(x\) and \(t\).

### 1.4 Projection

An **input-dependent projection** is a map

\[
P:X\to \Pi(V),
\]

where \(\Pi(V)\) is the set of orthogonal projections of \(\mathbb{R}^d\), applied either as

\[
\text{output projection:}\quad s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t),
\]

or

\[
\text{input projection:}\quad s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t).
\]

A projection is more restrictive than a general gate because it is linear, idempotent, and rank-deficient.

### 1.5 Input-path interference

I distinguish two precise notions.

#### (a) Depth-aliasing interference

Suppose an oracle with access to the current depth or state would apply a reference modulation

\[
g^*(x,t),\qquad t=0,\dots,D-1.
\]

A depth-independent gate \(g(x)\) produces the actual transition

\[
s_{t+1}=\Phi(g(x),s_t),
\]

where \(\Phi\) is the chosen gating/projection rule. The **depth-aliasing error** for input \(x\) is

\[
\mathrm{DA}(g;x)=\sum_{t=0}^{D-1}
\left\|
\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))-\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x))
\right\|.
\]

If \(\mathrm{DA}(g;x)>0\), then the lack of depth information corrupts the trajectory. This is the forward-pass sense in which shared sequential composition causes interference: the same gate is re-applied at every depth even though the correct modulation may need to change from depth to depth.

#### (b) Gradient interference

For two tasks \(A,B\) with losses \(\mathcal L_A,\mathcal L_B\) on the same frozen \(F\), the first-order change in \(\mathcal L_A\) caused by one gradient step on task \(B\) is proportional to

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_A,\nabla_\phi \mathcal L_B\rangle.
\]

Define the **task Jacobians**

\[
J_i(\phi)=\frac{\partial}{\partial \phi}
\bigl(s_D(x;\phi)\bigr)\Big|_{x\in X_i}.
\]

Task-wise gradient interference is

\[
I_{ij}=
\left\|
J_i(\phi) J_j(\phi)^\top
\right\|.
\]

For shared sequential composition, these Jacobians have a special structure:

\[
\frac{\partial s_D(x)}{\partial \phi}
=
\sum_{t=0}^{D-1}
\left(
\prod_{r=t+1}^{D-1} DF(s_r(x))
\right)
\frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial \phi}(g_\phi(x,t),s_t(x)).
\]

The same parameters \(\phi\) appear at every depth, so gradients from different tasks can overlap even when the inputs are disjoint. This is the training-time sense of input-path interference.

### 1.6 Computation-isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a set of tasks if:

1. each task’s desired output is produced exactly, and  
2. there is no interference: \(\mathrm{DA}(g;x)=0\) for all \(x\) in the forward sense, or \(I_{ij}=0\) for all task pairs in the gradient sense.

A mechanism **bounds interference** if the relevant quantity is at most \(\varepsilon>0\) uniformly over the task inputs.

### 1.7 Elimination versus bounding

- **Elimination** means exact equality of outputs and exact zero interference.
- **Bounding** means the deviation is at most \(\varepsilon\), with \(\varepsilon\) controlled by quantities such as the Lipschitz constant of \(F\), the depth \(D\), the gate error, and the number of parameters.

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses

### H1. Exact preservation of the unperturbed path forces the gate to be the identity

**Assumptions.**

- \(F\) is defined on an open set \(U\subseteq \mathbb{R}^d\).
- The gate is depth-independent output gating:

\[
s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t).
\]

- We require exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory:

\[
s_t(x)=F^t(x)\qquad\forall x\in U,\ \forall t.
\]

**Mathematical object gated.**  
The output of the frozen map \(F\) at every depth.

**Claim.**  
Under these assumptions, \(g(x)=\mathbf{1}\) on every coordinate where \(F(s_t(x))\neq 0\). If \(F\) is componentwise nonzero along trajectories on \(U\), then \(g(x)=\mathbf{1}\) for all \(x\in U\).

**Proof.**  
At \(t=0\), exact preservation requires

\[
g(x)\odot F(x)=F(x).
\]

Componentwise:

\[
(g_i(x)-1)F_i(x)=0.
\]

Wherever \(F_i(x)\neq 0\), we get \(g_i(x)=1\). Wherever \(F_i(x)=0\), the gate is invisible, but it is also useless for isolation. The same argument applies at every depth.

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem.** It is a direct consequence of the preservation equation. It shows that, under the strong requirement that the mechanism changes nothing, the only gate is the identity gate. In particular, a gate cannot simultaneously “do nothing” and “isolate tasks.”

**Weakest additional assumptions under which the obstruction disappears.**  
If we only require preservation of the final output \(s_D(x)=F^D(x)\), rather than the whole trajectory, non-identity gates may exist when \(F^D\) is non-injective. This is the underdetermined case; it is not a useful general construction.

**Classification.**  
Known / direct consequence.

---

### H2. Depth-independent gating cannot represent depth-varying modulation

**Assumptions.**

- The transition rule \(\Phi(g,s)\) is injective in the gate argument \(g\) for the states along the unperturbed trajectory.
- A task requires an ideal per-depth modulation \(g^*(x,t)\) that is not constant in \(t\).

**Mathematical object gated.**  
The entire per-depth modulation sequence \((g(x,0),\dots,g(x,D-1))\).

**Claim.**  
No depth-independent gate \(g(x)\) can produce exactly the same trajectory as \(g^*(x,t)\) for all \(t\).

**Proof.**  
Equality at every depth gives

\[
\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))=\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x)).
\]

By injectivity of \(\Phi(\cdot,s_t(x))\),

\[
g(x)=g^*(x,t)
\]

for every \(t\), contradicting the assumption that \(g^*(x,\cdot)\) is nonconstant.

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact representational theorem.** This is the cleanest formalization of the phrase “input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition”: a depth-independent gate is a point on the diagonal of the product space \(M^D\), while the task requirements occupy off-diagonal points.

**Weakest additional assumptions under which it becomes true.**  
The obstruction disappears if we allow:

- a depth-indexed shared gate \(g(x,t)\);
- a state-dependent gate, e.g. \(g(s_t)\), as in LSTM/GRU;
- per-depth trainable parameters, which is excluded by the research question.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence of the definition; the phrasing as a frozen-shared-path obstruction is a plausible adaptation of standard control-theoretic observability reasoning.

---

### H3. Projection mechanisms have a rank obstruction

**Assumptions.**

- The mechanism is an input-dependent orthogonal projection, e.g.

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t).
\]

- The unperturbed trajectory of \(x\) is such that

\[
\operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)),F(s_1(x)),\dots,F(s_{D-1}(x))\}
\]

has dimension \(m(x)\).

**Mathematical object projected.**  
The image space of the projection, i.e. the subspace of state space that survives each depth.

**Claim.**  
If the projection is to preserve the unperturbed trajectory exactly, then

\[
\operatorname{rank} P(x)\ge m(x).
\]

For \(K\) tasks whose relevant spans have dimensions \(m_i\), and whose projections must be mutually orthogonal to avoid gradient interference, necessarily

\[
\sum_{i=1}^K m_i\le d.
\]

Hence a projection can isolate at most \(\lfloor d/\min_i m_i\rfloor\) tasks.

**Proof.**  
Exact preservation requires

\[
P(x)F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x))
\]

for all \(t\), so every \(F(s_t(x))\) lies in \(\operatorname{Im}P(x)\). The dimension bound follows. Orthogonality of task subspaces is required for zero gradient interference; orthogonal subspaces of \(\mathbb{R}^d\) have total dimension at most \(d\).

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact linear-algebra theorem.**

**Weakest additional assumptions under which it becomes useful.**  
Tasks must live in low-dimensional invariant or approximately invariant subspaces. If the relevant computational subspaces have dimension \(1\), for example, a rank-1 projection can isolate up to \(d\) tasks.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence of linear algebra; the application to frozen shared paths is a plausible adaptation.

---

### H4. Finite parameter budget bounds exact gradient isolation

**Assumptions.**

- The gate/projection mechanism has \(p\) trainable parameters.
- Task output Jacobians are \(J_i\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times p}\).
- We require exact zero gradient interference:

\[
J_iJ_j^\top=0\qquad\forall i\neq j.
\]

**Mathematical object constrained.**  
The parameter space of the gate/projection mechanism.

**Claim.**  
If each task Jacobian has rank \(r_i=\operatorname{rank}J_i\), then

\[
\sum_i r_i\le p.
\]

In particular, if every task has full row rank and \(p\le d\), then at most one task can be isolated exactly. For a shared sequential composition with generic nonlinear \(F\), task Jacobians are generic sums over depth, so this is the typical situation.

**Proof.**  
The row spaces of the \(J_i\) must be mutually orthogonal subspaces of \(\mathbb{R}^p\). The sum of their dimensions cannot exceed \(p\).

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem.** It is the usual parameter-isolation bound, restated for trainable gates on a frozen shared path.

**Weakest additional assumptions under which exact isolation is possible for many tasks.**  
Each task must occupy a low-rank subspace of the gate-parameter Jacobian. This occurs, for example, when tasks use disjoint subsets of inputs or when the gate network is designed so that task-specific features activate disjoint hidden units.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence of linear algebra; the application to frozen-path gating is a plausible adaptation.

---

### H5. Bounded interference is possible exactly when the gated dynamics are non-expansive

**Assumptions.**

- The modulated transition is Lipschitz in state with constant \(L_\Phi\):

\[
\|\Phi(g,s)-\Phi(g,s')\|\le L_\Phi\|s-s'\|.
\]

- The per-depth gate error is bounded:

\[
\|\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))-\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x))\|\le \delta.
\]

**Mathematical object bounded.**  
The full trajectory error.

**Claim.**  
The output error satisfies

\[
\|s_D(x)-s_D^*(x)\|
\le
\delta\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}L_\Phi^k
=
\begin{cases}
\delta\dfrac{1-L_\Phi^D}{1-L_\Phi}, & L_\Phi<1,\\[1ex]
\delta D, & L_\Phi=1,\\[1ex]
\delta\dfrac{L_\Phi^D-1}{L_\Phi-1}, & L_\Phi>1.
\end{cases}
\]

**Proof.**  
Let \(e_t=\|s_t-s_t^*\|\). Then

\[
e_{t+1}
\le
\|\Phi(g,s_t)-\Phi(g,s_t^*)\|
+
\|\Phi(g,s_t^*)-\Phi(g^*,s_t^*)\|
\le
L_\Phi e_t+\delta.
\]

Solving the recurrence gives the claimed bound.

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem.**

**Consequence.**  
If the gated system is contractive, \(L_\Phi<1\), then interference is bounded uniformly in depth by \(\delta/(1-L_\Phi)\). If \(L_\Phi>1\), interference can grow exponentially in \(D\). This is why “bounding” is not automatic: for generic nonlinear \(F\), \(L_\Phi\) is often larger than \(1\), and small gate errors are amplified through the shared composition.

**Classification.**  
Known / direct consequence.

---

### H6. Depth-indexed shared gating: finite exact interpolation is conditionally possible

**Assumptions.**

- We allow a depth-indexed shared gate \(g_\phi(x,t)\), still with no per-depth parameter vectors.
- The task set is finite: \((x_i,y_i)\), \(i=1,\dots,N\).
- For each \(i\), there exists some per-depth modulation sequence \(g_{i,t}\) that realizes \(y_i\) under the transition rule.
- The parameterized family \(g_\phi\) can interpolate the finite data \((x_i,t)\mapsto g_{i,t}\), e.g. an MLP with enough parameters.

**Claim.**  
Under these assumptions, there exists a shared-parameter depth-indexed gate that produces exactly the desired outputs for all finite task inputs.

**Proof sketch.**  
The modulation requirement is a finite lookup table. Universal approximation on finite sets gives interpolation.

**Important caveat.**  
The decisive assumption is that the target modulation sequences \(g_{i,t}\) exist. For a generic nonlinear \(F\), this is **underdetermined**. For example, if \(F\) is contractive, many targets are unreachable regardless of gating. Thus this is a conditional existence theorem, not a general theorem.

**Verdict.**  
**Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined.**

**Weakest additional assumptions.**  
Reachability of the targets under the allowed modulation, plus sufficient gate capacity. Additionally, exact zero gradient interference requires the parameter-count condition of H4.

**Classification.**  
Plausible adaptation of universal approximation; the reachability part is a conjecture.

---

## 3. Prior Art

### 3.1 Orthogonal gradient and gradient-projection methods

**Examples.** GEM / A-GEM, Orthogonal Gradient Descent, TRGP, GPM.

**What they address.**  
They project gradients in parameter space so that updates for a new task do not increase losses on previous tasks.

**Do they address the same mathematical problem?**  
Only partially. They address training-time gradient interference, which corresponds to my second notion of interference. They do **not** address depth-aliasing in a frozen shared composition. Moreover, they typically require unfreezing or at least modifying the main network; here the shared path is frozen.

**Classification.**  
Known, partially relevant.

### 3.2 Parameter isolation and masking

**Examples.** PackNet, Piggyback, Hard Attention to the Task, Supermasks.

**What they address.**  
They allocate disjoint parameter subsets to tasks, preventing cross-task updates.

**Same problem?**  
Not the same. They introduce per-task masks or per-task parameters, which is a form of parameter isolation. The research question explicitly asks for an input-dependent gate/projection on a single frozen shared path, not for partitioning the shared path’s parameters.

**Classification.**  
Known, related but different mechanism.

### 3.3 Progressive networks and path-based continual learning

**Examples.** Progressive Neural Networks, PathNet.

**What they address.**  
They add new columns or paths for new tasks while freezing old ones.

**Same problem?**  
No. They avoid interference by adding task-specific architecture, which is effectively per-task parameter growth. The question here is whether a *single* input-dependent mechanism can isolate paths within one frozen shared composition.

**Classification.**  
Known, different problem.

### 3.4 Hypernetworks

**Examples.** Ha et al., Chunked Hypernetworks.

**What they address.**  
A hypernetwork generates weights for a main network as a function of an input or task embedding.

**Same problem?**  
Partly. A hypernetwork can generate input-dependent per-depth modulations. But the generated weights are typically per-layer, which violates the “no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters” constraint in spirit. A hypernetwork also replaces or augments the main weights rather than gating a frozen shared path.

**Classification.**  
Known, partially relevant.

### 3.5 Recurrent neural networks with gating

**Examples.** LSTM, GRU.

**What they address.**  
They use input- and state-dependent gates inside a shared recurrent computation.

**Same problem?**  
This is the closest prior art. LSTM gates are applied at every time step with shared parameters, exactly like gating a shared sequential composition. The crucial difference is that LSTM gates depend on the current hidden state \(h_{t-1}\), not only on the original input \(x\). This supports H2: state information is what makes recurrent gating effective. It is also empirical evidence, not a theorem, that state-conditioning resolves many of the pathologies of depth-independent control.

**Classification.**  
Known, directly relevant, but with a different information assumption.

### 3.6 Conditional computation and mixture of experts

**Examples.** Mixture of Experts, sparsely gated layers.

**What they address.**  
Input-dependent routing to different experts or subnetworks.

**Same problem?**  
Not the same. MoE routes inputs to different parameter sets, typically with per-layer routers, and its goal is capacity scaling or efficiency, not preserving a frozen shared-path computation.

**Classification.**  
Known, structurally similar but not the same mathematical problem.

### 3.7 Depth-recurrent and weight-tied transformers

**Examples.** ALBERT, Universal Transformers, LoopFormer.

**What they address.**  
They reuse the same block across depth and sometimes use adaptive computation time.

**Same problem?**  
Partially. They study shared-depth composition, but they do not address task isolation via input-dependent gates. Adaptive computation time is state-dependent halting, not input-dependent task isolation.

**Classification.**  
Known, background context.

---

## 4. Novelty Classification

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formal notion of depth-aliasing interference in a frozen shared composition | Apparently novel as a packaged definition; components are known from control theory |
| H1: exact preservation forces identity gating | Direct consequence |
| H2: depth-independent gating cannot represent depth-varying modulation | Direct consequence |
| H3: projection rank obstruction | Direct consequence |
| H4: gradient-isolation parameter bound | Direct consequence |
| H5: contraction/expansion bound on interference | Direct consequence |
| H6: conditional finite interpolation by depth-indexed shared gate | Plausible adaptation / conjecture |
| Claim that state or depth information is necessary for robust gating in shared composition | Plausible adaptation; consistent with LSTM design but not proven here as a theorem |

No experimental result is used as evidence for any theorem. The exact results above are purely analytical.

---

## 5. Failure Condition and Obstruction

The strict version of the proposed mechanism is:

- input-dependent gate/projection,
- no per-depth trainable parameters,
- frozen shared path,
- no retraining of \(F\).

Under this strict version, **exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible for generic nonlinear \(F\)**. The obstruction has four independent parts.

### 5.1 Diagonalization obstruction

A depth-independent gate \(g(x)\) is forced to lie on the diagonal of the product space of depth-wise modulations:

\[
(g(x),g(x),\dots,g(x))\in M^D.
\]

Any task whose ideal modulation depends on depth lies off this diagonal. This is a representational impossibility, not a training failure.

### 5.2 Preservation–identity obstruction

If the mechanism is required to preserve the unperturbed computation exactly, then output gating forces \(g(x)=\mathbf{1}\) wherever \(F\) is nonzero. The only gate that changes nothing is the identity gate. Thus a gate cannot both preserve the frozen computation and alter the path for isolation.

### 5.3 Rank and parameter budget obstruction

Even if the depth-aliasing obstruction is removed by allowing a depth-indexed shared gate, exact gradient isolation requires the task Jacobians to be mutually orthogonal. The sum of their ranks cannot exceed the number of trainable gate parameters \(p\). For generic tasks, this permits only one task when \(p\le d\).

### 5.4 Expansive-dynamics obstruction

Bounding interference is possible if the gated dynamics are contractive. For generic nonlinear \(F\), the Lipschitz constant of the composed system is \(>1\), and small gate errors are amplified exponentially in depth. Hence even bounded interference is not guaranteed without additional assumptions on \(F\).

---

## 6. Conclusion

The answer to the research question is:

- **Elimination** of input-path interference by a depth-independent input-dependent gate/projection on a frozen shared path is **impossible in general**.
- **Bounding** is possible if the gated system is contractive or if the depth is small enough that exponential amplification is controlled.
- **Controllability** can be achieved only by enriching the information available to the gate: either allow a depth-indexed shared gate \(g(x,t)\), allow state-dependent gating \(g(s_t)\), allow per-task parameter blocks, or impose strong low-rank/invariant-subspace structure on the tasks.

The most precise obstruction is the **diagonalization obstruction**: a depth-independent gate is a single point on the diagonal of the space of depth-wise modulations, while the tasks generically require off-diagonal points. This obstruction is independent of optimization, capacity, and training data. It is the mathematical core of why input-path interference cannot, in general, be eliminated by the proposed mechanism.

# Moderator Synthesis — Round 2 Consolidation

**Role:** Synthesis and consensus evaluation of the STRATEGIST, CRITIC, and OPTIMIZER contributions on the BDH input-path interference question.

Before consolidating, one factual check is warranted, since the entire analysis hinges on BDH genuinely exhibiting weight-shared sequential composition across depth (if it did not, the question would misfire at the premise level):

[SEARCH: "Dragon Hatchling" BDH weight tying shared parameters across depth recurrent layer]

---

## 0. Evidence-Base Status

- The BDH paper (arXiv:2509.26507) is confirmed to exist and to describe a scale-free network of locally-interacting neuron particles with transformer-comparable performance. Third-party implementations (BDH-MLX) corroborate the architecture's general character.
- **Retrieval limitation:** the internal project documents (`2026-08-*` progress reports, `paper-parity-plan`, `bdh-continual-learning-plan_v2.md`, `cl-h1-report.md`) could **not** be retrieved through available channels; searches returned irrelevant material. Consequently, neither participant nor this moderation could cross-check the formal models against your concrete implementation or against the H1 experimental report dated today. All conclusions below are therefore **structural**, derived from the abstract "shared sequential composition" model that both analysts adopted consistently. Instantiation with BDH's literal update equations remains an open verification step (see §3.3).

Both principal analyses proceeded from the same formal skeleton — frozen map $F$, recurrence $s_{t+1}=\Phi(g(x),s_t)$, depth-agnostic input-conditioned gate/projection — which makes genuine consensus evaluation possible.

---

## 1. Points of Consensus (High Confidence)

**C1 — Formal framework agreement.** The definitions of shared sequential composition, frozen path, gating vs. projection, and the elimination/bounding distinction were independently convergent. No participant disputed another's definitions. *Consensus: strong.*

**C2 — Exact elimination is impossible under the strict reading.** Three independent obstructions were derived, by complementary methods:

- **Identity-forcing** (STRATEGIST H1/H3; OPTIMIZER H1): exact trajectory preservation under output gating $g\odot F(s)$ forces $g=\mathbf{1}$ wherever $F$ is nonzero along the trajectory. A mechanism required to change nothing can isolate nothing.
- **Diagonalization** (OPTIMIZER H2; STRATEGIST's "temporal information deficit" is its informal statement): a depth-agnostic gate occupies the diagonal of the product space $M^D$ of per-depth modulations; any task whose ideal modulation $g^*(x,t)$ is nonconstant in $t$ lies off-diagonal and is unreachable, given injectivity of $\Phi(\cdot,s_t)$ in the gate argument.
- **Rank/parameter-budget** (OPTIMIZER H3/H4): exact zero cross-task gradient interference through shared gate parameters $\phi$ requires mutually orthogonal task-Jacobian row spaces, hence $\sum_i \mathrm{rank}(J_i)\le p$. Generic tasks saturate this at one task when $p\le d$.

*Consensus: strong. This satisfies the brief's failure condition: the proposed isolation is impossible under the stated strict assumptions.*

**C3 — Bounding is conditional, not free.** Per-depth gate error $\delta$ under Lipschitz constant $L_\Phi$ yields output error $\le \delta\sum_k L_\Phi^k$: uniformly bounded iff $L_\Phi<1$, growing $\sim\delta D$ at $L_\Phi=1$, exponentially otherwise (OPTIMIZER H5; STRATEGIST H2 agrees and correctly notes contraction bounds trajectory *separation*, not modification-induced interference). Trained LLM-scale maps typically have $L_\Phi>1$, so bounding is a real architectural constraint, not a default. *Consensus: strong.*

**C4 — Escape routes require enriched information or structure.** All participants converge: relaxation is possible via (i) depth-indexed shared-parameter gates $g_\phi(x,t)$, (ii) state-conditioned gating ($g(s_t)$, LSTM-style — the nearest prior art, which violates the input-only constraint), (iii) low-rank/invariant task subspaces, or (iv) abandoning the frozen-path constraint. *Consensus: strong.*

**C5 — Prior-art triage.** Independent and concordant: orthogonal-gradient methods (GEM/A-GEM/GPM) operate on gradients in parameter space, not forward-pass composition — related but a different mathematical problem; progressive networks, PackNet, supermasks introduce per-task parameters/masks — constraint-violating; hypernetworks and MoE are structurally adjacent but generate per-layer outputs or route between experts — not the same problem; Universal Transformer/ALBERT establish the weight-tied backdrop without addressing isolation. **No surveyed prior art solves this exact problem.** *Consensus: strong.*

---

## 2. Disputes and Adjudications

**D1 — Novelty classification conflict.** STRATEGIST labels the impossibility result "apparently novel"; OPTIMIZER labels the equivalent statements "direct consequence."
*Adjudication:* OPTIMIZER is correct on the mathematics, STRATEGIST is correct on the contribution. Every ingredient (functional-equation identity forcing, diagonal argument, rank counting, Lipschitz error propagation) is textbook. What is novel is the **problem formulation** — isolation of computations within a frozen weight-tied path under an input-only gate, posed for continual learning. Write-up guidance: claim a new formalization and a clean negative result; do **not** claim new mathematics.

**D2 — STRATEGIST H4 is defective.** Its construction (Jacobian-null-space projection achieving preservation + isolation with $O(d\cdot D)$ parameters) collapses to the identity gate under its own preservation requirement, and conflates the Jacobian's null space with the output's annihilator. OPTIMIZER's H6 (conditional finite interpolation, contingent on reachability of target modulation sequences) is the correct weakened version. *Adjudication:* H4 withdrawn as stated; retained only as OPTIMIZER-style conjecture.

**D3 — STRATEGIST's "information gap" $\Delta_t = t\cdot d$.** Dimensions of informally-defined information sets are not a rigorous quantity. Superseded by OPTIMIZER's rank-theoretic arguments. Retain only as heuristic motivation.

**D4 — Interference has two distinct meanings; only OPTIMIZER separated them cleanly.** Forward-pass *depth-aliasing* vs. training-time *gradient interference*. See correction G1 below — this separation turns out to be more important than either participant realized.

---

## 3. Corrections and Gaps Identified in Moderation

**G1 — Cross-input forward-pass interference is structurally zero; the live channels are elsewhere.** With frozen $\theta$ and a *stateless* input-conditioned mechanism, each input's forward pass is computationally independent: $x_1$'s trajectory cannot be perturbed by $x_2$'s "presence." STRATEGIST's Definition 3 counterfactual ($\tilde h_t(x_1 \mid x_2 \text{ active})$) is therefore ill-defined as stated. The operationally meaningful interference notions are:

1. **Depth-aliasing within a pass** (obstruction B above); and
2. **Cross-time forgetting**: $\phi$ is shared across all inputs, so any update $\Delta\phi$ from task $B$ alters $g_\phi$ on task-$A$ inputs — first-order damage governed exactly by the Jacobian-overlap/rank condition (obstruction C). This, not intra-pass contamination, is the catastrophic-forgetting channel in this architecture.

Future work should define interference exclusively through these two channels.

**G2 — The preservation requirement must be asymmetric to be meaningful.** Requiring preservation on *all* inputs trivially forces the identity gate (C2). The continual-learning-relevant requirement is: exact preservation on old-task inputs $S_{\text{old}}$, unconstrained deviation on new-task inputs $S_{\text{new}}$. Under this reading the identity obstruction restricts to $S_{\text{old}}$; non-trivial gates become admissible off it; the surviving barriers are the diagonal obstruction (whenever old-task-faithful modulation must vary with depth) and the rank/budget obstruction for gradient-exactness. **Neither participant developed this asymmetric variant; it should be the primary next theorem target.**

**G3 — BDH instantiation is outstanding.** BDH's block has residual structure and elementwise multiplicative (Hebbian-type) terms. Consequences: (a) output-gating the full map would gate the residual stream — likely harmful; gating only the delta term is the natural placement; (b) BDH's native plasticity must be explicitly disabled for the "frozen path" premise to hold. These checks against `paper.tex`/the repository were not performed in-round.

**G4 — Scope decision required on $t$-conditioning.** A shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ (depth index as *input*, e.g., via a fixed embedding) adds $O(1)$ parameters and **does not violate "no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters" as literally worded**. This is the pivotal loophole: every impossibility result proven here targets the strictly depth-agnostic gate and dissolves — into conditional statements (reachability + capacity + rank budget) — once $t$ is admitted. The team must rule explicitly on whether the intended constraint forbids $t$-conditioning; the theorems' applicability hinges on this interpretation.

---

## 4. Consolidated Answer to the Research Question

| Reading of the question | Verdict | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Depth-agnostic gate/projection, frozen nonlinear $F$, **exact elimination** | **Impossible** (identity-forcing + diagonalization + rank obstructions) | Proven; direct consequences |
| Same, **bounding** | Possible iff gated dynamics non-expansive ($L_\Phi<1$) or depth-limited error budget; else exponential amplification | Proven conditional theorem |
| Enriched gate ($t$-conditioned shared-parameter, or state-conditioned), **controllability** | Feasible conditional on reachability, gate capacity, low-rank task subspaces; exact multi-task gradient isolation still capped by $\sum_i \mathrm{rank}(J_i)\le p$ | Conjecture / plausible adaptation |

The failure condition contemplated by the brief is **met for the strict reading**, with a precise obstruction characterization: a depth-agnostic gate is confined to the diagonal of the modulation product space, and exactness requirements (preservation, gradient orthogonality) collapse either to the identity mechanism or to a rank budget permitting at most one generic task. No optimization method, capacity increase, or training regime circumvents these; they are representational, not algorithmic.

---

## 5. Final Novelty Ledger (Adjudicated)

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formalization: isolation within frozen weight-tied composition via input-only gate, CL-framed | **Apparently novel** (as packaging) |
| Identity-forcing lemma | **Direct consequence** |
| Diagonalization obstruction | **Direct consequence** (control-theoretic flavor) |
| Rank/parameter-budget bound on exact gradient isolation | **Direct consequence** |
| Contraction/amplification error bound | **Direct consequence** |
| Necessity of state/depth information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/ACT practice; proven only under injectivity assumptions) |
| $t$-conditioned shared gate achieves isolation broadly | **Conjecture** (reachability undetermined) |
| Any empirical effect observed in `cl-h1-report` | Inadmissible as theorem evidence until reconciled with the above |

---

## 6. Recommended Next Steps

1. **Restate the theorem set under asymmetric preservation** (old-task exact / new-task free) and prove that the diagonal and rank obstructions survive while the identity obstruction dies. This is the honest CL formalization and the highest-value open result.
2. **Instantiate $F$ with BDH's concrete update** from `paper.tex`; fix gate placement relative to the residual stream; confirm plasticity disabling.
3. **Rule on the $t$-conditioning interpretation** of the constraint; if permitted, specify a minimal $g_\phi(x,t)$ (fixed depth embedding into the existing gate network) and test — labeling outcomes strictly as empirical conjecture.
4. **Reconcile with the local reports** (`plan_v2`, `cl-h1-report`) once retrievable; verify that the implemented mechanism corresponds to one of the formally scoped variants rather than an unscoped mixture.
5. **Write-up posture:** present C2/C3 as a clean impossibility-and-bounding package for weight-tied architectures; cite LSTM/GRU, adaptive computation time, and universal-transformer literature as nearest neighbors; claim formalization novelty, not mathematical novelty.

**Consensus summary:** full agreement on framework, on the impossibility of exact elimination under strict assumptions, on conditional bounding, and on prior-art triage; disagreements were confined to novelty labeling and one defective construction, both adjudicated above. The investigation's productive frontier is the asymmetric-preservation reformulation and the $t$-conditioning scope decision.
