# Final Strategic Assessment: Input-Path Interference in BDH via Gating/Projection

**Date:** 2026-08-25 | **Status:** Complete

---

## Executive Summary

Under the strict constraints stated in the research question — depth-agnostic input-dependent gate/projection, frozen shared path, no per-depth trainable parameters, no retraining — **exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible** for generic nonlinear shared compositions. This is proven via three independent obstructions (identity-forcing, diagonalization, rank budget). Bounding is possible only under contractivity assumptions that are unlikely to hold for trained LLM-scale maps. The productive frontier is relaxing the depth-agnostic constraint to admit a depth-indexed shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$, which dissolves the impossibility into conditional feasibility statements.

---

## 1. Formal Definitions

### 1.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let $V = \mathbb{R}^d$ be the state space. A **depth-$D$ shared sequential composition** is defined by a fixed map $F: V \to V$ and the recurrence:

$$s_0(x) = x, \quad s_{t+1}(x) = F(s_t(x)), \quad t = 0, \ldots, D-1$$

The unperturbed trajectory is $\tau_F(x) = (s_0(x), s_1(x), \ldots, s_D(x))$.

**Classification:** Direct characterization of BDH's weight-tied recurrent depth, confirmed by the BDH-MLX implementation ("Same weights reused in all layers (recurrent depth)").

### 1.2 Frozen Shared Path

$F$ is **frozen** if $\partial F / \partial \theta = 0$ during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection mechanism $\phi$ may be trained.

**Classification:** Known — standard continual learning constraint.

### 1.3 Input-Path Interference (Two Precise Notions)

**Forward-pass depth-aliasing:** A depth-agnostic gate $g(x)$ is applied identically at every depth. If the ideal modulation for input $x$ is $g^*(x,t)$ (varying with depth), the depth-aliasing error is:

$$\mathrm{DA}(g; x) = \sum_{t=0}^{D-1} \left\| \Phi(g(x), s_t(x)) - \Phi(g^*(x,t), s_t(x)) \right\|$$

**Gradient interference (cross-task forgetting):** For tasks $A, B$ with shared gate parameters $\phi$, the first-order damage from a $B$-update to $A$-performance is proportional to $\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_A, \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_B \rangle$. The task Jacobians are:

$$J_i(\phi) = \frac{\partial}{\partial \phi} s_D(x; \phi) \Big|_{x \in X_i}$$

**Critical correction from moderation:** Cross-input forward-pass interference is **structurally zero** with frozen $\theta$ and a stateless input-conditioned mechanism — each input's forward pass is computationally independent. The live interference channels are depth-aliasing (within a pass) and gradient interference (across training steps).

**Classification:** The depth-aliasing notion is **apparently novel** as a packaged definition; components are known from control theory. The gradient notion is **known** from continual learning.

### 1.4 Gating

An **input-dependent gate** $g: X \to \mathbb{R}^d$ is applied identically at every depth:

- Output gating: $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$
- Input gating: $s_{t+1} = F(g(x) \odot s_t)$

The gate has **no per-depth parameters**: the same $g(x)$ is used at every $t$.

**Classification:** Known mechanism (LSTM, MoE), but the depth-independence + frozen-path combination is novel.

### 1.5 Projection

An **input-dependent projection** $P: X \to \Pi(V)$ maps each input to an orthogonal projection, applied as $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$ or $s_{t+1} = F(P(x) s_t)$.

**Classification:** Known (gradient projection methods), formalized for forward-pass application.

### 1.6 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** if: (1) each task's desired output is produced exactly, and (2) $\mathrm{DA}(g;x) = 0$ for all $x$ (forward) and $I_{ij} = 0$ for all task pairs (gradient).

**Classification:** **Apparently novel** — this specific formulation combining shared composition, frozen parameters, and forward-pass isolation does not appear in surveyed literature.

### 1.7 Elimination vs. Bounding

- **Elimination:** Exact equality of outputs and exact zero interference.
- **Bounding:** Deviation $\leq \varepsilon$, with $\varepsilon$ controlled by Lipschitz constant, depth, gate error, and parameter count.

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation of approximation theory concepts.

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses

### H1. Identity-Forcing Lemma (Negative)

**Assumptions:** $F$ defined on open $U \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$; depth-independent output gating $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$; exact trajectory preservation required: $s_t(x) = F^t(x)$ for all $x \in U$, all $t$.

**Mathematical object gated:** Output of $F$ at every depth.

**Claim:** $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ wherever $F(s_t(x)) \neq 0$.

**Proof:** At $t=0$: $g(x) \odot F(x) = F(x)$ implies $(g_i(x) - 1) F_i(x) = 0$ componentwise. Where $F_i(x) \neq 0$, $g_i(x) = 1$. Same argument at every depth. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** Direct consequence.

**Weakest assumptions for escape:** If only final-output preservation $s_D(x) = F^D(x)$ is required (not whole trajectory), non-identity gates may exist when $F^D$ is non-injective. This is underdetermined and not generally useful.

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### H2. Diagonalization Obstruction (Negative)

**Assumptions:** $\Phi(g, s)$ is injective in $g$ along the unperturbed trajectory; ideal modulation $g^*(x,t)$ is nonconstant in $t$.

**Mathematical object gated:** The entire per-depth modulation sequence $(g(x,0), \ldots, g(x,D-1))$.

**Claim:** No depth-independent gate $g(x)$ can reproduce $g^*(x,t)$ for all $t$.

**Proof:** Equality at every depth gives $\Phi(g(x), s_t(x)) = \Phi(g^*(x,t), s_t(x))$. By injectivity, $g(x) = g^*(x,t)$ for every $t$, contradicting nonconstancy. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact representational theorem.** A depth-independent gate is confined to the diagonal of $M^D$; tasks requiring depth-varying modulation lie off-diagonal.

**Weakest assumptions for escape:** Allow depth-indexed shared gate $g_\phi(x,t)$; or state-dependent gate $g(s_t)$ (LSTM-style); or per-depth trainable parameters (excluded by constraint).

**Classification:** Direct consequence; the frozen-shared-path phrasing is a plausible adaptation of control-theoretic observability reasoning.

---

### H3. Projection Rank Obstruction (Negative)

**Assumptions:** Input-dependent orthogonal projection $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$; trajectory span $\operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)), \ldots, F(s_{D-1}(x))\}$ has dimension $m(x)$.

**Mathematical object projected:** Image space of $P(x)$.

**Claim:** Exact preservation requires $\operatorname{rank} P(x) \geq m(x)$. For $K$ tasks with mutually orthogonal projections: $\sum_{i=1}^K m_i \leq d$. At most $\lfloor d / \min_i m_i \rfloor$ tasks can be isolated.

**Proof:** $P(x) F(s_t(x)) = F(s_t(x))$ forces $F(s_t(x)) \in \operatorname{Im} P(x)$. Orthogonal subspaces of $\mathbb{R}^d$ have total dimension $\leq d$. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact linear-algebra theorem.**

**Weakest assumptions for escape:** Tasks must live in low-dimensional invariant subspaces. If $m_i = 1$, up to $d$ tasks can be isolated.

**Classification:** Direct consequence; application to frozen shared paths is a plausible adaptation.

---

### H4. Parameter-Budget Bound on Gradient Isolation (Negative)

**Assumptions:** Gate has $p$ trainable parameters; task Jacobians $J_i \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times p}$; exact zero gradient interference required: $J_i J_j^\top = 0$ for $i \neq j$.

**Mathematical object constrained:** Parameter space of the gate mechanism.

**Claim:** $\sum_i \operatorname{rank}(J_i) \leq p$. If every task has full row rank and $p \leq d$, at most one task can be isolated exactly.

**Proof:** Row spaces of $J_i$ must be mutually orthogonal subspaces of $\mathbb{R}^p$. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.**

**Weakest assumptions for escape:** Each task must occupy a low-rank subspace of the gate-parameter Jacobian (e.g., disjoint input subsets, or gate architecture with task-specific feature activation).

**Classification:** Direct consequence; the frozen-path application is a plausible adaptation.

---

### H5. Error Amplification Bound (Conditional)

**Assumptions:** Modulated transition is $L_\Phi$-Lipschitz in state; per-depth gate error $\leq \delta$.

**Mathematical object bounded:** Full trajectory error.

**Claim:**

$$\|s_D(x) - s_D^*(x)\| \leq \delta \sum_{k=0}^{D-1} L_\Phi^k = \begin{cases} \delta \frac{1 - L_\Phi^D}{1 - L_\Phi} & L_\Phi < 1 \\ \delta D & L_\Phi = 1 \\ \delta \frac{L_\Phi^D - 1}{L_\Phi - 1} & L_\Phi > 1 \end{cases}$$

**Proof:** Recurrence $e_{t+1} \leq L_\Phi e_t + \delta$ solved by standard methods. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.**

**Consequence:** Bounding is uniform in depth iff $L_\Phi < 1$ (contractive). For $L_\Phi > 1$, errors grow exponentially. Trained LLM-scale maps typically have $L_\Phi > 1$, so bounding is a real architectural constraint, not a default.

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### H6. Depth-Indexed Shared Gate: Conditional Feasibility (Positive, Conjectural)

**Assumptions:** Depth-indexed shared gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ (shared parameters, $t$ as input); finite task set $(x_i, y_i)$; target modulation sequences $g_{i,t}$ exist and are reachable; sufficient gate capacity.

**Claim:** There exists a shared-parameter depth-indexed gate producing exact desired outputs for all finite task inputs.

**Proof sketch:** Finite lookup table; universal approximation on finite sets gives interpolation. $\square$

**Critical caveat:** The existence of target modulation sequences $g_{i,t}$ is **underdetermined** for generic nonlinear $F$. If $F$ is contractive, many targets are unreachable regardless of gating.

**Verdict:** **Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined.**

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation of universal approximation; reachability is a conjecture.

---

## 3. Prior Art Analysis

| Prior Art | Same Mathematical Problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|
| **Orthogonal gradient methods** (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | No — operate on gradients in parameter space during training; do not address forward-pass depth-aliasing in frozen composition | Known, partially relevant |
| **Parameter isolation / masking** (PackNet, Supermasks) | No — introduce per-task masks or per-task parameters; constraint-violating | Known, related but different |
| **Progressive networks / PathNet** | No — add task-specific architecture; effectively per-task parameter growth | Known, different problem |
| **Hypernetworks** (Ha et al.) | Partially — can generate input-dependent per-depth modulations, but output is per-layer (violates constraint in spirit) | Known, partially relevant |
| **LSTM / GRU** | **Closest** — shared-parameter gates at every time step. Crucially, gates depend on current state $h_{t-1}$, not only input $x$. This is the key difference: state-conditioning is what makes recurrent gating effective | Known, directly relevant, different information assumption |
| **Mixture of Experts** | No — routes to different parameter sets with per-layer routers; different problem | Known, structurally similar |
| **Universal Transformer / ALBERT** | Partially — weight-tied depth, but no task isolation via gates | Known, background context |

**No surveyed prior art solves this exact problem.** The formulation — isolation of computations within a frozen weight-tied path under an input-only gate, posed for continual learning — appears to be new.

---

## 4. Obstruction Characterization

The failure condition is met. The obstruction has four independent parts:

### 4.1 Diagonalization Obstruction
A depth-independent gate $g(x)$ is confined to the diagonal of $M^D$. Tasks requiring depth-varying modulation lie off-diagonal. This is representational, not algorithmic.

### 4.2 Preservation–Identity Obstruction
Exact trajectory preservation under output gating forces $g(x) = \mathbf{1}$ wherever $F$ is nonzero. A gate that changes nothing isolates nothing.

### 4.3 Rank / Parameter-Budget Obstruction
Exact gradient isolation requires mutually orthogonal task-Jacobian row spaces. $\sum_i \operatorname{rank}(J_i) \leq p$ permits at most one generic task when $p \leq d$.

### 4.4 Expansive-Dynamics Obstruction
For generic nonlinear $F$, $L_\Phi > 1$, and small gate errors amplify exponentially in depth. Even bounded interference requires architectural constraints on $F$.

---

## 5. Conditions Under Which Obstructions Vanish

| Obstruction | Escape Route |
|---|---|
| Diagonalization | Depth-indexed shared gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ or state-conditioned gate $g(s_t)$ |
| Identity-forcing | Require only final-output preservation (not whole trajectory); or relax to asymmetric preservation (exact on old tasks, free on new) |
| Rank budget | Low-rank task subspaces; disjoint feature activation in gate architecture |
| Expansive dynamics | Architectural constraint $L_\Phi < 1$; or limit depth $D$ |

---

## 6. Novelty Ledger (Adjudicated)

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formalization: isolation within frozen weight-tied composition via input-only gate, CL-framed | **Apparently novel** (as packaging) |
| Identity-forcing lemma (H1) | **Direct consequence** |
| Diagonalization obstruction (H2) | **Direct consequence** (control-theoretic flavor) |
| Rank/parameter-budget bound (H3, H4) | **Direct consequence** |
| Contraction/amplification error bound (H5) | **Direct consequence** |
| Necessity of state/depth information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/ACT practice) |
| $t$-conditioned shared gate achieves isolation broadly (H6) | **Conjecture** (reachability undetermined) |

---

## 7. Critical Open Questions

### 7.1 Asymmetric Preservation (Highest Priority)

The identity obstruction applies when preservation is required on **all** inputs. The continual-learning-relevant requirement is asymmetric: exact preservation on old-task inputs $S_{\text{old}}$, unconstrained on new-task inputs $S_{\text{new}}$. Under this reading, the identity obstruction restricts to $S_{\text{old}}$; non-trivial gates become admissible off it. The diagonal and rank obstructions survive. **This reformulation should be the primary next theorem target.**

### 7.2 Scope of $t$-Conditioning

A shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ with $t$ as input (e.g., via a fixed depth embedding) adds $O(1)$ parameters and **does not violate "no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters" as literally worded**. This is the pivotal loophole: every impossibility result targets the strictly depth-agnostic gate and dissolves into conditional statements once $t$ is admitted. The team must rule explicitly on whether the intended constraint forbids $t$-conditioning.

### 7.3 BDH Instantiation

BDH's block has residual structure and elementwise multiplicative (Hebbian-type) terms. Consequences:
- Output-gating the full map would gate the residual stream — likely harmful
- Gating only the delta term is the natural placement
- BDH's native plasticity must be explicitly disabled for the "frozen path" premise to hold

These checks against `paper.tex` and the repository were not performed in this analysis.

---

## 8. Strategic Recommendation

**The strict reading of the research question yields a clean impossibility result.** The investigation should:

1. **Publish the impossibility-and-bounding package** as a formal negative result for weight-tied architectures, citing LSTM/GRU and Universal Transformer as nearest neighbors. Claim formalization novelty, not mathematical novelty.

2. **Develop the asymmetric-preservation variant** as the honest CL formalization. This is the highest-value open result.

3. **Decide on $t$-conditioning scope.** If permitted, specify a minimal $g_\phi(x,t)$ and test empirically — labeling outcomes strictly as empirical conjecture.

4. **Instantiate with BDH's concrete update equations** from `paper.tex`; fix gate placement relative to the residual stream; confirm plasticity disabling.

5. **Reconcile with implementation reports** (`plan_v2`, `cl-h1-report`) once retrievable; verify that the implemented mechanism corresponds to one of the formally scoped variants.

---

## 9. Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without introducing per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

**Elimination:** Impossible in general under the stated constraints. Three independent obstructions (identity-forcing, diagonalization, rank budget) are proven as direct consequences of the definitions.

**Bounding:** Possible if and only if the gated dynamics are non-expansive ($L_\Phi < 1$) or depth is small enough to control exponential amplification. This is a property of $F$ and the gating rule, not achievable by the gate alone.

**Controllability:** Achievable only by enriching the gate's information — either via depth-indexed shared-parameter conditioning $g_\phi(x,t)$, state-dependent conditioning $g(s_t)$, or strong low-rank/invariant-subspace structure on the tasks. Each escape route transforms the impossibility into a conditional feasibility statement with its own capacity and reachability requirements.

**[Critic]**

## Critical Review of the Consolidated BDH Isolation Analysis

**Overall assessment.** The analysis is a useful exercise in abstraction and identifies some real distinctions (especially forward-pass *depth aliasing* versus training-time *gradient interference*). But the central claim—that exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible under the stated constraints—is overstated. Several “theorems” are either tautological under their assumptions, correct only for a degenerate version of the problem, or depend on unproved identifications between forward-pass subspaces and gradient orthogonality. The analysis also does not instantiate the actual BDH recurrence from `paper.tex`, so the results remain conditional on an abstraction that may not match the architecture.

---

## 1. The analysis is not grounded in the actual BDH equations

The main object is abstracted as

\[
s_0 = x,\qquad s_{t+1}=F(s_t).
\]

But the report does not verify from the paper that BDH is actually a simple deterministic iterated map of this form. BDH is described as a scale-free network of locally interacting neuron particles, and the report itself claims—without evidence—that the block has “residual structure and Hebbian-type terms.” If BDH includes:

- local particle interactions,
- residual streams,
- input-dependent routing,
- Hebbian plasticity,
- stochasticity,

then the abstraction \(s_{t+1}=F(s_t)\) may omit exactly the mechanisms that matter for continual learning.

The source cited for shared parameters is a third-party README (“same weights reused in all layers”), not the paper equations. That is not enough to validate the formal model. In particular, gating the whole block \(g(x)\odot F(s_t)\) is very different from gating only a residual delta \(s_t + g(x)\odot \Delta(s_t)\), or gating local interactions inside the particle graph.

**Gap:** the analysis should start from the actual BDH update equation and locate exactly where a gate or projection would be inserted. Without that, the impossibility claims are architecture-agnostic, and possibly architecture-inapplicable.

---

## 2. The notion of “input-path interference” is unstable across the analysis

The original draft defined interference as one input’s forward trajectory changing because another input’s mechanism is active. The moderator correctly observed that this cross-input forward-pass interference is structurally zero when parameters are frozen and the gate is stateless. But then the analysis substitutes a different notion: *depth-aliasing*, the mismatch between a depth-independent gate and an ideal depth-varying modulation.

These are not the same mathematical problem.

- **Depth-aliasing** is a representational issue: can a depth-constant gate imitate a depth-varying gain?
- **Continual-learning interference** is a parameter-update issue: updating shared gate parameters for task B degrades task A.

The final report calls the first notion “input-path interference,” but the actual catastrophic-forgetting channel in this setting must involve the trainable gate parameters \(\phi\), because \(F\) is frozen. The analysis only partially connects these two.

**Gap:** the paper should define the continual-learning objective explicitly:

- Tasks \(T_1,\dots,T_k\) arrive sequentially.
- \(F\) is frozen.
- \(\phi\) is updated.
- Preservation is required only on previous tasks.

This asymmetric setup is the relevant one, and it is not what the main impossibility proof uses.

---

## 3. The identity-forcing theorem is true but trivial

H1 says: under output gating,

\[
s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

exact preservation of the entire unperturbed trajectory forces \(g(x)=\mathbf 1\) wherever \(F_i\neq 0\).

This is correct, but it is almost a tautology: if the mechanism is required to reproduce the exact unmodified computation at every depth for every input, then the only possibility is no modification. The theorem does not show that isolation is impossible. It shows that **preservation of everything and alteration of anything are incompatible**, which is essentially the definition of exact preservation.

The relevant condition should be:

\[
\tilde s_t(x)=s_t(x)\quad \forall x\in S_{\text{old}},\ \forall t,
\]

while allowing \(\tilde s_t(x)\neq s_t(x)\) for \(x\in S_{\text{new}}\).

Under this asymmetric condition, the identity obstruction may disappear. The report acknowledges this only as future work, but the headline conclusion is drawn from the stronger, less relevant condition.

---

## 4. The diagonalization obstruction is not a general theorem

H2 assumes that an ideal modulation \(g^*(x,t)\) is nonconstant in \(t\), and that the transition rule \(\Phi(g,s)\) is injective in \(g\) along the trajectory. If so, a depth-independent \(g(x)\) cannot match the ideal sequence.

This is true given those assumptions, but the assumptions are not established.

- A depth-constant gate can produce depth-varying effects because the state \(s_t\) changes. For input gating,

\[
s_{t+1}=F(g(x)\odot s_t),
\]

the same \(g(x)\) can produce different effective updates at different depths because \(s_t\) differs. Therefore the set of reachable trajectories is not simply the diagonal of the product space of depth-wise modulations.

- For output gating, injectivity of \(\Phi(\cdot,s_t)\) fails on any coordinate where \(F_i(s_t)=0\), which is common with ReLU/GLU/zero activations.

Thus the “diagonalization obstruction” is conditional on a strong injectivity assumption and on the premise that the target trajectory requires nonconstant gate inputs in the same coordinate system. It is not a proof that generic tasks require off-diagonal modulation sequences. One would need a reachability argument in control theory, showing that the image of the constant-gate map does not contain the desired task map.

---

## 5. The projection rank theorem does not imply the isolation bound

H3 states:

- Exact preservation \(P(x)F(s_t)=F(s_t)\) forces

\[
\operatorname{rank}P(x)\ge \dim\operatorname{span}\{F(s_t)\}.
\]

This is correct linear algebra.

But the next step—that mutually orthogonal projections are required to avoid gradient interference—is not proved and is likely false as a general statement.

Orthogonality of the image subspaces of \(P(x_A)\) and \(P(x_B)\) is a forward-pass geometric property. Cross-task gradient interference is a property of the parameterization of \(P(x)\). If \(P(x)\) is produced by a neural network, two different inputs may have non-orthogonal or identical image subspaces but still have gradients that are orthogonal, or vice versa. The connection between these two notions depends on the architecture of the projection generator and is not automatic.

Therefore the capacity bound

\[
\sum_i m_i \le d
\]

does not follow. It only follows if one assumes a very special architecture in which each task’s projection is a fixed, learned orthogonal subspace with independent parameters. That is a much stronger assumption than “input-dependent projection.”

---

## 6. The parameter-budget bound is notationally sloppy and not operational

H4 defines task Jacobians \(J_i\in\mathbb R^{d\times p}\) as derivatives of \(s_D(x;\phi)\) with respect to \(\phi\), but tasks are sets of inputs, not single inputs. The aggregation matters.

The condition

\[
J_iJ_j^\top=0
\]

is not equivalent to zero gradient interference for general loss functions. The first-order change in task A’s loss after a gradient step on task B is governed by

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_A,\ \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_B\rangle,
\]

which involves the loss gradients, not just output Jacobians. Even at the output-Jacobian level, orthogonality of all Jacobian row spaces is a very strong condition.

Finally, the conclusion “at most one task when \(p\le d\)” is not the relevant regime. A gate network attached to an LLM-scale BDH path would typically have \(p\gg d\). If \(p\) is large, the rank budget can admit many tasks. The theorem gives no practical bound without estimating the actual Jacobian ranks.

---

## 7. The error bound statement has an incorrect “iff”

H5 derives

\[
e_{t+1}\le L_\Phi e_t+\delta,
\]

hence

\[
e_D\le \delta\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}L_\Phi^k.
\]

This is correct. But the conclusion that bounding is possible **exactly when \(L_\Phi<1\)** is not accurate. For any finite depth \(D\), the sum is finite even if \(L_\Phi>1\). The bound grows exponentially with \(D\), but it still exists.

The correct statement is:

- a bound **uniform in depth** exists iff \(L_\Phi<1\);
- for fixed finite \(D\), a bound exists for any finite \(L_\Phi\).

The analysis also asserts that trained LLM-scale maps typically have \(L_\Phi>1\). That is an empirical claim about BDH or LLMs, not proved from the paper or code. It should be labeled as such and tested.

---

## 8. H6 is conditional to the point of tautology

H6 states that a depth-indexed shared gate \(g_\phi(x,t)\) can exactly realize finite task outputs if:

1. some modulation sequence \(g_{i,t}\) realizes each target output, and  
2. \(g_\phi\) can interpolate those sequences.

Under those assumptions, the conclusion is immediate. The nontrivial question is whether assumption 1 holds: **which target input-output maps are reachable by the frozen shared path plus the allowed gate class?** That question is unproved and marked as conjecture.

Therefore H6 does not establish controllability. It reformulates the problem without solving it.

---

## 9. “Controllability” is never formally defined

The research question asks whether interference can be eliminated, bounded, or **made controllable**. The analysis focuses on elimination and bounding, but does not define controllability.

A proper formalization would likely treat the gate at each depth as a control input and ask whether the composed system

\[
s_{t+1}=F(s_t)+\text{ or }\odot\text{ gate control}
\]

can steer states to desired outputs. This would invoke nonlinear reachability, injectivity of the transition map, or approximate controllability results for recurrent networks.

Because controllability is not formalized, the report’s statement that “controllability can be achieved only by enriching gate information” is not a theorem; it is an intuitive recommendation.

---

## 10. The \(t\)-conditioning loophole is unresolved and ambiguous

The report correctly identifies that a shared-parameter gate receiving depth index \(t\) as input would avoid the strict depth-agnostic obstruction. But it does not resolve whether this is allowed.

Two cases:

- **Fixed non-learned depth encoding**, e.g. sinusoidal positional encoding: adds \(O(1)\) trainable parameters, likely does not violate the letter of “no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters.”
- **Learned depth embedding**: adds \(O(D)\) trainable embedding rows, which is exactly per-depth trainable parameters, violating the constraint.

The final recommendation says “decide on \(t\)-conditioning scope,” but the impossibility result is only valid for the stricter depth-agnostic reading. Until this scope is fixed, the answer is conditional, not definitive.

---

## 11. Prior-art review is incomplete and partly too dismissive

The report reviews parameter isolation, progressive networks, gradient projection, hypernetworks, MoE, LSTMs, and weight-tied transformers. However, some directly relevant literature is missing or dismissed too quickly:

- **FiLM / feature-wise linear modulation**:

\[
y = \gamma(x)\odot h + \beta(x),
\]

is essentially an input-dependent affine gate applied to a shared layer. Multi-task and continual variants are highly relevant.

- **Fast weight programmers and recurrent hypernetworks** that generate input-dependent modulations for a fixed recurrent core are closer to the proposed mechanism than the report admits.
- **Nonlinear RNN reachability / observability theory** would be the correct framework for the controllability question, but is absent.
- **Echo state networks** and reservoir computing may provide relevant results on fixed recurrent paths plus trained output/gate layers.

The classification “no surveyed prior art solves this exact problem” may be true, but the prior-art search is not exhaustive enough to support a strong novelty claim.

---

## 12. Novelty labeling is inconsistent

Earlier rounds label the impossibility result “apparently novel.” Later adjudication correctly says the mathematical ingredients are “direct consequences.” The final report mostly uses “direct consequence,” but still calls the packaged problem formalization “apparently novel.”

This is acceptable, but it should be made consistently: the contribution is a **problem formulation and synthesis of elementary obstructions**, not new mathematics. Overclaiming novelty would be a weakness.

---

## 13. Missing reconciliation with the H1 implementation report

The local file `2026-08-25_cl-h1-report.md` was not retrieved. If it reports empirical behavior, the formal analysis neither explains nor refutes it. The task explicitly says not to treat experimental effects as theorem evidence, but at minimum the analysis should check which formal variant the implementation corresponds to.

If the implementation used a depth-indexed or state-dependent gate, then the strict impossibility results are irrelevant to that implementation. If it used a depth-independent gate, the analysis should be directly tested against the measured behavior.

---

## Bottom line

The analysis successfully identifies:

- the distinction between forward-pass depth aliasing and training-time gradient interference,
- the fact that exact preservation of the full unperturbed trajectory forces identity under multiplicative output gating,
- the standard Lipschitz error-amplification bound.

But it fails to prove the main impossibility claim in the continual-learning-relevant setting. The actual relevant condition is **asymmetric preservation**: old tasks must be preserved, new tasks may change. That problem is not solved. The diagonalization, projection-rank, and parameter-budget obstructions do not survive as general theorems without additional, currently unproved assumptions.

The correct characterization is:

- **Strict global-preservation setting:** impossibility is trivial and proved.
- **Asymmetric CL setting:** open; the current analysis provides only necessary conditions and conjectures.
- **BDH-specific setting:** unresolved because the actual update equations were not instantiated.
- **Controllability:** not formally defined.

The report should be rewritten to state these boundaries explicitly, rather than announcing a general impossibility result.

**[Optimizer]**

# Refined Analysis: Input-Path Interference, Gating/Projection, and Computation Isolation in a Frozen Shared Sequential Composition

*This document is a revised version of the earlier draft. It incorporates the critical review by (i) clarifying the different meanings of “interference,” (ii) sharply separating exact results from conditional or conjectural statements, (iii) correcting overclaiming in the diagonalization and projection arguments, and (iv) making the continual-learning-relevant asymmetric problem the central object of analysis.*

---

## 0. Evidence and Scope

The public BDH paper (arXiv:2509.26507) and third-party material confirm that BDH is a scale-free, biologically inspired network of locally interacting neuron particles and that it reuses shared parameters across depth (“recurrent depth” in existing implementations). The internal project reports named in the task could not be retrieved during this analysis, so no claim below is checked against the literal BDH update equations or the H1 implementation report.

Accordingly, this analysis is about the following **abstraction**, which the reports assume:

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F_\theta(s_t(x)),\qquad t=0,\dots,D-1.
\]

All theorems are proved for this abstraction. They are not automatically theorems about BDH until \(F\) is replaced by the actual BDH particle update. In particular, BDH may have a residual structure or Hebbian-style multiplicative terms; the *placement* of a gate (on the whole block, on a residual delta, or on an internal interaction) is part of the design and is not settled here.

---

## 1. Formalizations

### 1.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let \(\mathcal{X}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^d\) be the input/state space and let \(F:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\) be a fixed map.

A **depth-\(D\) shared sequential composition** is the iteration

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)).
\]

The unperturbed trajectory is

\[
\tau_F(x)=\bigl(s_0(x),s_1(x),\dots,s_D(x)\bigr).
\]

If BDH uses residual connections, the relevant recurrence is instead

\[
s_{t+1}(x)=s_t(x)+F(s_t(x)).
\]

The theory below applies to the abstract iterated map; each gating theorem must be re-instantiated for the residual variant.

### 1.2 Frozen Shared Path

The shared path \(F_\theta\) is **frozen** if its parameters \(\theta\) are not updated during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection mechanism may be trained:

\[
\frac{\partial F_\theta}{\partial \theta}=0
\]

during all adaptation steps. Nothing below relies on retraining \(F\).

### 1.3 Gating and Projection

**Input-dependent gate.** A map

\[
g:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}^d
\]

that is applied identically at every depth. The two canonical placements are:

\[
\text{output gating:}\quad s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

\[
\text{input gating:}\quad s_{t+1}=F\bigl(g(x)\odot s_t\bigr).
\]

For residual blocks, the natural variant is

\[
\text{delta gating:}\quad s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t).
\]

The gate may have trainable parameters \(\phi\), but the strict version of the research question forbids per-depth trainable parameter vectors. We will also consider the relaxed variant

\[
g_\phi(x,t),
\]

where the depth index is an **input** to the gate rather than an index into a separate parameter table. If \(t\) is encoded by a fixed, non-trainable embedding, this does not introduce per-depth trainable parameters. If a learned embedding indexed by \(t\) is used, it does.

**Input-dependent projection.** A map

\[
P:\mathcal{X}\to \Pi(\mathbb{R}^d),
\]

where \(\Pi(\mathbb{R}^d)\) is the set of orthogonal projections, applied as

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t)\quad\text{or}\quad s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t).
\]

### 1.4 Input-Path Interference: Three Distinct Notions

The word “interference” is ambiguous. We separate three notions.

**(A) Cross-input forward-pass contamination.**

If \(F\) is frozen and the gate is a stateless function of the input \(x\), then the trajectory of \(x_1\) is *computationally independent* of the presence or absence of \(x_2\). Formally,

\[
\tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1\mid x_2\text{ processed elsewhere}) = \tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1).
\]

Thus this notion, which appeared in the original draft, is **structurally zero** in this setting. It is not a meaningful measure of interference here.

**(B) Forward depth-aliasing.**

A depth-agnostic gate \(g(x)\) applies the same modulation at every depth. If an oracle with access to depth \(t\) would apply a reference modulation \(g^*(x,t)\), the **depth-aliasing error** for input \(x\) is

\[
\mathrm{DA}(g;x)
=
\sum_{t=0}^{D-1}
\left\|
\Phi\bigl(g(x),s_t(x)\bigr)
-
\Phi\bigl(g^*(x,t),s_t(x)\bigr)
\right\|,
\]

where \(\Phi\) is the chosen gating/projection rule. This is a *representational* mismatch inside a single forward pass.

**(C) Training-time gradient interference (cross-task forgetting).**

Because the gate parameters \(\phi\) are shared across all inputs, updating \(\phi\) on task \(B\) may change the gate output on task-\(A\) inputs. First-order damage to task \(A\) from one gradient step on task \(B\) is proportional to

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_A(\phi),\; \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_B(\phi)\rangle,
\]

where \(\mathcal{L}_i\) is the task loss. If this is nonzero, the shared gate is a vector of forgetting.

The operationally relevant channels for continual learning are **(B)** (within a pass) and **(C)** (across passes). Cross-input forward contamination, notion **(A)**, is not a live channel under a frozen stateless model.

### 1.5 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a task family \(\{(S_i,y_i)\}_{i=1}^K\) if:

1. each task output is produced exactly:

\[
\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\qquad\forall x\in S_i,\ \forall i;
\]

2. there is zero forward depth-aliasing error on the task trajectories whenever a reference trajectory is specified;

3. there is zero first-order cross-task gradient interference:

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_i(\phi),\nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_j(\phi)\rangle=0
\qquad\forall i\neq j.
\]

A mechanism **bounds interference** if the relevant error is at most \(\varepsilon\), with \(\varepsilon\) controlled by explicit quantities: Lipschitz constants, depth, gate error, capacity, task geometry.

### 1.6 Elimination versus Bounding

- **Elimination** means exact equality of outputs and exact zero interference in the sense above.
- **Bounding** means a finite worst-case deviation \(\varepsilon\).
- A third mode, **controllability**, is often invoked informally. We formalize it as *reachability*: a task target \(y_i(x)\) is **reachable** if there exists some gate parameterization \(\phi\) such that \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\) for all \(x\in S_i\). The mechanism is controllable over a task sequence if every finite sequence of reachable tasks is simultaneously realizable by a single \(\phi\).

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses

### H1. Global Exact Preservation Forces the Gate to Be the Identity

**Assumptions.**

- \(F\) is defined on an open set \(U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^d\).
- The mechanism is output gating with a depth-agnostic gate:

\[
s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t).
\]

- Exact preservation of the *entire unperturbed trajectory* is required:

\[
\tilde s_t(x)=F^t(x)\qquad\forall x\in U,\ \forall t=0,\dots,D.
\]

**Mathematical object gated.**  
The output of the frozen map \(F\) at every depth.

**Claim.**  
For every \(x\) and every coordinate \(i\),

\[
\bigl(g_i(x)-1\bigr)\, F_i(s_t(x))=0.
\]

Hence, wherever \(F_i(s_t(x))\neq0\) along the trajectory, \(g_i(x)=1\). If \(F\) is componentwise nonzero along all trajectories on \(U\), then \(g(x)=\mathbf{1}\) for all \(x\).

**Proof.**  
At depth \(t\), exact preservation gives

\[
g(x)\odot F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x)).
\]

Componentwise, \((g_i(x)-1)F_i(s_t(x))=0\). Repeating for all \(t\) gives the result. \(\square\)

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem**, but it is close to a tautology: *if the mechanism is required to change nothing, then it changes nothing.* It does **not** prove that computation isolation is impossible. It proves that exact global preservation and nontrivial gating are incompatible.

**Weakest assumptions for escape.**  
If preservation is required only for final outputs, or only on a subset \(S_{\text{old}}\) of inputs, the identity obstruction becomes restricted to that subset. This is the continual-learning-relevant relaxation.

**Classification.**  
Known / direct consequence.

---

### H2. Asymmetric Preservation: The Honest Continual-Learning Setting

**Assumptions.**

- Old-task input set \(S_{\text{old}}\subset\mathcal{X}\).
- New-task input set \(S_{\text{new}}\subset\mathcal{X}\).
- Output gating \(s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t)\).
- Exact preservation is required only on old inputs:

\[
\tilde s_t(x)=F^t(x)\qquad\forall x\in S_{\text{old}},\ \forall t.
\]

No preservation constraint is imposed on \(S_{\text{new}}\).

**Mathematical object constrained.**  
The restriction of the gate function \(g\) to \(S_{\text{old}}\).

**Claim.**  
For \(x\in S_{\text{old}}\),

\[
g_i(x)=1
\quad\text{whenever}\quad
\exists t:\ F_i(s_t(x))\neq0.
\]

On the zero-coordinate set

\[
Z(x)=\{i:\ F_i(s_t(x))=0\ \forall t\},
\]

the gate is unconstrained by preservation. On \(S_{\text{new}}\), the gate is entirely unconstrained by this requirement.

**Proof.**  
Same componentwise argument as H1, restricted to \(S_{\text{old}}\). \(\square\)

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem.** This theorem replaces the trivial global statement with a useful necessary condition. In particular, if the old-task trajectories have support in all coordinates, then the gate is forced to be identity on all of \(S_{\text{old}}\).

**Remark.** If additionally old and new inputs are disjoint and the gate network can interpolate between the identity mapping on \(S_{\text{old}}\) and the desired nontrivial mapping on \(S_{\text{new}}\), then exact isolation is *not* ruled out by the identity obstruction. The real obstruction becomes task separability and gate capacity, not preservation per se.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence, but the asymmetric framing is the correct continual-learning formalization.

---

### H3. Input-Only Mechanisms Cannot Resolve Task Ambiguity at Overlapping Inputs

**Assumptions.**

- The mechanism is a deterministic function of the input: \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=\mathcal{M}_\phi(x)\).
- Two tasks \(A,B\) share an input: \(x\in S_A\cap S_B\).
- Their target outputs differ: \(y_A(x)\neq y_B(x)\).

**Mathematical object constrained.**  
The input–output map \(\mathcal{M}_\phi\).

**Claim.**  
No single parameter vector \(\phi\) can satisfy both tasks. Exact computation isolation is impossible for this task family.

**Proof.**  
The map is a function: for a fixed \(\phi\), it returns exactly one output for the input \(x\). Two different required outputs are incompatible. \(\square\)

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem.** This is a genuine obstruction of the “input-dependent-only” constraint, independent of depth, nonlinearity, and capacity.

**Weakest assumptions for escape.**  
Tasks must have disjoint input supports, or target functions must agree on overlaps, or the mechanism must be augmented with a task/context input. Any of these relaxations removes the obstruction.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence; essentially an identifiability condition.

---

### H4. Depth-Agnostic Gating Is a Representational Limitation, Not a General Behavioral Impossibility

**Assumptions.**

- The transition is \(\Phi(g,s)\).
- The *gate-value sequence* required by an oracle is \(g^*(x,0),\dots,g^*(x,D-1)\), nonconstant in \(t\).
- The transition is injective in the gate argument along the trajectory.

**Mathematical object gated.**  
The sequence of gate values applied at each depth.

**Claim.**  
A depth-agnostic gate \(g(x)\) cannot reproduce the nonconstant gate-value sequence \(g^*(x,t)\).

**Proof.**  
If it did, equality at every depth would give

\[
\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))=\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x)).
\]

By injectivity, \(g(x)=g^*(x,t)\) for every \(t\), contradicting nonconstancy. \(\square\)

**Important correction.**  
This theorem is **not** a general behavioral impossibility. A constant-in-depth gate can produce depth-varying effective behavior because the state \(s_t\) changes.

**Counterexample to the stronger claim.**  
Let \(F(s)=s\) and \(D=2\). Output gating gives

\[
s_2=g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x.
\]

A constant gate \(g(x)=(\alpha,\beta)\) produces

\[
s_2=(\alpha^2 x_1,\ \beta^2 x_2).
\]

A depth-varying gate sequence \(g^*_0=(c,1)\), \(g^*_1=(1,c)\) would produce \((c x_1,\ c x_2)\), which is also achievable by the constant gate \((\sqrt c,\sqrt c)\) when \(c\ge0\). More generally, constant gates can emulate many depth-varying controls by exploiting state feedback.

Thus the earlier claim that “a depth-agnostic gate is confined to the diagonal of the modulation product space” is a statement about **gate values**, not about **reachable input–output maps**. It does not, by itself, prove an impossibility for task behaviors.

**Verdict.**  
**Underdetermined as a general theorem.** The representational statement is true; the behavioral conclusion does not follow.

**Weakest assumptions for a behavioral theorem.**  
One would need a control-theoretic reachability analysis: show that the image of the constant-gate map does not contain the desired task map for a specific \(F\), task set, and gate class. This is task-specific.

**Classification.**  
The representational lemma is a direct consequence. The broad impossibility is **false** as stated; the reachability question is open.

---

### H5. Projection Mechanisms: A Necessary Rank Condition

**Assumptions.**

- Output projection:

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t).
\]

- Exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory of \(x\) is required.

**Mathematical object projected.**  
The image subspace of the projection \(P(x)\).

**Claim.**  
If

\[
m(x)=\dim\operatorname{span}\left\{F(s_0(x)),F(s_1(x)),\dots,F(s_{D-1}(x))\right\},
\]

then exact preservation requires

\[
\operatorname{rank} P(x)\ge m(x).
\]

**Proof.**  
Exact preservation gives

\[
P(x)F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x))
\]

for every \(t\). Hence all \(F(s_t(x))\) belong to \(\operatorname{Im}P(x)\), whose dimension is \(\operatorname{rank}P(x)\). \(\square\)

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact linear-algebra theorem.**

**What this theorem does not say.**  
It does **not** imply a cross-task capacity bound \(\sum_i m_i\le d\). Orthogonality of image subspaces of two projections is a *forward-pass geometric* property, whereas cross-task gradient interference is a property of the *parameterization* of \(P(x)\). Two inputs can have non-orthogonal image subspaces but orthogonal gate gradients, or vice versa. The earlier draft conflated these. The rank budget reappears below only under an explicit parameterization assumption.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence.

---

### H6. Gradient Interference and the Parameter Budget

**Assumptions.**

- The gate/projection mechanism has \(p\) trainable parameters \(\phi\).
- For task \(i\), define per-example output Jacobian

\[
J_i(x)=\frac{\partial}{\partial \phi}\tilde s_D(x;\phi).
\]

- Let \(R_i\subseteq\mathbb{R}^p\) be the row space of the collection of Jacobians over task \(i\).
- We require *zero first-order interference for every smooth loss and every example*.

**Mathematical object constrained.**  
The parameter space of the gate mechanism.

**Claim.**  
If zero interference is required for all smooth losses, then the row spaces \(\{R_i\}\) must be mutually orthogonal:

\[
R_i\perp R_j\qquad\forall i\neq j.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\sum_{i=1}^K \dim R_i\le p.
\]

**Proof.**  
For losses \(\mathcal{L}_i\), first-order interference is

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_i,\ \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_j\rangle.
\]

For this to vanish for *arbitrary* loss functions, the linear functionals induced by the Jacobian row spaces must be mutually orthogonal, which forces row-space orthogonality. Orthogonal subspaces of \(\mathbb{R}^p\) have total dimension at most \(p\). \(\square\)

**Important caveats.**

1. For fixed, specific losses, zero gradient interference is the weaker condition

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_i,\ \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_j\rangle=0,
\]

which can hold even when Jacobian row spaces overlap.
2. The bound is **not** a depth obstruction; it applies to any shared-parameter mechanism.
3. If \(p\) is large relative to \(d\), the bound does not stop many tasks from being isolated. It becomes restrictive only when the task Jacobians have full row rank and \(p\le d\).

**Verdict.**  
**True as an exact theorem under the stated generality.** It is standard parameter-isolation accounting, not a new result.

**Classification.**  
Direct consequence.

---

### H7. Error Amplification: Bounding Is Conditional on Dynamics, Not Automatic

**Assumptions.**

- The gated transition \(\Phi(g,s)\) is Lipschitz in state with constant \(L\):

\[
\|\Phi(g,s)-\Phi(g,s')\|\le L\|s-s'\|.
\]

- The per-depth gate error against a reference is bounded:

\[
\|\Phi(g(x),s_t^*(x))-\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t^*(x))\|\le \delta_t.
\]

**Mathematical object bounded.**  
The final-state error.

**Claim.**

\[
\|s_D(x)-s_D^*(x)\|
\le
\sum_{t=0}^{D-1} L^{D-1-t}\delta_t.
\]

If \(\delta_t\le\delta\), then

\[
e_D\le
\begin{cases}
\delta\,\dfrac{1-L^D}{1-L}, & L<1,\\[1ex]
\delta D, & L=1,\\[1ex]
\delta\,\dfrac{L^D-1}{L-1}, & L>1.
\end{cases}
\]

**Proof.**  
Let \(e_t=\|s_t-s_t^*\|\). Then

\[
e_{t+1}
\le
\|\Phi(g,s_t)-\Phi(g,s_t^*)\|
+
\|\Phi(g,s_t^*)-\Phi(g^*,s_t^*)\|
\le
L e_t+\delta_t.
\]

Solving the linear recurrence gives the stated bound. \(\square\)

**Precise interpretation.**

- For **fixed finite depth \(D\)**, the bound is finite for every finite \(L\). There is no “iff \(L<1\)” statement for finite depth.
- A bound that is **uniform in depth** exists iff \(L<1\). At \(L=1\), the bound grows linearly in \(D\); at \(L>1\), exponentially.

**Empirical caveat.**  
The claim “trained LLM-scale maps typically have \(L>1\)” is an empirical conjecture, not a theorem. It should be tested for a given BDH model.

**Classification.**  
Known / direct consequence.

---

### H8. Finite-Task Reachability: A Conditional Construction, Otherwise Underdetermined

**Assumptions.**

- The task family is finite: \((x_i,y_i)\), \(i=1,\dots,N\), with task inputs possibly grouped into tasks.
- The gate family has sufficient capacity to interpolate finite sets.
- For each task input \(x\), the required output is reachable by the allowed gate class:

\[
y_i(x)\in \mathcal{R}_D(x):=\{\tilde s_D(x;\phi):\phi\in\Phi\}.
\]

- If an input appears in more than one task, the target outputs agree on the overlap.

**Mathematical object controlled.**  
The finite input–output relation of the gated composition.

**Claim.**  
Under these assumptions, there exists a parameter vector \(\phi\) realizing all finite task targets exactly.

**Proof sketch.**  
The requirements form a finite set of constraints. Reachability gives a target gate output for each input; universal/interpolation capacity of the gate family fits those finite constraints. \(\square\)

**Counterexamples showing underdetermination.**

- If \(F(s)=0\), then \(\mathcal{R}_D(x)=\{0\}\) for every \(x\), regardless of gating. Nontrivial outputs are impossible.
- If the same input belongs to two tasks with different target outputs, H3 makes simultaneous realization impossible.
- If \(F(s)=s\) and the gate is multiplicative output gating with \(D=2\), the reachable set is

\[
\{g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x\}.
\]

Many targets are reachable only if coordinatewise signs and roots are permitted; others are not.

**Verdict.**  
**Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined.** This is not a theorem about all nonlinear \(F\).

**Weakest additional assumptions.**  
Reachability of each target, disjoint task inputs, and gate capacity are sufficient. The reachability condition is the nontrivial one and must be checked for the concrete \(F\).

**Classification.**  
Plausible adaptation of finite interpolation and universal approximation; the reachability part is a **conjecture** unless proved for a specific \(F\).

---

## 3. Summary of Verdicts

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Status |
|---|---|---|
| H1: Global preservation forces identity | True | Exact theorem; direct consequence |
| H2: Asymmetric preservation constraint | True | Exact theorem; CL-relevant |
| H3: Overlapping inputs with different targets | Impossible | Exact theorem; identifiability |
| H4: Depth-agnostic gate-value limitation | Representational only | Direct consequence; behavioral claim false/underdetermined |
| H5: Projection rank condition | True | Exact linear algebra; not sufficient for isolation |
| H6: Gradient interference parameter budget | True | Exact under strong generality |
| H7: Error amplification bound | True | Exact; finite-depth vs uniform-depth distinction |
| H8: Finite-task reachability | Conditional | Plausible adaptation; reachability is conjecture |

---

## 4. Prior Art: Does It Address the Same Mathematical Problem?

| Prior art | What it actually does | Same problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gradient projection / orthogonal gradient methods (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | Projects gradients in parameter space so new-task updates do not increase old-task losses | Partly: addresses training-time gradient interference; not forward depth-aliasing; usually modifies the main network | Known, partially relevant |
| Parameter isolation / masks (PackNet, Supermasks, Piggyback) | Allocates disjoint parameter subsets to tasks | No: creates per-task parameters/masks; constraint-violating in the stated setting | Known, related but different |
| Progressive networks / PathNet | Adds new task-specific columns/paths | No: per-task architectural growth | Known, different problem |
| Hypernetworks / fast weight programmers | Generate weights or modulations for a main network | Partly: can generate input-dependent modulations; typically per-layer outputs, which violates the “no per-depth parameters” constraint in spirit | Known, partially relevant |
| FiLM / feature-wise linear modulation | Input-dependent affine modulation of hidden features | Partly: same gating algebra, but usually per-layer parameters; not a frozen weight-tied path | Known, directly relevant |
| LSTM / GRU | Shared-parameter state-dependent gates in a recurrent path | Closest: same composition structure; but gates see the current state, not only the original input | Known, nearest prior art with a different information assumption |
| Mixture of experts / conditional computation | Input-dependent routing to expert parameter sets | No: per-layer routers and distinct experts | Known, structurally similar |
| Reservoir computing / echo state networks | Fixed random recurrent path with trained readout | Partly: frozen recurrent core plus trainable output; not input-dependent forward gating for task isolation | Known, relevant background |
| Weight-tied transformers (Universal Transformer, ALBERT) | Reuse a block across depth | Partly: same composition; no task-isolation gating | Known, background context |
| Nonlinear RNN observability / reachability theory | Characterizes what a recurrent map can reach | Yes for the reachability question, but not used in the draft | Known, should be used for H8 |

**Conclusion.** No surveyed prior art solves the exact problem: isolation of computations inside one frozen shared sequential composition using only an input-dependent gate, with no per-depth trainable parameters. The closest neighbors are LSTM/GRU, FiLM, and reservoir computing.

---

## 5. Novelty Ledger

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formalization of two live interference channels (depth-aliasing and gradient interference) in a frozen weight-tied path | **Apparently novel as packaging**; ingredients known |
| H1: exact global preservation forces identity gate | **Direct consequence** |
| H2: asymmetric preservation constraint on old inputs | **Direct consequence**, but CL-relevant reframing |
| H3: overlapping-input task ambiguity | **Direct consequence** |
| H4: depth-agnostic gate-value representational limitation | **Direct consequence**; behavioral conclusion underdetermined/not generally true |
| H5: projection rank condition | **Direct consequence** |
| H6: parameter budget for exact zero gradient interference | **Direct consequence** |
| H7: Lipschitz error amplification | **Direct consequence** |
| H8: finite-task reachability via gate interpolation | **Plausible adaptation / conjecture** |
| Claim that state or depth information is necessary for robust gating | **Plausible adaptation**, consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice, not a general theorem |
| Empirical claim “LLM-scale \(F\) is expansive” | **Empirical conjecture**, not theorem |

No experimental result is used as evidence for any theorem in this document.

---

## 6. Failure Condition and Main Obstructions

The research question asks whether input-path interference can be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable without per-depth trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path.

The strictest reading is:

- depth-agnostic gate/projection,
- frozen \(F\),
- exact preservation of the original computation on all inputs.

Under that strict reading:

1. **Elimination is impossible** in the uninteresting sense of H1: the gate must be identity on all inputs, so it provides no isolation.
2. **Bounding** is possible only under the dynamics condition of H7: uniform bounds require \(L<1\); otherwise errors can grow with depth.
3. **Controllability** is not a well-posed theorem without a reachability analysis of the specific \(F\) and gate class.

However, the strict reading is **not** the relevant continual-learning formulation.

We therefore treat the **asymmetric continual-learning reading** as central:

- old tasks: exact preservation of \(F^D(x)\) on \(S_{\text{old}}\);
- new tasks: free to change;
- no task label in the input;
- no per-depth trainable parameters.

Under this reading, the results are:

- The identity obstruction restricts to \(S_{\text{old}}\) (H2).
- Overlapping task inputs with different demands are impossible (H3).
- Exact zero cross-task gradient interference imposes a rank/parameter budget if demanded for all losses (H6).
- Whether nontrivial old/new isolation is possible is **underdetermined** without a reachability analysis (H4, H8).

**The main obstruction is therefore not a single theorem.** It is a conjunction of:

1. **Task identifiability**: input-only gates cannot separate same-input different-output tasks.
2. **Preservation–identity tension**: global preservation kills the gate; asymmetric preservation restricts it to old-task zero-coordinate directions.
3. **Gradient coupling**: shared gate parameters couple tasks unless heavy orthogonality/low-rank conditions are imposed.
4. **Dynamics amplification**: if the gated composition is expanding, small deviations from ideal gate behavior are amplified exponentially in depth.

None of these is an algorithmic failure; they are representational and algebraic. But none of them, by itself, proves a general impossibility of asymmetric continual learning with a frozen shared path. The negative claim must therefore be scoped:

> **Exact elimination is impossible under the trivial global-preservation reading. Under the asymmetric continual-learning reading, the problem is open; this analysis provides necessary conditions and conditional constructions, not a general impossibility.**

---

## 7. What Would Make the Question Decidable?

### 7.1 Instantiate \(F\)

The first step is to replace the abstract \(F\) with BDH’s actual update from `paper.tex`. The gate placement then becomes concrete:

- If BDH uses a residual block \(s_{t+1}=s_t+F(s_t)\), gating the whole block may gate the residual stream; the natural placement is \(s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)\).
- If BDH has Hebbian-style multiplicative terms, a gate on those terms is not the same as a gate on the full block.
- BDH’s native plasticity must be explicitly disabled for the frozen-path premise.

### 7.2 Resolve the \(t\)-conditioning interpretation

The deepest loophole is the meaning of “no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters”:

- A gate \(g_\phi(x,t)\) where \(t\) is a **fixed, non-trainable encoding** adds \(O(1)\) trainable parameters and does not literally violate the constraint.
- A **learned depth embedding** adds \(O(D)\) trainable parameters and does violate the constraint.

Every theorem above assumes the former is excluded. If the team permits fixed depth indexing, the depth-agnostic obstruction disappears, and the question becomes a reachability/interpolation question (H8). The team must decide this before any experiment is interpreted.

### 7.3 Formalize controllability

The research question asks whether interference can be “made controllable.” That requires a definition. A workable definition is reachability of finite task targets by the gate class without modifying \(F\). The control-theoretic machinery of nonlinear discrete-time reachability and observability should be applied to the specific \(F\).

---

## 8. Direct Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without introducing per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

**Elimination.**

- Under the trivial global-preservation reading: **impossible**, because the only gate that preserves everything is the identity gate.
- Under the meaningful asymmetric continual-learning reading: **not generally proved either possible or impossible**. The surviving necessary conditions are task-input disjointness (H3), identity restriction on old-task coordinates (H2), and gradient orthogonality/low-rank budgets for exact zero cross-task interference (H6).

**Bounding.**

- **Possible conditionally.** If the gated transition is non-expansive (\(L<1\)), bounds are uniform in depth. If \(L>1\), deviations can grow exponentially in depth. A finite-depth bound always exists for finite \(L\), but may be uselessly large.

**Controllability.**

- **Underdetermined without a reachability analysis.** If each task target is reachable by the gate class, finite task sets can be interpolated, subject to gate capacity and task-input disjointness. Whether BDH’s frozen map admits such reachability is not known from the public material.

**Overall.**

The strict form of the proposed mechanism does not provide a general solution. The obstruction is not any single nonlinearity of \(F\); it is the combination of input-only task identification, shared-parameter gradient coupling, and preservation constraints. The productive next step is to instantiate the abstract \(F\) with BDH’s concrete update, decide the \(t\)-conditioning question, and then either prove or disprove reachability for the specific gate class.

## 0. Evidence-Base Status (unchanged, recorded for the archive)

- The BDH paper (arXiv:2509.26507) and third-party implementations corroborate that BDH reuses **shared parameters across depth** ("recurrent depth") with sparse non-negative activations. The *literal* update equations from `paper.tex` were never retrieved in-debate; neither were the internal reports (`2026-08-*`, `plan_v2`, `cl-h1-report`). Consequently every result below is proved for the **abstraction** $s_{t+1}=\Phi(g(x),s_t)$ over a frozen map $F$, with a residual variant $s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)$ treated as the likely BDH-shaped instantiation. Instantiation remains a mandatory follow-up (§8).
- No experimental result from any report was admitted as evidence for any theorem. This discipline held through all three rounds and is confirmed in the audit (§9).

---

## 1. Trajectory of the Debate (process record)

The debate executed exactly the correction loop the brief demanded:

1. **Round 2** produced a unified impossibility package — identity-forcing + diagonalization + rank budget — headlined as "exact elimination is impossible in general."
2. **Round 3 CRITIC** landed five substantive defeaters: (i) H1 is true but near-tautological and drawn from a degenerate preservation requirement; (ii) the diagonalization argument is a statement about gate *values*, not reachable *behaviors* — constant-depth gates emulate depth-varying effects through state feedback, and injectivity fails at zero activations; (iii) the projection capacity bound $\sum m_i \le d$ committed a category error (image-subspace geometry ≠ parameter-space gradient geometry); (iv) the error-bound "iff $L<1$" misquantified (finite-$D$ vs. uniform-in-$D$); (v) "controllability" was never defined. Plus: missing BDH grounding, missing prior art (FiLM, fast-weight programmers, reservoir computing), and the unresolved $t$-conditioning scope.
3. **Round 3 responses:** OPTIMIZER conceded essentially all points and *restructured* — adding two genuinely new exact results (asymmetric-preservation constraint; overlapping-input identifiability), withdrawing the invalid corollary, fixing the quantifiers, and reframing the headline. STRATEGIST incorporated the asymmetric-preservation and $t$-conditioning flags into its own §7 while retaining a stronger headline.

**Adjudication principle applied throughout:** a claim enters the record only at the strength of its *weakest surviving proof*.

---

## 2. Final Adjudicated Verdicts

|---|-------|--------------|-------|
| **V1** | Identity-forcing: exact trajectory preservation on input set $S$ under depth-agnostic multiplicative gating forces $g_i(x)=1$ for every $(x,i)$ with coordinate $i$ active along $x$'s trajectory | **TRUE — exact theorem, direct consequence** | Near-tautological. Its real content is *locative*: it identifies precisely where gate freedom lives (never-active coordinates; non-$S$ inputs). |
| **V2** | Cross-input forward-pass contamination is structurally zero for stateless mechanisms on a frozen path | **ESTABLISHED** | Caveat added in synthesis: any *stateful* component inside the gate (running-statistics normalization, caches) revives this channel. Statelessness must be listed as an explicit design premise. |
| **V3** | Identifiability: a deterministic input-only mechanism cannot satisfy two tasks demanding different outputs at a shared input | **TRUE — exact theorem** | The sharpest structural limit of "input-dependent only," independent of depth, nonlinearity, capacity. Practical force in LM-CL depends on measure-theoretic overlap of task supports; do not overclaim either direction. |
| **V4** | Diagonalization: depth-agnostic gate confined to diagonal of modulation space | **Representational lemma TRUE; behavioral impossibility NOT established — downgraded** | Counterexample accepted ($F(s)=s$, $D=2$: constant gate matches depth-varying final outputs via state feedback); injectivity fails at zeros. Superseded by the open reachability question. |
| **V5** | Projection rank: $\operatorname{rank}P(x)\ge m(x)$ necessary for exact preservation | **TRUE — exact linear algebra** | Capacity corollary $\sum_i m_i \le d$ **WITHDRAWN** (invalid inference from forward-pass geometry to gradient geometry). Survives only under a special independent-subspace parameterization that violates the spirit of the constraints anyway. |

Worst-case guarantee, not typical-case behavior. |

| **V8** | Reachability/interpolation: finite task sets realizable iff targets reachable by gate class + capacity + disjointness/agreement | **CONDITIONAL; crux is a conjecture** | Reachability on a concrete frozen $F$ is the decisive unproven assumption; counterexamples ($F\equiv 0$; sign/root obstructions) show it fails generically without hypotheses on $F$. |

## 3. Consensus Evaluation

**Full consensus (all three roles, post-revision):**
- The formal skeleton (shared composition, frozen path, gate/projection placements, elimination-vs-bounding distinction).
- V1–V3, V5–V8 as scoped above.
- The two-channel picture: the live interference channels are **forward depth-aliasing** (within a pass) and **cross-time gradient interference** (across passes); naive cross-input contamination is dead (V2).
- Prior-art triage, extended in Round 3: LSTM/GRU (nearest neighbor; state-conditioned), FiLM (same gating algebra, per-layer parameters), fast-weight programmers/hypernetworks (adjacent, per-layer outputs), reservoir computing (frozen core + trained readout), orthogonal-gradient methods (parameter-space, complementary — see §7-C1), progressive networks/PackNet/supermasks (constraint-violating), MoE (different problem), Universal Transformer/ALBERT (backdrop). **No surveyed work solves the exact problem**: isolation within one frozen weight-tied composition via an input-only gate without per-depth trainable parameters.

**Residual dissent, adjudicated:**
- STRATEGIST's Round-3 headline ("elimination impossible in general") versus the OPTIMIZER/CRITIC scoped formulation. **Ruling:** the scoped formulation prevails. The unscoped headline holds only under global preservation on *all* inputs — a reading under which the gate must be inert everywhere, hence no adaptation anywhere, hence not a continual-learning question at all. STRATEGIST's own §7.1 concedes the asymmetric setting is the honest one and open; that concession is entered into the record as binding. The debate's central epistemic event is precisely this downgrade: **the general impossibility theorem dissolved upon inspection; what survives is a set of exact necessary conditions plus an open reachability problem.**

---

## 4. Final Answer to the Research Question

> *Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?*

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic status |
|---|---|---|
| **Elimination**, global-preservation reading (mechanism preserves $F$'s behavior on *all* inputs) | Trivially impossible — the only admissible gate is the identity, so the mechanism does nothing | Proved; direct consequence (degenerate) |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL reading (exact preservation on $S_{\text{old}}$, free on $S_{\text{new}}$) | **Open.** Necessary conditions established: identity confinement on old-task active coordinates (V1-scoped); input-disjointness/target-agreement (V3); worst-case gradient budget (V6); dynamics amplification (V7). No general possibility *or* impossibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite $L_\Phi$; **uniform-in-depth** bounding iff gated dynamics contractive ($L_\Phi<1$). Bounding is a property of the composed system, not achievable by gate design alone under expansion | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | **Not yet a well-posed question**: requires formal definition (reachability of task targets by the gate class on the concrete frozen $F$) and BDH instantiation | Undefined pending instantiation |

The brief's failure condition is therefore met **only in the degenerate reading**. In the CL-relevant reading the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture (reachability).* Reporting anything stronger would violate the novelty discipline.

---

## 5. Obstruction Characterization (deliverable under the failure clause)

There is no single obstruction theorem in the meaningful setting. The failure surface is a **conjunction of four necessary conditions**, each independently capable of killing the program, none individually fatal:

1. **Identifiability failure** — conflicting targets on overlapping inputs are unservable by any input-only mechanism (V3). In language-modeling continual learning, supports overlap extensively; escape requires context-sufficiency of the input or task conditioning.
2. **Preservation-geometry confinement** — the gate is pinned to the identity on every (old input, active coordinate) pair (V1-scoped). Freedom survives only on never-active coordinates (substantial under sparse activations) and on new-task inputs.
3. **Gradient coupling of shared $\phi$** — new-task updates generically move $g_\phi$ off the identity on old inputs; worst-case immunity costs a rank budget (V6) that is either restrictive ($p\le d$) or vacuous ($p\gg d$), and is unnecessary for specific loss pairs.
4. **Depth amplification** — under expansive gated dynamics, per-depth modulation error grows geometrically in $D$ (V7); bounding then demands architectural contractivity, which is an assumption on $F$, not a capability of the gate.

Plus three **scope conditionals** on which every result silently rests: (a) statelessness of the mechanism (else V2 dies); (b) the $t$-conditioning interpretation (below); (c) BDH's native Hebbian plasticity actually disabled (else the path is not frozen).

**The $t$-conditioning fork (recorded as the pivotal scope decision):** a shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ with $t$ supplied by a *fixed, non-trainable* encoding adds $O(1)$ parameters and does not violate the constraint as literally worded — and it dissolves V1's depth-agnostic restrictions and V4's residue entirely, converting the problem into pure reachability (V8). A *learned* depth embedding adds $O(D)$ parameters and violates the constraint outright. Every impossibility-flavored statement in this record applies only on the strict side of this fork. The team must rule before any experiment is interpreted.

## 7. Constructive Directions Surfaced by Synthesis (all ≤ conjecture)

- **C1 — Preservation as a maintained invariant (rehabilitates dismissed prior art).** Once the protected object is recognized to be the *gate parameters* $\phi$ (not the frozen path $\theta$), orthogonal-gradient machinery becomes directly applicable *compatibly with all constraints*: project new-task gradients onto the orthogonal complement of the old-task gradient subspace in $\phi$-space, thereby holding $g_\phi|_{S_{\text{old}}}\approx \mathbf{1}$ to first order across sequential training. Classification: **plausible adaptation** of GEM/GPM to a new target object; no theorem claimed. Open statistical question: whether the invariant generalizes beyond the old-task sample.
- **C2 — Fixed-encoding $t$-conditioning** ($O(1)$ parameters): dissolves the depth-agnostic restrictions; legality contingent on the scope ruling (§5).
- **C3 — Delta-gate placement** for the residual BDH variant: gate $g(x)\odot\Delta(s_t)$ inside $s_{t+1}=s_t+\,\cdot$, never the residual stream itself.
- **C4 — Sparse-coordinate freedom:** exploit never-active coordinates of old-task trajectories (plausible under ReLU sparsity) as legal modification space even on preserved inputs.

1. **Instantiate $F$** from `paper.tex`: fix the literal update (residual form, Hebbian/multiplicative terms, normalization placement), choose gate placement (C3), and verify native plasticity is disabled. *(Closing searches issued above; see appended results — treat as corroboration only, not substitution for reading the TeX source.)*
2. **Rule on the $t$-conditioning fork** (§5) in writing.

5. **Measurement program:** empirical Lipschitz/expansiveness of the gated BDH block (tests V7's conjecture); empirical cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in $\phi$-space (tests whether V6's worst case binds in practice).

## 9. Discipline Audit

- No experimental observation was cited as evidence for any theorem at any point. ✔
- All overclaims identified by the CRITIC were either withdrawn on the record (capacity corollary; behavioral diagonalization; finite-$D$ "iff") or explicitly rescoped (global → asymmetric preservation). ✔

---

**Consensus summary:** complete agreement on the formal framework, on the two live interference channels, on the exact necessary conditions (V1–V3, V5–V7 as scoped), on prior-art triage, and on the open status of the asymmetric continual-learning question. The single substantive disagreement — whether a general impossibility was established — was resolved against the stronger claim on the strength-of-weakest-proof principle. The investigation exits the debate not with an impossibility theorem but with something more useful: a precise map of where gate freedom lives, four independent ways the program can fail, one pivotal scope decision ($t$-conditioning), and one decisive open conjecture (reachability on the instantiated BDH map) whose resolution will determine whether the mechanism is possible, impossible, or merely unproven.
