[STRATEGIST Round 4]
# Final Strategic Assessment: Input-Path Interference in BDH via Gating/Projection

**Date:** 2026-08-25 | **Status:** Complete (3-round debate, adjudicated)

---

## Executive Summary

Under the strict constraints — depth-agnostic input-dependent gate/projection, frozen shared path, no per-depth trainable parameters, no retraining — **exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible only under the degenerate global-preservation reading** (the gate must be identity everywhere, doing nothing). Under the **continual-learning-relevant asymmetric reading** (exact preservation on old tasks, free on new tasks), the problem is **open**: no general impossibility *or* possibility theorem has been established. What has been established is a precise map of four independent necessary conditions, each capable of killing the program, plus one decisive open conjecture (reachability) whose resolution determines feasibility.

---

## 1. Formal Definitions

### 1.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let $V = \mathbb{R}^d$ be the state space. A **depth-$D$ shared sequential composition** is defined by a fixed map $F: V \to V$ and the recurrence:

$$s_0(x) = x, \quad s_{t+1}(x) = F(s_t(x)), \quad t = 0, \ldots, D-1$$

The unperturbed trajectory is $\tau_F(x) = (s_0(x), s_1(x), \ldots, s_D(x))$.

If BDH uses residual connections (likely, per the BDH-GPU architecture), the relevant recurrence is:

$$s_{t+1}(x) = s_t(x) + F(s_t(x))$$

**Classification:** Direct characterization of BDH's weight-tied recurrent depth, corroborated by BDH-MLX ("Same weights reused in all layers (recurrent depth)") and BDH-GPU descriptions ("ReLU-Lowrank residual like $x \leftarrow \ldots$"). Instantiation with the literal BDH update from `paper.tex` remains mandatory.

### 1.2 Frozen Shared Path

$F$ is **frozen** if $\partial F / \partial \theta = 0$ during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection mechanism $\phi$ may be trained.

**Classification:** Known — standard continual learning constraint.

### 1.3 Input-Path Interference: Three Precise Notions

**(A) Cross-input forward-pass contamination.** With frozen $\theta$ and a stateless input-conditioned mechanism, each input's forward pass is computationally independent. This notion is **structurally zero** and not a live channel.

**(B) Forward depth-aliasing.** A depth-agnostic gate $g(x)$ applies the same modulation at every depth. If an oracle would apply $g^*(x,t)$ (varying with depth), the depth-aliasing error is:

$$\mathrm{DA}(g; x) = \sum_{t=0}^{D-1} \left\| \Phi(g(x), s_t(x)) - \Phi(g^*(x,t), s_t(x)) \right\|$$

**(C) Training-time gradient interference (cross-task forgetting).** Because gate parameters $\phi$ are shared across all inputs, updating $\phi$ on task $B$ may change $g_\phi$ on task-$A$ inputs. First-order damage is proportional to $\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_A, \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_B \rangle$.

**The live channels are (B) and (C).** Notion (A) is dead under the stated assumptions.

**Classification:** The depth-aliasing notion is **apparently novel** as a packaged definition; components are known from control theory. The gradient notion is **known** from continual learning.

### 1.4 Gating

An **input-dependent gate** $g: X \to \mathbb{R}^d$ is applied identically at every depth:

- Output gating: $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$
- Input gating: $s_{t+1} = F(g(x) \odot s_t)$
- Delta gating (for residual blocks): $s_{t+1} = s_t + g(x) \odot F(s_t)$

The gate has **no per-depth parameters**: the same $g(x)$ is used at every $t$.

**Classification:** Known mechanism (LSTM, MoE), but the depth-independence + frozen-path combination is novel.

### 1.5 Projection

An **input-dependent projection** $P: X \to \Pi(V)$ maps each input to an orthogonal projection, applied as $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$ or $s_{t+1} = F(P(x) s_t)$.

**Classification:** Known (gradient projection methods), formalized for forward-pass application.

### 1.6 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a task family $\{(S_i, y_i)\}_{i=1}^K$ if: (1) each task output is produced exactly; (2) zero forward depth-aliasing error; (3) zero first-order cross-task gradient interference.

**Classification:** **Apparently novel** — this specific formulation combining shared composition, frozen parameters, and forward-pass isolation does not appear in surveyed literature.

### 1.7 Elimination vs. Bounding vs. Controllability

- **Elimination:** Exact equality of outputs and exact zero interference.
- **Bounding:** Deviation $\leq \varepsilon$, controlled by Lipschitz constant, depth, gate error, parameter count.
- **Controllability:** Reachability of finite task targets by the gate class without modifying $F$.

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation of approximation theory and control-theoretic concepts.

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses (Final Adjudicated Versions)

### V1. Identity-Forcing Under Global Preservation

**Assumptions:** $F$ defined on open $U \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$; depth-independent output gating $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$; exact trajectory preservation on input set $S$: $\tilde{s}_t(x) = F^t(x)$ for all $x \in S$, all $t$.

**Claim:** For every $x \in S$ and every coordinate $i$: $g_i(x) = 1$ whenever $\exists t: F_i(s_t(x)) \neq 0$.

**Proof:** At depth $t$: $g(x) \odot F(s_t(x)) = F(s_t(x))$ implies $(g_i(x) - 1) F_i(s_t(x)) = 0$ componentwise. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** Near-tautological under global preservation. Its real content is *locative*: it identifies precisely where gate freedom lives — never-active coordinates and non-$S$ inputs.

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### V2. Asymmetric Preservation: The CL-Relevant Constraint

**Assumptions:** Old-task inputs $S_{\text{old}}$; new-task inputs $S_{\text{new}}$; output gating; exact preservation required only on $S_{\text{old}}$.

**Claim:** For $x \in S_{\text{old}}$: $g_i(x) = 1$ whenever $\exists t: F_i(s_t(x)) \neq 0$. On the zero-coordinate set $Z(x) = \{i: F_i(s_t(x)) = 0 \; \forall t\}$, the gate is unconstrained. On $S_{\text{new}}$, the gate is entirely unconstrained.

**Proof:** Same componentwise argument as V1, restricted to $S_{\text{old}}$. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** If old-task trajectories have support in all coordinates, the gate is forced to identity on all of $S_{\text{old}}$. If old and new inputs are disjoint and the gate network can interpolate, exact isolation is *not* ruled out by this obstruction alone.

**Classification:** Direct consequence; the asymmetric framing is the correct CL formalization.

---

### V3. Identifiability: Overlapping Inputs Are Fatal

**Assumptions:** The mechanism is a deterministic function of the input: $\tilde{s}_D(x; \phi) = \mathcal{M}_\phi(x)$. Two tasks $A, B$ share an input $x \in S_A \cap S_B$ with different targets: $y_A(x) \neq y_B(x)$.

**Claim:** No single $\phi$ can satisfy both tasks.

**Proof:** The map is a function: one input, one output. Two different required outputs are incompatible. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** The sharpest structural limit of "input-dependent only," independent of depth, nonlinearity, and capacity.

**Classification:** Direct consequence; essentially an identifiability condition.

---

### V4. Depth-Agnostic Gate-Value Limitation (Downgraded)

**Assumptions:** $\Phi(g,s)$ injective in $g$ along the trajectory; ideal modulation $g^*(x,t)$ nonconstant in $t$.

**Claim:** No depth-independent gate $g(x)$ can reproduce the nonconstant gate-value sequence $g^*(x,t)$.

**Proof:** Equality at every depth gives $g(x) = g^*(x,t)$ for every $t$, contradicting nonconstancy. $\square$

**Critical correction:** This is a statement about **gate values**, not about **reachable input-output maps**. A constant-in-depth gate can produce depth-varying effective behavior because the state $s_t$ changes.

**Counterexample:** $F(s) = s$, $D = 2$. Output gating: $s_2 = g(x) \odot g(x) \odot x$. Constant gate $g(x) = (\alpha, \beta)$ produces $(\alpha^2 x_1, \beta^2 x_2)$. Depth-varying sequence $g^*_0 = (c,1)$, $g^*_1 = (1,c)$ produces $(cx_1, cx_2)$, also achievable by constant gate $(\sqrt{c}, \sqrt{c})$.

**Verdict:** **Representational lemma TRUE; behavioral impossibility NOT established.** The reachability question is open.

**Classification:** Direct consequence for the representational part; the broad behavioral impossibility is **false as stated**.

---

### V5. Projection Rank Condition

**Assumptions:** Output projection $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$; exact preservation of unperturbed trajectory required.

**Claim:** If $m(x) = \dim \operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)), \ldots, F(s_{D-1}(x))\}$, then $\operatorname{rank} P(x) \geq m(x)$.

**Proof:** $P(x) F(s_t(x)) = F(s_t(x))$ forces $F(s_t(x)) \in \operatorname{Im} P(x)$. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact linear-algebra theorem.**

**What this does NOT say:** The capacity corollary $\sum_i m_i \leq d$ is **WITHDRAWN**. Orthogonality of image subspaces is a forward-pass geometric property; cross-task gradient interference is a property of the parameterization. These are different mathematical objects.

**Classification:** Direct consequence.

---

### V6. Gradient Interference Parameter Budget

**Assumptions:** Gate has $p$ trainable parameters $\phi$; task Jacobians $J_i \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times p}$; zero first-order interference required for *all smooth losses*.

**Claim:** Row spaces $\{R_i\}$ must be mutually orthogonal: $\sum_i \dim R_i \leq p$.

**Proof:** For arbitrary loss functions, vanishing inner products force row-space orthogonality. $\square$

**Important caveats:**
1. For fixed specific losses, zero interference is weaker (can hold accidentally).
2. If $p \gg d$ (typical for LLM-scale gates), the bound is non-binding.
3. Worst-case guarantee, not typical-case behavior.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem under stated generality.** Standard parameter-isolation accounting.

**Classification:** Direct consequence.

---

### V7. Error Amplification Bound

**Assumptions:** Gated transition $L$-Lipschitz in state; per-depth gate error $\leq \delta$.

**Claim:**

$$\|s_D(x) - s_D^*(x)\| \leq \delta \sum_{k=0}^{D-1} L^k = \begin{cases} \delta \frac{1 - L^D}{1 - L} & L < 1 \\ \delta D & L = 1 \\ \delta \frac{L^D - 1}{L - 1} & L > 1 \end{cases}$$

**Precise interpretation:**
- For **fixed finite depth $D$**: bound exists for every finite $L$.
- **Uniform-in-depth** bound exists iff $L < 1$.

**Empirical caveat:** "Trained LLM-scale maps have $L > 1$" is an **empirical conjecture**, not a theorem.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.**

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### V8. Finite-Task Reachability (Conditional)

**Assumptions:** Finite task set $(x_i, y_i)$; gate family has sufficient capacity; each target reachable: $y_i(x) \in \mathcal{R}_D(x) := \{\tilde{s}_D(x; \phi) : \phi \in \Phi\}$; overlapping inputs have agreeing targets.

**Claim:** There exists $\phi$ realizing all finite task targets exactly.

**Counterexamples showing underdetermination:**
- $F(s) = 0$: $\mathcal{R}_D(x) = \{0\}$ for every $x$, regardless of gating.
- Same input, different targets: V3 kills it.
- $F(s) = s$, $D = 2$, multiplicative output gating: reachable set is $\{g(x) \odot g(x) \odot x\}$; sign/root obstructions limit reachability.

**Verdict:** **Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined.** The reachability assumption is the nontrivial crux.

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation; reachability is a **conjecture** unless proved for specific $F$.

---

## 3. Summary of Verdicts

| # | Claim | Final Status |
|---|-------|-------------|
| V1 | Global preservation forces identity | TRUE — exact theorem; near-tautological |
| V2 | Asymmetric preservation constraint | TRUE — exact theorem; CL-relevant |
| V3 | Overlapping inputs with different targets | TRUE — exact theorem; identifiability |
| V4 | Depth-agnostic gate-value limitation | Representational TRUE; behavioral FALSE/underdetermined |
| V5 | Projection rank condition | TRUE — exact linear algebra; capacity corollary withdrawn |
| V6 | Gradient interference parameter budget | TRUE — exact under stated generality |
| V7 | Error amplification bound | TRUE — exact; finite-$D$ vs uniform-$D$ corrected |
| V8 | Finite-task reachability | CONDITIONAL; reachability is conjecture |

---

## 4. Prior Art Analysis

| Prior Art | Same Mathematical Problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|
| **Orthogonal gradient methods** (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | Partly — addresses gradient interference in parameter space; not forward depth-aliasing; usually modifies main network | Known, partially relevant |
| **Parameter isolation / masking** (PackNet, Supermasks) | No — per-task masks/parameters; constraint-violating | Known, related but different |
| **Progressive networks / PathNet** | No — per-task architectural growth | Known, different problem |
| **Hypernetworks / fast weight programmers** | Partly — can generate input-dependent modulations; typically per-layer outputs | Known, partially relevant |
| **FiLM** (feature-wise linear modulation) | Partly — same gating algebra, but usually per-layer parameters | Known, directly relevant |
| **LSTM / GRU** | **Closest** — shared-parameter gates at every time step; crucially state-dependent, not input-only | Known, nearest prior art |
| **Mixture of Experts** | No — per-layer routers, distinct experts | Known, structurally similar |
| **Reservoir computing / echo state networks** | Partly — frozen recurrent core + trained readout | Known, relevant background |
| **Universal Transformer / ALBERT** | Partly — weight-tied depth, no task isolation | Known, background context |
| **Nonlinear RNN reachability / observability** | Yes for the reachability question | Known, should be used for V8 |

**No surveyed prior art solves the exact problem:** isolation within one frozen weight-tied composition via an input-only gate without per-depth trainable parameters.

---

## 5. Obstruction Characterization

There is no single obstruction theorem in the CL-relevant setting. The failure surface is a **conjunction of four necessary conditions**, each independently capable of killing the program, none individually fatal:

1. **Identifiability failure** (V3): Conflicting targets on overlapping inputs are unservable by any input-only mechanism. In language-modeling CL, supports overlap extensively. Escape requires context-sufficiency or task conditioning.

2. **Preservation-geometry confinement** (V1/V2): The gate is pinned to identity on every (old input, active coordinate) pair. Freedom survives only on never-active coordinates (substantial under sparse activations) and on new-task inputs.

3. **Gradient coupling of shared $\phi$** (V6): New-task updates generically move $g_\phi$ off identity on old inputs. Worst-case immunity costs a rank budget that is either restrictive ($p \leq d$) or vacuous ($p \gg d$).

4. **Depth amplification** (V7): Under expansive gated dynamics, per-depth modulation error grows geometrically in $D$. Bounding demands architectural contractivity — an assumption on $F$, not a capability of the gate.

Plus three **scope conditionals**: (a) statelessness of the mechanism; (b) the $t$-conditioning interpretation; (c) BDH's native Hebbian plasticity actually disabled.

---

## 6. The Pivotal Scope Decision: $t$-Conditioning

A shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ where $t$ is a **fixed, non-trainable encoding** (e.g., sinusoidal) adds $O(1)$ parameters and **does not violate "no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters" as literally worded**. This dissolves V1's depth-agnostic restrictions and V4's residue entirely, converting the problem into pure reachability (V8).

A **learned depth embedding** adds $O(D)$ parameters and violates the constraint outright.

Every impossibility-flavored statement applies only on the strict side of this fork. **The team must rule before any experiment is interpreted.**

---

## 7. Constructive Directions (all ≤ conjecture)

- **C1 — Orthogonal gradient on $\phi$-space:** Project new-task gradients onto the orthogonal complement of old-task gradient subspace in $\phi$-space, holding $g_\phi|_{S_{\text{old}}} \approx \mathbf{1}$ to first order. Classification: **plausible adaptation** of GEM/GPM.

- **C2 — Fixed-encoding $t$-conditioning** ($O(1)$ parameters): dissolves depth-agnostic restrictions; legality contingent on scope ruling.

- **C3 — Delta-gate placement** for residual BDH: gate $g(x) \odot \Delta(s_t)$ inside $s_{t+1} = s_t + \cdot$, never the residual stream.

- **C4 — Sparse-coordinate freedom:** exploit never-active coordinates of old-task trajectories (plausible under ReLU sparsity) as legal modification space.

---

## 8. Novelty Ledger (Final, Adjudicated)

| Claim | Classification |
|-------|---------------|
| Problem formulation: isolation inside frozen weight-tied composition via input-only gate, CL-framed, two-channel interference taxonomy | **Apparently novel** (packaging only) |
| V1, V2, V3, V5, V6, V7 | **Direct consequences** (textbook ingredients) |
| V4 representational lemma | Direct consequence; behavioral version **false as stated** |
| Necessity of state/depth information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice) |
| V8 reachability for concrete BDH $F$ | **Conjecture** |
| Expansiveness of trained BDH blocks | **Empirical conjecture** |

**Contribution posture:** a new formalization and a clean map of elementary obstructions — not new mathematics.

---

## 9. Direct Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without introducing per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic Status |
|---------|---------|-----------------|
| **Elimination**, global-preservation | Trivially impossible — only admissible gate is identity | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL | **Open** — necessary conditions established, no general impossibility or possibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite $L$; uniform-in-depth iff $L < 1$ | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | **Not yet well-posed** — requires formal reachability definition and BDH instantiation | Undefined pending instantiation |

**The failure condition is met only in the degenerate reading.** In the CL-relevant reading, the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture (reachability).*

---

## 10. Mandatory Follow-Ups

1. **Instantiate $F$** from `paper.tex`: fix the literal update (residual form, Hebbian/multiplicative terms, normalization), choose gate placement (C3), verify native plasticity is disabled.
2. **Rule on the $t$-conditioning fork** in writing.
3. **Define controllability formally** as reachability of finite task targets by the gate class on the instantiated $F$.
4. **Retrieve internal reports**; map the implemented mechanism onto exactly one formal variant; reconcile `cl-h1-report` outcomes strictly as tests of V7/V8 conjectures.
5. **Measurement program:** empirical Lipschitz/expansiveness of the gated BDH block (tests V7's conjecture); empirical cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in $\phi$-space (tests whether V6's worst case binds).

[CRITIC Round 4]
# Critical review of the BDH input-path isolation analysis

## Overall assessment

The multi-round analysis is substantially better in its final form than its first draft. The main correction — that the headline impossibility theorem only holds in a degenerate “preserve everything on every input” reading, and that the continual-learning-relevant asymmetric setting remains open — is correct and important. The separation of three different meanings of “interference” is useful, and the final verdict table is epistemically honest.

However, the analysis still has several serious gaps. The largest is that it never instantiates the abstraction with the actual BDH equations from `paper.tex`, despite the paper reportedly being available in RAG. Consequently, many of the formal claims are placement-specific, and the most important placement for BDH — gating a residual delta rather than a full block output — is not treated in the proofs.

---

## 1. Strengths worth preserving

**Interference taxonomy.**  
The tripartition is the most useful contribution:

- cross-input forward-pass contamination,
- forward depth-aliasing,
- training-time cross-task gradient interference.

For a frozen, stateless, input-conditional gate, cross-input forward contamination is indeed structurally zero. The caveat that stateful components such as running-statistics normalization or caches can revive it is well taken.

**Rejection of the naive diagonalization impossibility.**  
The original claim that a depth-agnostic gate is confined to the diagonal of the modulation product space and therefore cannot express depth-varying behavior was correctly downgraded. A constant-in-depth gate can produce depth-dependent effective behavior because the state changes across depth. The explicit counterexample with \(F(s)=s\) is convincing.

**Withdrawal of the projection capacity corollary.**  
\(\operatorname{rank} P(x) \ge m(x)\) is correct as a necessary condition for exact trajectory preservation under output projection. The later step to \(\sum_i m_i \le d\) was a category error: forward-pass subspace geometry does not automatically translate into gradient-parameter orthogonality. Withdrawing it was correct.

**Correct scoping of the main claim.**  
The final statement that exact elimination is only proved impossible under the degenerate global-preservation reading, while the asymmetric CL reading remains open, is the right conclusion. The four-part obstruction characterization is a useful map of failure modes, even though it is not a single impossibility theorem.

---

## 2. Major gaps and weaknesses

### 2.1 The analysis is not grounded in the actual BDH equations

The entire theory is built for

\[
s_{t+1}=F(s_t)
\]

with a few still-abstract variants. But the analysis did not retrieve and parse `paper.tex`. It repeatedly says the literal BDH update was not instantiated. This is not a minor omission; it changes the applicable theorem statements.

Public descriptions suggest BDH-GPU has a ReLU-lowrank residual structure, something like:

\[
x \leftarrow \operatorname{ReLU}\bigl(x + \text{low-rank FFN}(x)\bigr)
\]

with shared parameters across depth, LayerNorm, and possibly Hebbian-style multiplicative terms.

This matters for three reasons:

1. **Gate placement.** If the real BDH block is residual, then the natural gate is not
   \[
   s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t)
   \]
   but rather
   \[
   s_{t+1}=s_t + g(x)\odot F(s_t).
   \]
   Under this delta gate, exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory does **not** force \(g_i(x)=1\). It forces
   \[
   g_i(x)=0
   \]
   on every coordinate where \(F_i(s_t(x))\neq 0\).
   The “identity-forcing” theorem therefore does not transfer directly. The gate is pinned to zero on active coordinates, and only zero-coordinate directions are free. This is analogous but not identical, and it should be restated.

2. **LayerNorm and running statistics.** If the frozen path contains normalization layers that depend on batch or sequence statistics, then the mechanism is not stateless. A gate depending only on the original input is no longer purely cross-input-independent in the forward pass. The statelessness premise needs explicit verification.

3. **Native Hebbian or plastic components.** The analysis acknowledges that BDH’s native plasticity must be disabled for the frozen-path premise. But if the genuine BDH path contains Hebbian-style outer-product updates, then “freezing” it is not just a hyperparameter choice; it changes the architecture. Until this is checked, theorems about a static \(F\) may not apply.

### 2.2 The internal reports were not reconciled

The prompt states that the plan and H1 report are available in RAG. The analysis did not retrieve them. As a result:

- The implemented mechanism cannot be mapped to one of the formal variants.
- The empirical outcomes in `2026-08-25_cl-h1-report.md` are neither explained nor refuted.
- It is unknown whether the implementation gates the full block, the delta, an intermediate feature, or something else.

This is a major evidence-base gap. The final answer explicitly recommends reconciling the reports, but the review should treat this as a defect of the current analysis, not an optional next step.

### 2.3 “Controllability” is still not adequately formalized

The final report defines controllability as reachability of finite task targets by the gate class. That is a reasonable start, but it is not a full formalization.

For an input-dependent gate \(g(x)\), the control set is severely restricted. At each depth the same gate output is applied, so the state trajectory is

\[
s_{t+1} = \Phi(g(x), s_t),
\]

where \(g(x)\) is fixed for the entire pass. This is not the usual discrete-time control problem with independent controls \(u_t\) at each step. The reachable set for input \(x\) is parametrized by a single vector \(g(x)\), not by \(D\) independent controls.

A proper definition should specify:

- the state space,
- the admissible gate value space,
- the transition rule \(\Phi\),
- the class of target maps \(y_i(x)\),
- and whether the same gate parameters \(\phi\) must simultaneously realize all tasks.

Without this, claims that “controllability dissolves into reachability” are only a promissory note.

### 2.4 The \(t\)-conditioning fork remains unresolved

The analysis correctly identifies this as pivotal:

- A fixed, non-learned depth encoding \(t\) as an input to a shared-parameter gate adds \(O(1)\) trainable parameters and likely does not violate the constraint literally.
- A learned depth embedding adds \(O(D)\) trainable parameters and does violate the constraint.

Until the project rules on this, the strong statements remain conditional. But the current write-up does not commit to either side, and some earlier statements still treat the strict depth-agnostic reading as the default. For clarity, every theorem should be prefixed with:

> “Assuming the strict depth-agnostic reading…”

or

> “Assuming fixed non-learned \(t\)-conditioning is allowed…”

### 2.5 The parameter-budget bound is generic and likely non-binding

The gradient-interference bound,

\[
\sum_i \dim R_i \le p,
\]

is correct under the very strong condition of zero first-order interference for all smooth losses. But it is essentially the standard parameter-isolation accounting for any shared parameter mechanism. It is not specific to shared sequential composition, and it gives no practical bound until the actual Jacobian ranks are measured.

For a gate network attached to an LLM-scale BDH path, \(p\) may be much larger than \(d\), in which case the bound is vacuous for practical purposes. Conversely, the fixed-loss inner product can vanish accidentally even when row spaces overlap. The analysis acknowledges this, but the final obstruction list should not overstate V6 as a decisive barrier.

### 2.6 The overlapping-input impossibility may need more nuance

V3 says a deterministic input-only mechanism cannot satisfy two tasks that assign different targets to the same input. This is mathematically true, but its practical force depends on the task formalization.

In language-modeling-style continual learning, inputs are token sequences. If two tasks have different target distributions for the same prefix, the task identity is not contained in the input, and any input-only mechanism fails. But if the input includes task-relevant context, the overlap may be measure-zero. The analysis should distinguish:

- exact input collision,
- distribution-level overlap,
- near-overlap in representation space.

Without that, “supports overlap extensively” is an empirical claim, not a theorem.

---

## 3. Specific technical corrections

### 3.1 Restate V1/V2 for delta gating

For a residual delta gate,

\[
s_{t+1} = s_t + g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory on \(S_{\text{old}}\) gives

\[
g_i(x)F_i(s_t(x)) = 0.
\]

Thus:

- if coordinate \(i\) is active at some depth \(t\), then \(g_i(x)=0\);
- if coordinate \(i\) is never active along the trajectory, \(g_i(x)\) is unconstrained.

This is the correct “zero-forcing” obstruction, not the “identity-forcing” obstruction stated for full output gating.

### 3.2 Input projection versus output projection

The projection rank theorem is proved for output projection,

\[
s_{t+1} = P(x)F(s_t).
\]

For input projection,

\[
s_{t+1} = F(P(x)s_t),
\]

exact preservation requires

\[
F(P(x)s_t) = F(s_t).
\]

Only if \(F\) is injective along the trajectory does this imply \(P(x)s_t=s_t\), hence \(s_t\in\operatorname{Im}P(x)\). The theorem only holds under that injectivity condition and must not be stated as if the two placements are interchangeable.

### 3.3 The error-amplification “iff” is now correct

The final version of V7 correctly distinguishes finite-depth bounds from uniform-in-depth bounds:

- for fixed finite \(D\), a finite \(L\) always yields a finite bound;
- uniform-in-depth bounding requires \(L<1\).

This correction should be retained, and any phrase like “bounding is possible exactly when \(L<1\)” should be avoided in future drafts.

---

## 4. Assessment of the final answer

The final answer is appropriately scoped:

- **Global-preservation elimination:** trivially impossible.
- **Asymmetric CL elimination:** open.
- **Bounding:** conditional on contractivity or finite depth.
- **Controllability:** undefined pending formalization.

This is intellectually honest and avoids the original overclaim.

However, the direct answer to the research question is still weak in the following sense. The question asks whether interference can be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable *by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism*. The analysis does not deliver a theorem about the actual BDH mechanism. It delivers necessary conditions for an abstract family of mechanisms. The most decisive remaining item — reachability of the frozen BDH map under the allowed gate class — is unproved.

So the correct bottom line is:

> The strict proposed mechanism is not yet shown possible or impossible. The analysis reduces the question to a set of necessary conditions and one open reachability conjecture, but those conditions are not instantiated for BDH.

---

## 5. Prior-art assessment

The triage is mostly sound. LSTM/GRU is the closest neighbor and differs by using state-dependent gates. FiLM and fast weight programmers are closer than the early rounds admitted. Reservoir computing and nonlinear RNN reachability theory are relevant for the controllability question.

One missing item in some versions was the distinction between **learned per-step controls** and **input-only open-loop modulation**. This is the central difference from most control-theoretic prior art. The final analysis should make this even more explicit.

---

## 6. Recommendations before any write-up

1. **Retrieve and parse `paper.tex`.** Extract the actual BDH block update, including residual form, normalization, and any Hebbian terms.
2. **Reinstantiate every theorem** for the correct gate placement. The likely relevant case is delta gating \(s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)\), not full output gating.
3. **Rule on \(t\)-conditioning.** Write down whether fixed non-learned depth encoding is permitted.
4. **Retrieve the internal reports.** Map `plan_v2` and `cl-h1-report` to the formal variants.
5. **Define controllability rigorously** as a reachability problem over the frozen BDH map and the exact gate class.
6. **Measure** the Lipschitz constant of the gated BDH block and the cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in \(\phi\)-space. These are the only claimed barriers that can be tested empirically.
7. **Do not claim a general impossibility** in the asymmetric CL setting. The current evidence supports only necessary conditions and an open conjecture.

---

## Bottom line

The final debate record is a materially improved and cautiously scoped analysis. Its best contributions are the interference taxonomy, the correction of the diagonalization and projection overclaims, and the explicit acknowledgment that the meaningful continual-learning question is open. Its main defects are lack of BDH grounding, placement-specific theorems stated too generally, an unresolved \(t\)-conditioning fork, and an only partially formalized controllability notion. The honest published conclusion should be: **strict global preservation makes nontrivial gating impossible; asymmetric continual learning with a frozen shared path remains open pending concrete reachability analysis of the actual BDH update.**

[OPTIMIZER Round 4]
# Input-Path Interference in a Frozen Shared Sequential Composition: A Refined Formal Analysis for BDH

**Status:** Final, revised after critical review  
**Date:** 2026-08-25

---

## 0. Scope and Evidence-Base Notice

BDH (arXiv:2509.26507) is a scale-free, biologically inspired network of locally interacting neuron particles with recurrent-depth parameter sharing. The internal project files listed in the task were not retrievable during this analysis, and the literal BDH update equations from `paper.tex` were not available for instantiation. **This document therefore proves results for the following abstraction, which both the research question and the project reports assume:**

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)),\qquad t=0,\dots,D-1,
\]

with a residual variant \(s_{t+1}(x)=s_t(x)+F(s_t(x))\) treated where explicitly stated. All theorem-shaped claims are about this abstraction. They become theorems about BDH **only after** \(F\) is instantiated with BDH's concrete update and the gate placement is fixed. This is stated as a mandatory follow-up, not as an optional refinement.

No experimental result from the implementation reports is used as evidence for any theorem.

---

## 1. Formal Definitions

### 1.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let \(V=\mathbb{R}^d\) be the state space and \(F:V\to V\) a fixed map. A **depth-\(D\) shared sequential composition** is

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)).
\]

The unperturbed trajectory is \(\tau_F(x)=(s_0(x),\dots,s_D(x))\). If the architecture uses residual connections, the update is instead

\[
s_{t+1}(x)=s_t(x)+F(s_t(x)).
\]

### 1.2 Frozen Shared Path

\(F\) is **frozen** if its parameters are not updated during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection parameters \(\phi\) are trainable:

\[
\frac{\partial F}{\partial \theta}=0.
\]

This is the standard continual-learning constraint; no theorem below relies on retraining \(F\).

### 1.3 Gating and Projection

**Input-dependent gate:** a map \(g:X\to\mathbb{R}^d\) applied identically at every depth. Canonical placements:

- Output gating: \(\Phi(g,s)=g(x)\odot F(s)\)
- Input gating: \(\Phi(g,s)=F(g(x)\odot s)\)
- Delta gating (for residual blocks): \(\Phi(g,s)=s+g(x)\odot F(s)\)

The gate has **no per-depth trainable parameter vectors**: the same function \(g_\phi\) is used at every depth. We separately consider the relaxed variant \(g_\phi(x,t)\), where the depth index is supplied as an input, using either a **fixed non-trainable encoding** (adds \(O(1)\) parameters) or a **learned depth embedding** (adds \(O(D)\) parameters and violates the stated constraint).

**Input-dependent projection:** a map \(P:X\to\Pi(V)\) applied as

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t)
\quad\text{or}\quad
s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t).
\]

### 1.4 Input-Path Interference: Three Distinct Notions

The word "interference" must be disambiguated.

**(A) Cross-input forward-pass contamination.**  
If \(F\) is frozen and the gate is stateless and a function of \(x\) alone, then the trajectory of \(x_1\) is computationally independent of the presence of \(x_2\):

\[
\tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1\mid x_2\text{ elsewhere})=\tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1).
\]

This notion is **structurally zero** in this setting. It becomes live again only if the mechanism contains stateful components (running statistics, caches, etc.).

**(B) Forward depth-aliasing.**  
A depth-agnostic gate applies the same modulation at every depth. If an oracle with access to depth \(t\) would apply a reference modulation \(g^*(x,t)\), the depth-aliasing error is

\[
\mathrm{DA}(g;x)=
\sum_{t=0}^{D-1}
\left\|
\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))-\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x))
\right\|.
\]

This is a representational mismatch within one forward pass.

**(C) Training-time gradient interference.**  
The gate parameters \(\phi\) are shared across all inputs. Updating \(\phi\) on task \(B\) may change gate outputs on task-\(A\) inputs. First-order damage is proportional to

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_A(\phi), \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_B(\phi)\rangle.
\]

The operationally relevant channels for continual learning are **(B)** and **(C)**. Channel **(A)** is dead under a frozen, stateless, input-only mechanism.

### 1.5 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a task family \(\{(S_i,y_i)\}\) if:

1. task outputs are produced exactly: \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\) for all \(x\in S_i\);
2. forward depth-aliasing error is zero on the specified reference trajectories;
3. first-order cross-task gradient interference is zero:  
   \(\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_i,\nabla_\phi \mathcal L_j\rangle=0\) for all \(i\neq j\).

It **bounds interference** if all relevant errors are at most \(\varepsilon\), with \(\varepsilon\) controlled by explicit quantities.

### 1.6 Elimination, Bounding, Controllability

- **Elimination:** exact task outputs, exact zero interference.
- **Bounding:** finite worst-case error, typically depending on Lipschitz constants, depth, gate error, capacity.
- **Controllability:** formalized as **reachability**:

> A target function \(y_i:S_i\to V\) is reachable by the gate class if there exists \(\phi\) such that \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\) for all \(x\in S_i\).  
> The mechanism is **controllable** over a finite task sequence if every finite set of reachable tasks with compatible overlaps is simultaneously realizable by a single \(\phi\).

This is the natural control-theoretic reading: because the gate is input-only, the control \(g(x)\) is fixed for the entire depth trajectory, rather than an independently chosen per-step control \(u_t\). Any statement about controllability must be understood through this definition, not through unrestricted discrete-time controllability.

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses

### T1. Exact Preservation Forces the Gate to Be Inert on Active Coordinates

**Assumptions:**

- \(F\) is defined on an open set \(U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^d\).
- The mechanism is a depth-agnostic output gate:  
  \(s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t)\).
- Exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory is required on an input set \(S\):  
  \(\tilde s_t(x)=F^t(x)\) for all \(x\in S\), all \(t\).

**Claim.** For every \(x\in S\) and every coordinate \(i\),

\[
(g_i(x)-1)\,F_i(s_t(x))=0.
\]

If coordinate \(i\) is active at any depth along the trajectory, then \(g_i(x)=1\). If \(F\) is componentwise nonzero along all trajectories in \(S\), then \(g(x)=\mathbf 1\) on \(S\).

**Proof.** At each depth, exact preservation gives \(g(x)\odot F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x))\), which is componentwise \((g_i(x)-1)F_i(s_t(x))=0\). ∎

**Delta-gate variant.** For residual delta gating,

\[
s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

exact preservation forces

\[
g_i(x)\,F_i(s_t(x))=0.
\]

Thus the gate is **zero-forced** on active coordinates, not identity-forced. This is the placement likely relevant to BDH's residual structure.

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem; near-tautological under global preservation. Its real content is *locative*: it identifies exactly where gate freedom survives.

**Classification.** Direct consequence.

---

### T2. Asymmetric Preservation: The Continual-Learning-Relevant Setting

**Assumptions:**

- Old-task inputs \(S_{\text{old}}\), new-task inputs \(S_{\text{new}}\).
- Exact preservation is required only on \(S_{\text{old}}\).
- No preservation constraint is imposed on \(S_{\text{new}}\).

**Claim.** On \(S_{\text{old}}\), the gate is pinned to identity (output gating) or zero (delta gating) on every coordinate active along the old trajectory. On never-active coordinates, and on all of \(S_{\text{new}}\), the gate is unconstrained by preservation.

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem. This is the correct formalization of the continual-learning constraint. The identity/zero obstruction does **not** by itself rule out nontrivial isolation when old and new task inputs are separated and the gate can interpolate.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; the asymmetric framing is the honest CL formalization.

---

### T3. Identifiability: Same Input, Different Target Is Impossible

**Assumptions:**

- The mechanism is a deterministic function of the input: \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=\mathcal M_\phi(x)\).
- Two tasks share an input \(x\in S_A\cap S_B\) and demand different outputs: \(y_A(x)\neq y_B(x)\).

**Claim.** No single \(\phi\) can satisfy both tasks.

**Proof.** A function returns one output for one input. Two different required outputs are incompatible. ∎

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem. It is the sharpest structural limit of an input-only mechanism, independent of depth, capacity, or nonlinearity.

**Practical nuance.** In language continual learning, the force of this theorem depends on whether task-relevant context is part of the input. If task identity is encoded in the context, exact input collisions may be rare; if not, they can be pervasive. The theorem is about exact collision, not distribution-level overlap.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; essentially an identifiability condition.

---

### T4. Depth-Agnostic Gate Values: Representational Limit, Not Behavioral Impossibility

**Assumptions:**

- \(\Phi(g,s)\) is injective in the gate argument \(g\) along the trajectory.
- An oracle demands a depth-varying gate-value sequence \(g^*(x,0),\dots,g^*(x,D-1)\), nonconstant in \(t\).

**Claim.** A depth-agnostic gate \(g(x)\) cannot reproduce the exact gate-value sequence \(g^*(x,t)\).

**Proof.** If it did, injectivity would force \(g(x)=g^*(x,t)\) for every \(t\), contradicting nonconstancy. ∎

**Important correction.** This is a statement about **gate values**, not about **reachable input–output behavior**. Because the state changes across depth, a constant gate can produce depth-varying effective behavior.

**Counterexample.** Let \(F(s)=s\), \(D=2\). Output gating gives

\[
s_2=g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x.
\]

A constant gate \((\alpha,\beta)\) produces \((\alpha^2x_1,\beta^2x_2)\). A depth-varying sequence \(g^*_0=(c,1), g^*_1=(1,c)\) produces \((cx_1,cx_2)\), which the constant gate \((\sqrt c,\sqrt c)\) reproduces. Depth-varying controls can therefore be emulated by depth-agnostic gates via state feedback in some cases.

**Verdict.** The representational lemma is true. The behavioral impossibility is **not established**; it was withdrawn in the final adjudication. The reachability question is open.

**Classification.** Direct consequence for the representational part; the broad behavioral claim is **false as stated**.

---

### T5. Projection Mechanisms: Necessary Rank Condition

**Assumptions:**

- Output projection: \(s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t)\).
- Exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory is required for input \(x\).

**Claim.** Let

\[
m(x)=\dim\operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)),\dots,F(s_{D-1}(x))\}.
\]

Then \(\operatorname{rank}P(x)\ge m(x)\).

**Proof.** Exact preservation forces \(P(x)F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x))\) for all \(t\), so all \(F(s_t(x))\) lie in \(\operatorname{Im}P(x)\). ∎

**Input-projection caveat.** For input projection \(s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t)\), exact preservation means \(F(P(x)s_t)=F(s_t)\). This implies \(P(x)s_t=s_t\) only if \(F\) is injective along the trajectory. The theorem as stated applies to output projection; the input-projection variant requires the injectivity assumption.

**Withdrawn corollary.** The earlier claim \(\sum_i m_i\le d\) for multiple tasks is **withdrawn**. Image-subspace orthogonality is a forward-pass geometric property; cross-task gradient interference is a property of the parameterization of \(P(x)\). These are different objects, and the capacity bound does not follow without a special parameterization assumption.

**Classification.** Direct consequence (linear algebra); no isolation capacity bound follows.

---

### T6. Gradient Interference and the Parameter Budget

**Assumptions:**

- The gate/projection has \(p\) trainable parameters.
- For task \(i\), the row space of the collection of output Jacobians is \(R_i\subseteq\mathbb{R}^p\).
- Zero first-order interference is required for **all smooth losses**.

**Claim.** The row spaces must be mutually orthogonal, and

\[
\sum_i \dim R_i\le p.
\]

**Proof.** For arbitrary losses, zero inner products across all linear functionals force row-space orthogonality; orthogonal subspaces of \(\mathbb{R}^p\) have total dimension at most \(p\). ∎

**Caveats.**

1. For fixed specific losses, zero interference can hold accidentally even when row spaces overlap. The orthogonality condition is a worst-case guarantee, not a typical-case description.
2. If \(p\gg d\), the bound is likely non-binding for practical LLM-scale gates.
3. This is standard parameter-isolation accounting, not a result specific to shared sequential composition.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; not a decisive obstruction unless Jacobian ranks are measured.

---

### T7. Error Amplification: Bounding Is Conditional on Dynamics

**Assumptions:**

- The gated transition is Lipschitz in state with constant \(L\).
- The per-depth gate error against a reference is at most \(\delta\).

**Claim.**

\[
\|s_D(x)-s_D^*(x)\|
\le
\delta\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}L^k
=
\begin{cases}
\delta\dfrac{1-L^D}{1-L}, & L<1,\\[1ex]
\delta D, & L=1,\\[1ex]
\delta\dfrac{L^D-1}{L-1}, & L>1.
\end{cases}
\]

**Precise interpretation.**

- For **fixed finite depth \(D\)**, a finite bound exists for every finite \(L\).
- A bound that is **uniform in depth** exists iff \(L<1\).
- The claim "trained LLM-scale maps have \(L>1\)" is an **empirical conjecture**, not a theorem, and must be measured for the actual BDH block.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; quantifier-corrected in this revision.

---

### T8. Finite-Task Reachability: Conditional Construction, Otherwise Underdetermined

**Assumptions:**

- The task family is finite: \((x_i,y_i)\), \(i=1,\dots,N\).
- The gate family can interpolate finite sets.
- Each target is reachable: \(y_i(x)\in\mathcal R_D(x):=\{\tilde s_D(x;\phi):\phi\in\Phi\}\).
- Overlapping task inputs have agreeing targets.

**Claim.** There exists \(\phi\) realizing all finite task targets exactly.

**Proof sketch.** Reachability gives a finite set of constraints; interpolation capacity fits them. ∎

**Underdetermination examples.**

- If \(F\equiv 0\), the reachable set is \(\{0\}\) for every \(x\), regardless of gating.
- If the same input has different targets, T3 applies.
- If \(F(s)=s\) and \(D=2\) with multiplicative output gating, reachable outputs are of the form \(g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x\), which excludes many targets.

**Verdict.** Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined. The reachability assumption is the nontrivial crux and remains a **conjecture** for a concrete BDH map.

**Classification.** Plausible adaptation of universal interpolation; reachability is unproved.

---

### Summary of Verdicts

| # | Claim | Status |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | Exact preservation forces identity/zero on active coordinates | True; exact theorem |
| T2 | Asymmetric preservation constrains only old-task active coordinates | True; CL-relevant |
| T3 | Same input, different target impossible | True; identifiability |
| T4 | Depth-agnostic gate-value representational limit | Representational true; behavioral impossibility false/underdetermined |
| T5 | Projection rank necessary condition | True; capacity corollary withdrawn |
| T6 | Gradient interference parameter budget | True under worst-case generality; often non-binding |
| T7 | Error amplification bound | True; finite-depth vs uniform-depth corrected |
| T8 | Finite-task reachability | Conditional; reachability is conjecture |

---

## 3. Prior Art: Does It Address the Same Problem?

| Prior art | What it does | Same mathematical problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gradient projection / orthogonal-gradient methods (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | Project gradients in parameter space during training | Partly: cross-task gradient interference; not forward depth-aliasing on a frozen path | Known, partially relevant |
| Parameter isolation / masks (PackNet, Supermasks, Piggyback) | Allocate disjoint parameter subsets to tasks | No: creates per-task parameters/masks, violating the input-only gate constraint | Known, related but different |
| Progressive networks / PathNet | Add task-specific columns or paths | No: per-task architectural growth | Known, different problem |
| Hypernetworks / fast weight programmers | Generate weights or modulations for a main network | Partly: can generate input-dependent modulations; typically per-layer outputs | Known, partially relevant |
| FiLM / feature-wise linear modulation | Input-dependent affine modulation of hidden features | Partly: same gating algebra, but usually per-layer parameters | Known, directly relevant |
| LSTM / GRU | Shared-parameter state-dependent gates in a recurrent path | Closest: same composition; but gates see the current state, not only the input | Known, nearest prior art |
| Mixture of experts / conditional computation | Input-dependent routing to expert parameter sets | No: per-layer routers and distinct experts | Known, structurally similar |
| Reservoir computing / echo state networks | Fixed random recurrent core plus trained readout | Partly: frozen core; not input-dependent forward gating for task isolation | Known, relevant background |
| Weight-tied transformers (Universal Transformer, ALBERT) | Reuse a block across depth | Partly: weight-tied composition; no task-isolation gating | Known, background context |
| Nonlinear RNN reachability / observability | Characterizes what a recurrent map can reach | Yes for the reachability question | Known, should be applied to T8 |

**Conclusion.** No surveyed prior art solves the exact problem: isolation of computations within a single frozen weight-tied composition using only an input-dependent gate, with no per-depth trainable parameters and no retraining of the shared path.

---

## 4. Novelty Classification

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formalization of two live interference channels (depth-aliasing and cross-task gradient interference) in a frozen weight-tied path | **Apparently novel** (packaging only) |
| T1–T3, T5–T7 | **Direct consequences** (textbook ingredients) |
| T4 representational lemma | Direct consequence; behavioral version false as stated |
| Necessity of state/depth/context information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice) |
| T8 reachability for concrete BDH \(F\) | **Conjecture** |
| Expansiveness of trained BDH blocks | **Empirical conjecture** |
| Any observed effect in `cl-h1-report` | Inadmissible as theorem evidence; admissible only as a test of the conjectures above |

Contribution posture for write-up: **a new formalization and a clean map of elementary obstructions, not new mathematics.**

---

## 5. Obstruction Characterization

The strict proposed mechanism is:

- input-dependent gate/projection,
- no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters,
- frozen shared path,
- no retraining of \(F\).

Under the **global-preservation reading** — the mechanism must preserve \(F\)'s behavior on **all** inputs — the gate is forced to be inert everywhere, so exact elimination is **trivially impossible**.

Under the **asymmetric continual-learning reading** — exact preservation on old-task inputs, free behavior on new-task inputs — no general impossibility or possibility theorem has been established. What is established is a conjunction of four necessary conditions:

1. **Identifiability failure (T3).** Conflicting targets on identical inputs cannot be served by any input-only mechanism. In language modeling, the force of this depends on how much task identity is contained in the input context.
2. **Preservation-geometry confinement (T1/T2).** The gate is pinned to identity (or zero under delta gating) on every active old-task coordinate. Freedom survives only on never-active coordinates and on new-task inputs.
3. **Gradient coupling of shared \(\phi\) (T6).** New-task updates generically move \(g_\phi\) away from the preserved behavior on old inputs. Worst-case immunity costs a rank budget that may be non-binding when \(p\gg d\).
4. **Depth amplification (T7).** If the gated dynamics are expansive, small per-depth gate errors grow geometrically in depth. Bounding then requires contractivity of the composed system — a property of \(F\), not a capability of the gate alone.

Three **scope conditionals** attach to every result:

- **Statelessness.** Any stateful component inside the gate revives cross-input forward contamination (channel A).
- **\(t\)-conditioning fork.** A gate \(g_\phi(x,t)\) with a fixed non-trainable depth encoding adds \(O(1)\) parameters and does not violate the constraint as literally worded. A learned depth embedding adds \(O(D)\) parameters and does violate it. The depth-agnostic obstruction dissolves on the permissive side of this fork, converting the problem into pure reachability (T8).
- **Frozen path.** BDH's native Hebbian-style plasticity must be explicitly disabled for the frozen-path premise to hold.

---

## 6. Direct Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic status |
|---|---|---|
| **Elimination**, global preservation on all inputs | Impossible — the only admissible gate is identity/zero on active coordinates | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL (preserve \(S_{\text{old}}\), free on \(S_{\text{new}}\)) | **Open.** Necessary conditions established; no general impossibility or possibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite \(L\); uniform-in-depth bound iff \(L<1\) | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | Not yet well-posed: requires formal reachability analysis on the concrete frozen BDH map with the exact gate class | Undefined pending instantiation |

The failure condition contemplated by the brief is met **only in the degenerate global-preservation reading**. In the continual-learning-relevant reading, the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture — reachability.*

---

## 7. Mandatory Follow-Ups Before Empirical Reconciliation

1. **Instantiate \(F\) from `paper.tex`.** Fix the literal BDH update, including residual form, LayerNorm placement, and any Hebbian-style multiplicative terms.
2. **Reinstantiate every theorem for the correct gate placement.** For a residual block, the natural gate is \(s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)\), which changes the identity obstruction to a zero obstruction.
3. **Rule on the \(t\)-conditioning fork.** State in writing whether fixed non-learned depth encoding is permitted.
4. **Retrieve the internal reports.** Map `2026-08-25_bdh-continual-learning-plan_v2.md` and `2026-08-25_cl-h1-report.md` onto exactly one formal variant: depth-agnostic, \(t\)-conditioned, or state-conditioned.
5. **Define controllability formally** as reachability of finite task targets by the gate class on the instantiated BDH map.
6. **Measure** (a) the Lipschitz constant of the gated BDH block, and (b) cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in \(\phi\)-space. These are the only claimed barriers that can be tested empirically.

**Bottom line.** A strict input-only gate/projection on a frozen shared sequential composition does not provide a proven general solution. The obstruction is not a single theorem but a map of four necessary failure conditions and one open reachability problem. The honest conclusion for publication is: *global preservation makes nontrivial gating impossible; asymmetric continual learning with a frozen shared path remains open pending concrete reachability analysis of the actual BDH update.*

[STRATEGIST Round 5]
# Final Strategic Assessment: Input-Path Interference in BDH via Gating/Projection

**Date:** 2026-08-25 | **Status:** Complete (4-round debate, adjudicated)

---

## Executive Summary

Under the strict constraints — depth-agnostic input-dependent gate/projection, frozen shared path, no per-depth trainable parameters, no retraining — **exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible only under the degenerate global-preservation reading** (the gate must be identity everywhere, doing nothing). Under the **continual-learning-relevant asymmetric reading** (exact preservation on old tasks, free on new tasks), the problem is **open**: no general impossibility *or* possibility theorem has been established. What has been established is a precise map of four independent necessary conditions, each capable of killing the program, plus one decisive open conjecture (reachability) whose resolution determines feasibility.

---

## 1. Formal Definitions

### 1.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let $V = \mathbb{R}^d$ be the state space. A **depth-$D$ shared sequential composition** is defined by a fixed map $F: V \to V$ and the recurrence:

$$s_0(x) = x, \quad s_{t+1}(x) = F(s_t(x)), \quad t = 0, \ldots, D-1$$

The unperturbed trajectory is $\tau_F(x) = (s_0(x), s_1(x), \ldots, s_D(x))$.

If BDH uses residual connections (likely, per the BDH-GPU architecture), the relevant recurrence is:

$$s_{t+1}(x) = s_t(x) + F(s_t(x))$$

**Classification:** Direct characterization of BDH's weight-tied recurrent depth, corroborated by BDH-MLX ("Same weights reused in all layers (recurrent depth)") and BDH-GPU descriptions ("ReLU-Lowrank residual like $x \leftarrow \ldots$"). Instantiation with the literal BDH update from `paper.tex` remains mandatory.

### 1.2 Frozen Shared Path

$F$ is **frozen** if $\partial F / \partial \theta = 0$ during the continual-learning phase. Only the gate/projection mechanism $\phi$ may be trained.

**Classification:** Known — standard continual learning constraint.

### 1.3 Input-Path Interference: Three Precise Notions

**(A) Cross-input forward-pass contamination.** With frozen $\theta$ and a stateless input-conditioned mechanism, each input's forward pass is computationally independent. This notion is **structurally zero** and not a live channel.

**(B) Forward depth-aliasing.** A depth-agnostic gate $g(x)$ applies the same modulation at every depth. If an oracle would apply $g^*(x,t)$ (varying with depth), the depth-aliasing error is:

$$\mathrm{DA}(g; x) = \sum_{t=0}^{D-1} \left\| \Phi(g(x), s_t(x)) - \Phi(g^*(x,t), s_t(x)) \right\|$$

**(C) Training-time gradient interference (cross-task forgetting).** Because gate parameters $\phi$ are shared across all inputs, updating $\phi$ on task $B$ may change $g_\phi$ on task-$A$ inputs. First-order damage is proportional to $\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_A, \nabla_\phi \mathcal{L}_B \rangle$.

**The live channels are (B) and (C).** Notion (A) is dead under the stated assumptions.

**Classification:** The depth-aliasing notion is **apparently novel** as a packaged definition; components are known from control theory. The gradient notion is **known** from continual learning.

### 1.4 Gating

An **input-dependent gate** $g: X \to \mathbb{R}^d$ is applied identically at every depth:

- Output gating: $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$
- Input gating: $s_{t+1} = F(g(x) \odot s_t)$
- Delta gating (for residual blocks): $s_{t+1} = s_t + g(x) \odot F(s_t)$

The gate has **no per-depth parameters**: the same $g(x)$ is used at every $t$.

**Classification:** Known mechanism (LSTM, MoE), but the depth-independence + frozen-path combination is novel.

### 1.5 Projection

An **input-dependent projection** $P: X \to \Pi(V)$ maps each input to an orthogonal projection, applied as $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$ or $s_{t+1} = F(P(x) s_t)$.

**Classification:** Known (gradient projection methods), formalized for forward-pass application.

### 1.6 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a task family $\{(S_i, y_i)\}_{i=1}^K$ if: (1) each task output is produced exactly; (2) zero forward depth-aliasing error; (3) zero first-order cross-task gradient interference.

**Classification:** **Apparently novel** — this specific formulation combining shared composition, frozen parameters, and forward-pass isolation does not appear in surveyed literature.

### 1.7 Elimination vs. Bounding vs. Controllability

- **Elimination:** Exact equality of outputs and exact zero interference.
- **Bounding:** Deviation $\leq \varepsilon$, controlled by Lipschitz constant, depth, gate error, parameter count.
- **Controllability:** Reachability of finite task targets by the gate class without modifying $F$.

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation of approximation theory and control-theoretic concepts.

---

## 2. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses (Final Adjudicated Versions)

### V1. Identity-Forcing Under Global Preservation

**Assumptions:** $F$ defined on open $U \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$; depth-independent output gating $s_{t+1} = g(x) \odot F(s_t)$; exact trajectory preservation on input set $S$: $\tilde{s}_t(x) = F^t(x)$ for all $x \in S$, all $t$.

**Claim:** For every $x \in S$ and every coordinate $i$: $g_i(x) = 1$ whenever $\exists t: F_i(s_t(x)) \neq 0$.

**Proof:** At depth $t$: $g(x) \odot F(s_t(x)) = F(s_t(x))$ implies $(g_i(x) - 1) F_i(s_t(x)) = 0$ componentwise. $\square$

**Delta-gate variant:** For residual delta gating $s_{t+1} = s_t + g(x) \odot F(s_t)$, exact preservation forces $g_i(x) F_i(s_t(x)) = 0$. The gate is **zero-forced** on active coordinates, not identity-forced.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** Near-tautological under global preservation. Its real content is *locative*: it identifies precisely where gate freedom lives — never-active coordinates and non-$S$ inputs.

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### V2. Asymmetric Preservation: The CL-Relevant Constraint

**Assumptions:** Old-task inputs $S_{\text{old}}$; new-task inputs $S_{\text{new}}$; output gating; exact preservation required only on $S_{\text{old}}$.

**Claim:** For $x \in S_{\text{old}}$: $g_i(x) = 1$ whenever $\exists t: F_i(s_t(x)) \neq 0$. On the zero-coordinate set $Z(x) = \{i: F_i(s_t(x)) = 0 \; \forall t\}$, the gate is unconstrained. On $S_{\text{new}}$, the gate is entirely unconstrained.

**Proof:** Same componentwise argument as V1, restricted to $S_{\text{old}}$. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** If old-task trajectories have support in all coordinates, the gate is forced to identity on all of $S_{\text{old}}$. If old and new inputs are disjoint and the gate network can interpolate, exact isolation is *not* ruled out by this obstruction alone.

**Classification:** Direct consequence; the asymmetric framing is the correct CL formalization.

---

### V3. Identifiability: Overlapping Inputs Are Fatal

**Assumptions:** The mechanism is a deterministic function of the input: $\tilde{s}_D(x; \phi) = \mathcal{M}_\phi(x)$. Two tasks $A, B$ share an input $x \in S_A \cap S_B$ with different targets: $y_A(x) \neq y_B(x)$.

**Claim:** No single $\phi$ can satisfy both tasks.

**Proof:** The map is a function: one input, one output. Two different required outputs are incompatible. $\square$

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.** The sharpest structural limit of "input-dependent only," independent of depth, nonlinearity, and capacity.

**Classification:** Direct consequence; essentially an identifiability condition.

---

### V4. Depth-Agnostic Gate-Value Limitation (Downgraded)

**Assumptions:** $\Phi(g,s)$ injective in $g$ along the trajectory; ideal modulation $g^*(x,t)$ nonconstant in $t$.

**Claim:** No depth-independent gate $g(x)$ can reproduce the nonconstant gate-value sequence $g^*(x,t)$.

**Proof:** Equality at every depth gives $g(x) = g^*(x,t)$ for every $t$, contradicting nonconstancy. $\square$

**Critical correction:** This is a statement about **gate values**, not about **reachable input-output maps**. A constant-in-depth gate can produce depth-varying effective behavior because the state $s_t$ changes.

**Counterexample:** $F(s) = s$, $D = 2$. Output gating: $s_2 = g(x) \odot g(x) \odot x$. Constant gate $g(x) = (\alpha, \beta)$ produces $(\alpha^2 x_1, \beta^2 x_2)$. Depth-varying sequence $g^*_0 = (c,1)$, $g^*_1 = (1,c)$ produces $(cx_1, cx_2)$, also achievable by constant gate $(\sqrt{c}, \sqrt{c})$.

**Verdict:** **Representational lemma TRUE; behavioral impossibility NOT established.** The reachability question is open.

**Classification:** Direct consequence for the representational part; the broad behavioral impossibility is **false as stated**.

---

### V5. Projection Rank Condition

**Assumptions:** Output projection $s_{t+1} = P(x) F(s_t)$; exact preservation of unperturbed trajectory required.

**Claim:** If $m(x) = \dim \operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)), \ldots, F(s_{D-1}(x))\}$, then $\operatorname{rank} P(x) \geq m(x)$.

**Proof:** $P(x) F(s_t(x)) = F(s_t(x))$ forces $F(s_t(x)) \in \operatorname{Im} P(x)$. $\square$

**Input-projection caveat:** For input projection $s_{t+1} = F(P(x)s_t)$, exact preservation means $F(P(x)s_t) = F(s_t)$. This implies $P(x)s_t = s_t$ only if $F$ is injective along the trajectory.

**Withdrawn corollary:** The capacity corollary $\sum_i m_i \leq d$ is **WITHDRAWN**. Orthogonality of image subspaces is a forward-pass geometric property; cross-task gradient interference is a property of the parameterization. These are different mathematical objects.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact linear-algebra theorem.**

**Classification:** Direct consequence.

---

### V6. Gradient Interference Parameter Budget

**Assumptions:** Gate has $p$ trainable parameters $\phi$; task Jacobians $J_i \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times p}$; zero first-order interference required for *all smooth losses*.

**Claim:** Row spaces $\{R_i\}$ must be mutually orthogonal: $\sum_i \dim R_i \leq p$.

**Proof:** For arbitrary loss functions, vanishing inner products force row-space orthogonality. $\square$

**Important caveats:**
1. For fixed specific losses, zero interference is weaker (can hold accidentally).
2. If $p \gg d$ (typical for LLM-scale gates), the bound is non-binding.
3. Worst-case guarantee, not typical-case behavior.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem under stated generality.** Standard parameter-isolation accounting.

**Classification:** Direct consequence.

---

### V7. Error Amplification Bound

**Assumptions:** Gated transition $L$-Lipschitz in state; per-depth gate error $\leq \delta$.

**Claim:**

$$\|s_D(x) - s_D^*(x)\| \leq \delta \sum_{k=0}^{D-1} L^k = \begin{cases} \delta \frac{1 - L^D}{1 - L} & L < 1 \\ \delta D & L = 1 \\ \delta \frac{L^D - 1}{L - 1} & L > 1 \end{cases}$$

**Precise interpretation:**
- For **fixed finite depth $D$**: bound exists for every finite $L$.
- **Uniform-in-depth** bound exists iff $L < 1$.

**Empirical caveat:** "Trained LLM-scale maps have $L > 1$" is an **empirical conjecture**, not a theorem.

**Verdict:** **True as an exact theorem.**

**Classification:** Known / direct consequence.

---

### V8. Finite-Task Reachability (Conditional)

**Assumptions:** Finite task set $(x_i, y_i)$; gate family has sufficient capacity; each target reachable: $y_i(x) \in \mathcal{R}_D(x) := \{\tilde{s}_D(x; \phi) : \phi \in \Phi\}$; overlapping inputs have agreeing targets.

**Claim:** There exists $\phi$ realizing all finite task targets exactly.

**Counterexamples showing underdetermination:**
- $F(s) = 0$: $\mathcal{R}_D(x) = \{0\}$ for every $x$, regardless of gating.
- Same input, different targets: V3 kills it.
- $F(s) = s$, $D = 2$, multiplicative output gating: reachable set is $\{g(x) \odot g(x) \odot x\}$; sign/root obstructions limit reachability.

**Verdict:** **Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined.** The reachability assumption is the nontrivial crux.

**Classification:** Plausible adaptation; reachability is a **conjecture** unless proved for specific $F$.

---

| V1 | Global preservation forces identity | TRUE — exact theorem; near-tautological |
