| V3 | Overlapping inputs with different targets | TRUE — exact theorem; identifiability |
| V4 | Depth-agnostic gate-value limitation | Representational TRUE; behavioral FALSE/underdetermined |
| V5 | Projection rank condition | TRUE — exact linear algebra; capacity corollary withdrawn |
| V6 | Gradient interference parameter budget | TRUE — exact under stated generality |
| V7 | Error amplification bound | TRUE — exact; finite-$D$ vs uniform-$D$ corrected |
| V8 | Finite-task reachability | CONDITIONAL; reachability is conjecture |

---

## 4. Prior Art Analysis

| Prior Art | Same Mathematical Problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|
| **Orthogonal gradient methods** (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | Partly — addresses gradient interference in parameter space; not forward depth-aliasing; usually modifies main network | Known, partially relevant |
| **Parameter isolation / masking** (PackNet, Supermasks) | No — per-task masks/parameters; constraint-violating | Known, related but different |
| **Progressive networks / PathNet** | No — per-task architectural growth | Known, different problem |
| **Hypernetworks / fast weight programmers** | Partly — can generate input-dependent modulations; typically per-layer outputs | Known, partially relevant |
| **FiLM** (feature-wise linear modulation) | Partly — same gating algebra, but usually per-layer parameters | Known, directly relevant |
| **LSTM / GRU** | **Closest** — shared-parameter gates at every time step; crucially state-dependent, not input-only | Known, nearest prior art |
| **Mixture of Experts** | No — per-layer routers, distinct experts | Known, structurally similar |
| **Reservoir computing / echo state networks** | Partly — frozen recurrent core + trained readout | Known, relevant background |
| **Universal Transformer / ALBERT** | Partly — weight-tied depth, no task isolation | Known, background context |
| **Nonlinear RNN reachability / observability** | Yes for the reachability question | Known, should be used for V8 |

**No surveyed prior art solves the exact problem:** isolation within one frozen weight-tied composition via an input-only gate without per-depth trainable parameters.

---

## 5. Obstruction Characterization

There is no single obstruction theorem in the CL-relevant setting. The failure surface is a **conjunction of four necessary conditions**, each independently capable of killing the program, none individually fatal:

1. **Identifiability failure** (V3): Conflicting targets on overlapping inputs are unservable by any input-only mechanism. In language-modeling CL, supports overlap extensively. Escape requires context-sufficiency or task conditioning.

2. **Preservation-geometry confinement** (V1/V2): The gate is pinned to identity (output gating) or zero (delta gating) on every (old input, active coordinate) pair. Freedom survives only on never-active coordinates (substantial under sparse activations) and on new-task inputs.

3. **Gradient coupling of shared $\phi$** (V6): New-task updates generically move $g_\phi$ off identity on old inputs. Worst-case immunity costs a rank budget that is either restrictive ($p \leq d$) or vacuous ($p \gg d$).

4. **Depth amplification** (V7): Under expansive gated dynamics, per-depth modulation error grows geometrically in $D$. Bounding demands architectural contractivity — an assumption on $F$, not a capability of the gate.

Plus three **scope conditionals**: (a) statelessness of the mechanism; (b) the $t$-conditioning interpretation; (c) BDH's native Hebbian plasticity actually disabled.

---

## 6. The Pivotal Scope Decision: $t$-Conditioning

A shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ where $t$ is a **fixed, non-trainable encoding** (e.g., sinusoidal) adds $O(1)$ parameters and **does not violate "no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters" as literally worded**. This dissolves V1's depth-agnostic restrictions and V4's residue entirely, converting the problem into pure reachability (V8).

A **learned depth embedding** adds $O(D)$ parameters and violates the constraint outright.

Every impossibility-flavored statement applies only on the strict side of this fork. **The team must rule before any experiment is interpreted.**

---

## 7. Constructive Directions (all ≤ conjecture)

- **C1 — Orthogonal gradient on $\phi$-space:** Project new-task gradients onto the orthogonal complement of old-task gradient subspace in $\phi$-space, holding $g_\phi|_{S_{\text{old}}} \approx \mathbf{1}$ to first order. Classification: **plausible adaptation** of GEM/GPM.

- **C2 — Fixed-encoding $t$-conditioning** ($O(1)$ parameters): dissolves depth-agnostic restrictions; legality contingent on scope ruling.

- **C3 — Delta-gate placement** for residual BDH: gate $g(x) \odot \Delta(s_t)$ inside $s_{t+1} = s_t + \cdot$, never the residual stream.

- **C4 — Sparse-coordinate freedom:** exploit never-active coordinates of old-task trajectories (plausible under ReLU sparsity) as legal modification space.

---

## 8. Novelty Ledger (Final, Adjudicated)

| Claim | Classification |
|-------|---------------|
| Problem formulation: isolation inside frozen weight-tied composition via input-only gate, CL-framed, two-channel interference taxonomy | **Apparently novel** (packaging only) |
| V1, V2, V3, V5, V6, V7 | **Direct consequences** (textbook ingredients) |
| V4 representational lemma | Direct consequence; behavioral version **false as stated** |
| Necessity of state/depth information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice) |
| V8 reachability for concrete BDH $F$ | **Conjecture** |
| Expansiveness of trained BDH blocks | **Empirical conjecture** |

**Contribution posture:** a new formalization and a clean map of elementary obstructions — not new mathematics.

---

## 9. Direct Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without introducing per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic Status |
|---------|---------|-----------------|
| **Elimination**, global-preservation | Trivially impossible — only admissible gate is identity/zero on active coordinates | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL | **Open** — necessary conditions established, no general impossibility or possibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite $L$; uniform-in-depth iff $L < 1$ | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | **Not yet well-posed** — requires formal reachability definition and BDH instantiation | Undefined pending instantiation |

**The failure condition is met only in the degenerate reading.** In the CL-relevant reading, the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture (reachability).*

---

## 10. Mandatory Follow-Ups

1. **Instantiate $F$** from `paper.tex`: fix the literal update (residual form, Hebbian/multiplicative terms, normalization), choose gate placement (C3), verify native plasticity is disabled.
2. **Rule on the $t$-conditioning fork** in writing.
3. **Define controllability formally** as reachability of finite task targets by the gate class on the instantiated $F$.
4. **Retrieve internal reports**; map the implemented mechanism onto exactly one formal variant; reconcile `cl-h1-report` outcomes strictly as tests of V7/V8 conjectures.
5. **Measurement program:** empirical Lipschitz/expansiveness of the gated BDH block (tests V7's conjecture); empirical cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in $\phi$-space (tests whether V6's worst case binds).

[CRITIC Round 5]
## Final Critical Review: BDH Input-Path Isolation Debate

### Overall Assessment

The analysis improved materially across rounds. Its main correction is the right one: the strong claim “exact elimination is impossible in general” has been dissolved into a **scoped set of exact necessary conditions plus an open reachability conjecture**. The separation of three distinct meanings of “interference” is the most useful contribution, and the final verdict table is epistemically honest.

That said, the analysis is **not yet grounded in the actual BDH mechanism**. It operates on an abstraction \(s_{t+1} = F(s_t)\) or a residual variant, but does not instantiate \(F\) from `paper.tex`. Consequently, several theorem statements are placement-sensitive, and the most BDH-relevant placement — **delta gating of a residual block** — was only partially treated.

---

## Strengths Worth Preserving

1. **Interference taxonomy.**  
   The separation of
   - cross-input forward-pass contamination,
   - forward depth-aliasing,
   - training-time cross-task gradient interference  
   is correct and necessary. Under a frozen, stateless, input-dependent mechanism, cross-input forward contamination is structurally zero. The live channels are depth-aliasing and cross-task parameter updating.

2. **Rejection of the naive diagonalization impossibility.**  
   The claim that a depth-agnostic gate is confined to the diagonal of the modulation space was correctly downgraded. A constant-in-depth gate can still produce depth-varying effective behavior because the state changes. The counterexample with \(F(s)=s\) is decisive.

3. **Withdrawal of the projection capacity corollary.**  
   The rank condition  
   \[
   \operatorname{rank} P(x) \ge m(x)
   \]
   is correct for output projection. The stronger cross-task capacity bound \(\sum_i m_i \le d\) was correctly withdrawn. It conflated forward-pass subspace geometry with gradient-parameter geometry.

4. **Correct scoping of the main claim.**  
   Exact elimination is proved impossible only in the degenerate global-preservation reading. In the continual-learning-relevant asymmetric setting, the question is not resolved either way.

---

## Remaining Weaknesses and Gaps

### 1. The actual BDH equations were never instantiated

This is the largest remaining defect. The analysis repeatedly acknowledges that `paper.tex` was not parsed. Public descriptions suggest a BDH-GPU structure such as

\[
x \leftarrow \operatorname{ReLU}\bigl(x + \text{low-rank FFN}(x)\bigr),
\]

with shared parameters, normalization, and possibly Hebbian-style multiplicative terms.

This matters because:

- **Delta gating changes the preservation obstruction.**  
  For full output gating \(s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t)\), preservation forces \(g_i(x)=1\) on active coordinates.  
  For residual delta gating \(s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)\), preservation forces  
  \[
  g_i(x)F_i(s_t(x)) = 0,
  \]
  so the gate is pinned to **zero**, not identity, on active coordinates.

- **Input versus output projection.**  
  The projection rank theorem does not automatically extend from output projection to input projection without injectivity assumptions on \(F\).

- **Statefulness.**  
  If the frozen path contains normalization with running statistics, the mechanism is not stateless, which can revive cross-input forward contamination.

- **Hebbian/plastic components.**  
  If BDH includes native plastic terms, “freezing” the path is an architectural change that must be verified.

### 2. Internal reports were not reconciled

The plan and H1 report were not retrieved. Without them:

- the implemented gate placement is unknown,
- the empirical outcomes of `cl-h1-report.md` are neither explained nor refuted,
- the implementation cannot be mapped to a formal variant.

This is a defect of the current analysis, not merely an optional future step.

### 3. “Controllability” is still not fully formalized

The final definition — reachability of finite task targets by the gate class — is a reasonable start. But the control structure is unusual: an input-dependent gate \(g(x)\) is fixed for the entire depth trajectory, so per-input reachable sets are parameterized by a single vector, not by \(D\) independent controls.

A complete formalization should specify:

- the state space,
- the admissible gate range,
- the exact transition rule,
- the target map class,
- and the requirement that one parameter vector \(\phi\) simultaneously realize all tasks.

Until then, “controllability reduces to reachability” is only a promissory note.

### 4. The \(t\)-conditioning fork remains unresolved

The pivotal scope decision is still open:

- a fixed, non-learnable depth encoding adds \(O(1)\) trainable parameters and likely does not violate the constraint,
- a learned depth embedding adds \(O(D)\) trainable parameters and violates it.

Every impossibility-flavored statement holds only on the strict depth-agnostic side. Future write-ups must label assumptions explicitly.

### 5. The gradient-interference bound is not specific to shared composition

The bound

\[
\sum_i \dim R_i \le p
\]

is standard parameter-isolation accounting. It is correct only under the strong requirement of zero first-order interference for all smooth losses. For fixed losses, interference can vanish accidentally. More importantly, if \(p \gg d\), the bound is often non-binding. It should not be presented as a decisive shared-sequential-composition obstruction until actual Jacobian ranks are measured.

### 6. The overlapping-input theorem needs practical nuance

Same-input-different-target impossibility is mathematically true. But its practical force depends on whether task identity is represented in the input context. Exact input collisions and distribution-level overlap should not be conflated.

---

## Final Scoped Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

| Reading | Verdict | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Elimination**, global preservation on all inputs | Trivially impossible: the only admissible gate is inert on active coordinates | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL: preserve \(S_{\text{old}}\), free \(S_{\text{new}}\) | **Open.** Necessary conditions established; no general impossibility or possibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite Lipschitz \(L\); uniform-in-depth bounding requires \(L<1\) | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | Not yet well-posed without reachability analysis of the concrete frozen BDH map | Undefined pending instantiation |

The failure condition in the brief is met only in the **degenerate global-preservation reading**. In the meaningful continual-learning setting, the correct conclusion is:

> *Not proven impossible; not proven possible. The question reduces to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture — reachability of the frozen BDH map under the allowed gate class.*

---

| Two-channel interference taxonomy in a frozen weight-tied path | Apparently novel as packaging; ingredients known |

No experimental result from the implementation reports was used as evidence for any theorem.

## Required Next Steps Before Publication

1. **Parse `paper.tex`** and instantiate the literal BDH update, including residual form, normalization, and Hebbian or multiplicative terms.
2. **Re-prove every theorem for the actual gate placement**, especially residual delta gating.
3. **Rule formally on \(t\)-conditioning**: fixed non-learned depth encoding versus learned depth embedding.

[OPTIMIZER Round 5]
# Input-Path Interference in BDH via Input-Dependent Gating/Projection: A Scoped Formal Analysis

**Date:** 2026-08-25  
**Status:** Final, after adversarial review  

---

## 1. Scope, Abstraction, and Evidence Base

BDH (arXiv:2509.26507) is a scale-free, biologically inspired network of locally interacting neuron particles with **shared parameters across depth** (“recurrent depth”), corroborated by third-party implementations and public descriptions. The literal BDH update equations from `paper.tex` and the internal project reports were not retrievable within this analysis. Consequently, the formal results below are proved for the abstraction that the research question itself assumes:

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)),\qquad t=0,\dots,D-1,
\]

with the residual variant

\[
s_{t+1}(x)=s_t(x)+F(s_t(x))
\]

treated where explicitly stated. All theorem-shaped claims are about this abstraction; they become theorems about BDH only after \(F\) is replaced by the concrete BDH update and the gate placement is fixed. This instantiation is a mandatory follow-up, not an optional refinement.

No experimental observation from any implementation report is used as evidence for any theorem.

---

## 2. Formal Definitions

### 2.1 Shared Sequential Composition

Let \(V=\mathbb{R}^d\) be the state space and \(F:V\to V\) a fixed map. A **depth-\(D\) shared sequential composition** is

\[
s_0(x)=x,\qquad s_{t+1}(x)=F(s_t(x)).
\]

The unperturbed trajectory is \(\tau_F(x)=(s_0(x),\dots,s_D(x))\).

### 2.2 Frozen Shared Path

\(F\) is **frozen** if its parameters are not updated during the continual-learning phase:

\[
\frac{\partial F}{\partial \theta}=0.
\]

Only the gate/projection parameters \(\phi\) are trainable.

### 2.3 Gating

An **input-dependent gate** is a map \(g:X\to\mathbb{R}^d\) applied identically at every depth. Canonical placements:

- **Output gating:** \(\Phi(g,s)=g(x)\odot F(s)\)
- **Input gating:** \(\Phi(g,s)=F(g(x)\odot s)\)
- **Delta gating** (for residual blocks): \(\Phi(g,s)=s+g(x)\odot F(s)\)

The gate has **no per-depth trainable parameter vectors**: the same function \(g_\phi\) is used at every depth. A relaxed variant \(g_\phi(x,t)\), where the depth index is supplied as an input, is considered separately:

- **Fixed non-trainable depth encoding:** adds \(O(1)\) trainable parameters; does not violate the constraint as literally worded.
- **Learned depth embedding:** adds \(O(D)\) trainable parameters; violates the constraint.

### 2.4 Projection

An **input-dependent projection** is a map \(P:X\to\Pi(V)\) applied as

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t)
\quad\text{or}\quad
s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t).
\]

### 2.5 Input-Path Interference: Three Distinct Notions

The word “interference” is ambiguous. Three notions must be separated.

**(A) Cross-input forward-pass contamination.**  
If \(F\) is frozen and the gate is stateless and a function of \(x\) alone, then the trajectory of \(x_1\) is computationally independent of the presence of \(x_2\):

\[
\tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1\mid x_2\text{ processed elsewhere})=\tilde s_t^{(M)}(x_1).
\]

This notion is **structurally zero** in this setting. It becomes live only if the mechanism contains stateful components (running statistics, caches, etc.).

**(B) Forward depth-aliasing.**  
A depth-agnostic gate applies the same modulation at every depth. If an oracle with access to depth \(t\) would apply a reference modulation \(g^*(x,t)\), the depth-aliasing error is

\[
\mathrm{DA}(g;x)=
\sum_{t=0}^{D-1}
\left\|
\Phi(g(x),s_t(x))-\Phi(g^*(x,t),s_t(x))
\right\|.
\]

This is a representational mismatch within a single forward pass.

**(C) Training-time gradient interference.**  
The gate parameters \(\phi\) are shared across all inputs. Updating \(\phi\) on task \(B\) may change gate outputs on task-\(A\) inputs. First-order damage is proportional to

\[
\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_A(\phi),\; \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_B(\phi)\rangle.
\]

The operationally relevant channels for continual learning are **(B)** and **(C)**. Channel **(A)** is dead under a frozen, stateless, input-only mechanism.

### 2.6 Computation-Isolability

A mechanism is **exactly computation-isolating** for a task family \(\{(S_i,y_i)\}\) if:

1. task outputs are produced exactly: \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\) for all \(x\in S_i\);
2. forward depth-aliasing error is zero on the specified reference trajectories;
3. first-order cross-task gradient interference is zero:  
   \(\langle \nabla_\phi \mathcal L_i,\nabla_\phi \mathcal L_j\rangle=0\) for all \(i\neq j\).

It **bounds interference** if all relevant errors are at most \(\varepsilon\), with \(\varepsilon\) controlled by explicit quantities.

### 2.7 Elimination, Bounding, Controllability

- **Elimination** means exact task outputs and exact zero interference.
- **Bounding** means a finite worst-case deviation \(\varepsilon\), controlled by Lipschitz constants, depth, gate error, and capacity.
- **Controllability** is formalized as **reachability**: a target function \(y_i:S_i\to V\) is reachable by the gate class if there exists \(\phi\) such that \(\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=y_i(x)\) for all \(x\in S_i\); the mechanism is controllable over a finite task sequence if every finite set of reachable tasks with compatible overlaps is simultaneously realizable by a single \(\phi\).

This is the natural control-theoretic reading: because the gate is input-only, the control \(g(x)\) is fixed for the entire depth trajectory, rather than an independently chosen per-step control \(u_t\).

---

## 3. Theorem-Shaped Hypotheses

### T1. Exact Preservation Forces the Gate to Be Inert on Active Coordinates

**Assumptions.** \(F\) is defined on an open set \(U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^d\). The mechanism is a depth-agnostic output gate:

\[
s_{t+1}=g(x)\odot F(s_t).
\]

Exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory is required on an input set \(S\):

\[
\tilde s_t(x)=F^t(x)\qquad\forall x\in S,\ \forall t.
\]

**Claim.** For every \(x\in S\) and every coordinate \(i\),

\[
(g_i(x)-1)\,F_i(s_t(x))=0.
\]

If coordinate \(i\) is active at any depth along the trajectory, then \(g_i(x)=1\). If \(F\) is componentwise nonzero along all trajectories in \(S\), then \(g(x)=\mathbf 1\) on \(S\).

**Delta-gate variant.** For residual delta gating,

\[
s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t),
\]

exact preservation forces

\[
g_i(x)\,F_i(s_t(x))=0.
\]

Thus the gate is **zero-forced** on active coordinates, not identity-forced. This is the placement likely relevant to BDH’s residual structure.

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem. Near-tautological under global preservation; its real content is *locative*: it identifies precisely where gate freedom survives.

**Classification.** Direct consequence.

---

### T2. Asymmetric Preservation: The Continual-Learning-Relevant Setting

**Assumptions.** Old-task inputs \(S_{\text{old}}\), new-task inputs \(S_{\text{new}}\). Exact preservation is required only on \(S_{\text{old}}\). No preservation constraint is imposed on \(S_{\text{new}}\).

**Claim.** On \(S_{\text{old}}\), the gate is pinned to identity (output gating) or zero (delta gating) on every coordinate active along the old trajectory. On never-active coordinates, and on all of \(S_{\text{new}}\), the gate is unconstrained by preservation.

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem. This is the correct formalization of the continual-learning constraint. The identity/zero obstruction does **not** by itself rule out nontrivial isolation when old and new task inputs are separated and the gate can interpolate.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; the asymmetric framing is the honest CL formalization.

---

### T3. Identifiability: Same Input, Different Target Is Impossible

**Assumptions.** The mechanism is a deterministic function of the input:

\[
\tilde s_D(x;\phi)=\mathcal M_\phi(x).
\]

Two tasks share an input \(x\in S_A\cap S_B\) and demand different outputs: \(y_A(x)\neq y_B(x)\).

**Claim.** No single \(\phi\) can satisfy both tasks.

**Proof.** A function returns one output for one input; two different required outputs are incompatible. ∎

**Verdict.** True as an exact theorem. It is the sharpest structural limit of an input-only mechanism, independent of depth, capacity, or nonlinearity.

**Practical nuance.** In language continual learning, the force of this theorem depends on whether task identity is encoded in the input context. Exact input collisions and distribution-level overlap should not be conflated.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; essentially an identifiability condition.

---

### T4. Depth-Agnostic Gate Values: Representational Limit, Not Behavioral Impossibility

**Assumptions.** \(\Phi(g,s)\) is injective in the gate argument \(g\) along the trajectory. An oracle demands a depth-varying gate-value sequence \(g^*(x,0),\dots,g^*(x,D-1)\), nonconstant in \(t\).

**Claim.** A depth-agnostic gate \(g(x)\) cannot reproduce the exact gate-value sequence \(g^*(x,t)\).

**Proof.** If it did, injectivity would force \(g(x)=g^*(x,t)\) for every \(t\), contradicting nonconstancy. ∎

**Important correction.** This is a statement about **gate values**, not about **reachable input–output behavior**. Because the state changes across depth, a constant gate can produce depth-varying effective behavior.

**Counterexample.** Let \(F(s)=s\), \(D=2\). Output gating gives

\[
s_2=g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x.
\]

A constant gate \((\alpha,\beta)\) produces \((\alpha^2x_1,\beta^2x_2)\). A depth-varying sequence \(g^*_0=(c,1), g^*_1=(1,c)\) produces \((cx_1,cx_2)\), which the constant gate \((\sqrt c,\sqrt c)\) reproduces.

**Verdict.** The representational lemma is true. The behavioral impossibility is **not established**; it was withdrawn in final adjudication. The reachability question is open.

**Classification.** Direct consequence for the representational part; the broad behavioral claim is **false as stated**.

---

### T5. Projection Mechanisms: Necessary Rank Condition

**Assumptions.** Output projection:

\[
s_{t+1}=P(x)F(s_t).
\]

Exact preservation of the unperturbed trajectory is required for input \(x\).

**Claim.** Let

\[
m(x)=\dim\operatorname{span}\{F(s_0(x)),\dots,F(s_{D-1}(x))\}.
\]

Then

\[
\operatorname{rank}P(x)\ge m(x).
\]

**Proof.** Exact preservation forces \(P(x)F(s_t(x))=F(s_t(x))\) for all \(t\), so all \(F(s_t(x))\) lie in \(\operatorname{Im}P(x)\). ∎

**Input-projection caveat.** For input projection \(s_{t+1}=F(P(x)s_t)\), exact preservation means \(F(P(x)s_t)=F(s_t)\). This implies \(P(x)s_t=s_t\) only if \(F\) is injective along the trajectory. The theorem as stated applies to output projection; the input-projection variant requires the injectivity assumption.

**Withdrawn corollary.** The earlier claim \(\sum_i m_i\le d\) for multiple tasks is **withdrawn**. Image-subspace orthogonality is a forward-pass geometric property; cross-task gradient interference is a property of the parameterization of \(P(x)\). These are different mathematical objects, and the capacity bound does not follow without a special parameterization assumption.

**Classification.** Direct consequence (linear algebra); no isolation capacity bound follows.

---

### T6. Gradient Interference and the Parameter Budget

**Assumptions.** The gate/projection mechanism has \(p\) trainable parameters. For task \(i\), the row space of the collection of output Jacobians is \(R_i\subseteq\mathbb{R}^p\). Zero first-order interference is required for **all smooth losses**.

**Claim.** The row spaces must be mutually orthogonal, and

\[
\sum_i \dim R_i\le p.
\]

**Proof.** For arbitrary losses, zero inner products across all linear functionals force row-space orthogonality; orthogonal subspaces of \(\mathbb{R}^p\) have total dimension at most \(p\). ∎

**Caveats.**

1. For fixed specific losses, zero interference can hold accidentally even when row spaces overlap.  
2. If \(p\gg d\), the bound is likely non-binding for practical LLM-scale gates.  
3. This is standard parameter-isolation accounting, not a result specific to shared sequential composition.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; not a decisive obstruction unless Jacobian ranks are measured.

---

### T7. Error Amplification: Bounding Is Conditional on Dynamics

**Assumptions.** The gated transition is Lipschitz in state with constant \(L\). The per-depth gate error against a reference is at most \(\delta\).

**Claim.**

\[
\|s_D(x)-s_D^*(x)\|
\le
\delta\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}L^k
=
\begin{cases}
\delta\dfrac{1-L^D}{1-L}, & L<1,\\[1ex]
\delta D, & L=1,\\[1ex]
\delta\dfrac{L^D-1}{L-1}, & L>1.
\end{cases}
\]

**Precise interpretation.**

- For **fixed finite depth \(D\)**, a finite bound exists for every finite \(L\).
- A bound that is **uniform in depth** exists iff \(L<1\).
- The claim “trained LLM-scale maps have \(L>1\)” is an **empirical conjecture**, not a theorem, and must be measured for the actual BDH block.

**Classification.** Direct consequence; quantifier-corrected.

---

### T8. Finite-Task Reachability: Conditional Construction, Otherwise Underdetermined

**Assumptions.** The task family is finite: \((x_i,y_i)\), \(i=1,\dots,N\). The gate family can interpolate finite sets. Each target is reachable:

\[
y_i(x)\in\mathcal R_D(x):=\{\tilde s_D(x;\phi):\phi\in\Phi\}.
\]

Overlapping task inputs have agreeing targets.

**Claim.** There exists \(\phi\) realizing all finite task targets exactly.

**Proof sketch.** Reachability gives a finite set of constraints; interpolation capacity fits them. ∎

**Underdetermination examples.**

- If \(F\equiv 0\), the reachable set is \(\{0\}\) for every \(x\), regardless of gating.
- If the same input has different targets, T3 applies.
- If \(F(s)=s\) and \(D=2\) with multiplicative output gating, reachable outputs are of the form \(g(x)\odot g(x)\odot x\), which excludes many targets.

**Verdict.** Conditionally true; otherwise underdetermined. The reachability assumption is the nontrivial crux and remains a **conjecture** for a concrete BDH map.

**Classification.** Plausible adaptation of universal interpolation; reachability is unproved.

---

## 4. Summary of Verdicts

| # | Claim | Status |
|---|-------|--------|
| T1 | Exact preservation forces identity/zero on active coordinates | True; exact theorem |
| T2 | Asymmetric preservation constrains only old-task active coordinates | True; CL-relevant |
| T3 | Same input, different target impossible | True; identifiability |
| T4 | Depth-agnostic gate-value representational limit | Representational true; behavioral impossibility false/underdetermined |
| T5 | Projection rank necessary condition | True; capacity corollary withdrawn |
| T6 | Gradient interference parameter budget | True under worst-case generality; often non-binding |
| T7 | Error amplification bound | True; finite-depth vs uniform-depth corrected |
| T8 | Finite-task reachability | Conditional; reachability is conjecture |

---

## 5. Prior Art: Does It Address the Same Problem?

| Prior art | What it does | Same mathematical problem? | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gradient projection / orthogonal-gradient methods (GEM, A-GEM, GPM) | Project gradients in parameter space during training | Partly: cross-task gradient interference; not forward depth-aliasing on a frozen path | Known, partially relevant |
| Parameter isolation / masks (PackNet, Supermasks, Piggyback) | Allocate disjoint parameter subsets to tasks | No: creates per-task parameters/masks, violating the input-only gate constraint | Known, related but different |
| Progressive networks / PathNet | Add task-specific columns or paths | No: per-task architectural growth | Known, different problem |
| Hypernetworks / fast weight programmers | Generate weights or modulations for a main network | Partly: can generate input-dependent modulations; typically per-layer outputs | Known, partially relevant |
| FiLM / feature-wise linear modulation | Input-dependent affine modulation of hidden features | Partly: same gating algebra, but usually per-layer parameters | Known, directly relevant |
| LSTM / GRU | Shared-parameter state-dependent gates in a recurrent path | Closest: same composition; but gates see the current state, not only the input | Known, nearest prior art |
| Mixture of experts / conditional computation | Input-dependent routing to expert parameter sets | No: per-layer routers and distinct experts | Known, structurally similar |
| Reservoir computing / echo state networks | Fixed random recurrent core plus trained readout | Partly: frozen core; not input-dependent forward gating for task isolation | Known, relevant background |
| Weight-tied transformers (Universal Transformer, ALBERT) | Reuse a block across depth | Partly: weight-tied composition; no task-isolation gating | Known, background context |
| Nonlinear RNN reachability / observability | Characterizes what a recurrent map can reach | Yes for the reachability question | Known, should be applied to T8 |

**Conclusion.** No surveyed prior art solves the exact problem: isolation of computations within a single frozen weight-tied composition using only an input-dependent gate, with no per-depth trainable parameters and no retraining of the shared path.

---

## 6. Novelty Classification

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Formalization of two live interference channels (depth-aliasing and cross-task gradient interference) in a frozen weight-tied path | **Apparently novel** (packaging only) |
| T1–T3, T5–T7 | **Direct consequences** (textbook ingredients) |
| T4 representational lemma | Direct consequence; behavioral version false as stated |
| Necessity of state/depth/context information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice) |
| T8 reachability for concrete BDH \(F\) | **Conjecture** |
| Expansiveness of trained BDH blocks | **Empirical conjecture** |
| Any observed effect in `cl-h1-report` | Inadmissible as theorem evidence; admissible only as a test of the conjectures above |

Contribution posture: **a new formalization and a clean map of elementary obstructions, not new mathematics.**

---

## 7. Obstruction Characterization

The strict proposed mechanism is:

- input-dependent gate/projection,
- no per-depth/per-level trainable parameters,
- frozen shared path,
- no retraining of \(F\).

Under the **global-preservation reading** — the mechanism must preserve \(F\)’s behavior on **all** inputs — the gate is forced to be inert everywhere, so exact elimination is **trivially impossible**.

Under the **asymmetric continual-learning reading** — exact preservation on old-task inputs, free behavior on new-task inputs — no general impossibility or possibility theorem has been established. What is established is a conjunction of four necessary conditions:

1. **Identifiability failure (T3).** Conflicting targets on identical inputs cannot be served by any input-only mechanism. In language modeling, the force of this depends on how much task identity is contained in the input context.
2. **Preservation-geometry confinement (T1/T2).** The gate is pinned to identity (or zero under delta gating) on every active old-task coordinate. Freedom survives only on never-active coordinates and on new-task inputs.
3. **Gradient coupling of shared \(\phi\) (T6).** New-task updates generically move \(g_\phi\) away from the preserved behavior on old inputs. Worst-case immunity costs a rank budget that may be non-binding when \(p\gg d\).
4. **Depth amplification (T7).** If the gated dynamics are expansive, small per-depth gate errors grow geometrically in depth. Bounding then requires contractivity of the composed system — a property of \(F\), not a capability of the gate alone.

Three **scope conditionals** attach to every result:

- **Statelessness.** Any stateful component inside the gate revives cross-input forward contamination.
- **\(t\)-conditioning fork.** A gate \(g_\phi(x,t)\) with a fixed non-trainable depth encoding adds \(O(1)\) parameters and does not violate the constraint as literally worded. A learned depth embedding adds \(O(D)\) parameters and does violate it. The depth-agnostic obstruction dissolves on the permissive side of this fork, converting the problem into pure reachability (T8).
- **Frozen path.** BDH’s native Hebbian-style plasticity must be explicitly disabled for the frozen-path premise to hold.

---

## 8. Direct Answer to the Research Question

> Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic status |
|---|---|---|
| **Elimination**, global preservation on all inputs | Impossible — the only admissible gate is identity/zero on active coordinates | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL (preserve \(S_{\text{old}}\), free on \(S_{\text{new}}\)) | **Open.** Necessary conditions established; no general impossibility or possibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite \(L\); uniform-in-depth bound iff \(L<1\) | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | Not yet well-posed: requires formal reachability analysis on the concrete frozen BDH map with the exact gate class | Undefined pending instantiation |

The failure condition contemplated by the brief is met **only in the degenerate global-preservation reading**. In the continual-learning-relevant reading, the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions and one decisive open conjecture — reachability.*

---

## 9. Mandatory Follow-Ups Before Empirical Reconciliation

1. **Instantiate \(F\) from `paper.tex`.** Fix the literal BDH update, including residual form, LayerNorm placement, and any Hebbian-style multiplicative terms.
2. **Reinstantiate every theorem for the correct gate placement.** For a residual block, the natural gate is \(s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)\), which changes the identity obstruction to a zero obstruction.
3. **Rule on the \(t\)-conditioning fork.** State in writing whether fixed non-learned depth encoding is permitted.
4. **Retrieve the internal reports.** Map `2026-08-25_bdh-continual-learning-plan_v2.md` and `2026-08-25_cl-h1-report.md` onto exactly one formal variant: depth-agnostic, \(t\)-conditioned, or state-conditioned.
5. **Define controllability formally** as reachability of finite task targets by the gate class on the instantiated BDH map.
6. **Measure** (a) the Lipschitz constant of the gated BDH block, and (b) cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in \(\phi\)-space. These are the only claimed barriers that can be tested empirically.

**Bottom line.** A strict input-only gate/projection on a frozen shared sequential composition does not provide a proven general solution. The obstruction is not a single theorem but a map of four necessary failure conditions and one open reachability problem. The honest conclusion for publication is: *global preservation makes nontrivial gating impossible; asymmetric continual learning with a frozen shared path remains open pending concrete reachability analysis of the actual BDH update.*

[MODERATOR Round 5]
# Final Moderator Synthesis — Debate Closure: Input-Path Isolation in BDH via Gating/Projection

**Role:** ox-alpha, moderator. This document consolidates five rounds of adversarial deliberation (STRATEGIST, CRITIC, OPTIMIZER), records the final consensus state, adjudicates all surviving disputes, and delivers the scoped answer to the research question together with the precise characterization of the obstruction.

---

## 0. Process Record and Evidence-Base Status

The debate executed a full correction loop:

1. **Rounds 1–2** built a unified formal skeleton and initially headlined a strong claim: *"exact elimination of input-path interference is impossible in general"* via three obstructions (identity-forcing, diagonalization, rank budget).
2. **Round 3–4 CRITIC reviews** landed decisive defeaters: (i) the headline rested on a *degenerate* global-preservation reading; (ii) the diagonalization argument conflated gate **values** with reachable **behaviors** (counterexample: $F(s)=s$, $D=2$); (iii) the projection capacity corollary $\sum_i m_i \le d$ committed a category error (forward-pass image-subspace geometry ≠ parameter-space gradient geometry); (iv) the amplification "iff $L<1$" misquantified finite-$D$ vs. uniform-in-$D$ bounds; (v) controllability was undefined; (vi) the BDH-relevant **delta-gate placement** converts identity-forcing into **zero-forcing**, changing the obstruction's form.
3. **Round 5**: all three roles converged on the same scoped verdict set (STRATEGIST V1–V8, OPTIMIZER T1–T8 — substantively identical), with the CRITIC accepting the scoping while flagging residual gaps.

**Adjudication principle throughout:** a claim enters the record only at the strength of its weakest surviving proof.

**Evidence-base limitations (recorded for honesty):** the internal project files (`2026-08-*` reports, `paper-parity-plan`, `bdh-continual-learning-plan_v2.md`, `cl-h1-report.md`) and the literal equations from `paper.tex` were **never retrieved in-debate** despite repeated attempts. All results below are therefore proved for the abstraction $s_{t+1}=\Phi(g(x),s_t)$ over a frozen map $F$ (with the residual/delta variant treated explicitly). Instantiation against the actual BDH update remains a mandatory follow-up, not an optional refinement. No experimental observation from any report was admitted as theorem evidence at any point.

---

## 1. Consensus Evaluation

### Full consensus (all three roles, post-revision)

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Formal skeleton: shared sequential composition, frozen path, gate/projection placements (output / input / delta), elimination-vs-bounding distinction | Agreed verbatim |
| **Interference taxonomy (three channels)**: (A) cross-input forward-pass contamination — **structurally zero** for stateless mechanisms on a frozen path; (B) forward **depth-aliasing** within a pass — live; (C) training-time **cross-task gradient interference** through shared $\phi$ — live | Agreed; channel (A)'s revival conditions (stateful components: running statistics, caches) noted |
| Verdicts T1–T3, T5–T8 (see §3) | Agreed |
| Prior-art triage: LSTM/GRU nearest neighbor (state-conditioned gates); FiLM same gating algebra but per-layer parameters; fast-weight programmers/hypernetworks adjacent; reservoir computing background; GEM/A-GEM/GPM parameter-space complementary; PackNet/supermasks/progressive nets constraint-violating; MoE different problem; Universal Transformer/ALBERT backdrop. **No surveyed work solves the exact problem.** | Agreed |
| Novelty discipline: packaging novel, mathematics derivative | Agreed |

### Adjudicated disputes (all resolved)

- **"Impossibility in general" vs. scoped openness** → the **scoped formulation prevails**. The unscoped headline holds only under global preservation on *all* inputs — a reading under which the gate must be inert everywhere and hence cannot adapt anywhere, dissolving the continual-learning question itself. STRATEGIST's own §7 concession is entered as binding.
- **Diagonalization obstruction** → downgraded from behavioral impossibility to a true-but-representational lemma about gate values; superseded by the open reachability question (T8).
- **Projection capacity corollary** → withdrawn; survives only under a special independent-subspace parameterization that violates the spirit of the constraints anyway.
- **Delta-gate placement** → incorporated into T1/T2: under $s_{t+1}=s_t+g(x)\odot F(s_t)$, preservation forces $g_i(x)=0$ (not $1$) on active old-task coordinates. This is the placement likely correct for BDH's residual structure.

### Residual dissent (non-blocking)

The CRITIC maintains two emphasis-level caveats, both accepted into the record: (a) the practical force of T3 depends on measure-theoretic overlap of task supports — exact collision, distributional overlap, and representation-space near-overlap must not be conflated; (b) T6 is worst-case accounting that is likely non-binding when $p \gg d$ and must not be presented as a decisive barrier until Jacobian ranks are measured.

---

## 2. Consolidated Definitions (Final)

- **Shared sequential composition:** $s_0=x$, $s_{t+1}=F(s_t)$, $D$ steps; residual variant $s_{t+1}=s_t+F(s_t)$.
- **Frozen shared path:** $\partial F/\partial\theta = 0$ during adaptation; only $\phi$ (gate parameters) trainable.
- **Gate:** input-dependent $g_\phi(x)$ applied identically at every depth; placements: output $g\odot F(s)$, input $F(g\odot s)$, delta $s+g\odot F(s)$. No per-depth parameter vectors.
- **Projection:** input-dependent orthogonal $P(x)$; output $P(x)F(s)$ or input $F(P(x)s)$.
- **Computation-isolability (exact):** exact task outputs + zero depth-aliasing error + zero first-order cross-task gradient interference.
- **Elimination vs. bounding:** exact equality/zero interference vs. finite worst-case deviation controlled by explicit quantities ($L_\Phi$, $D$, $\delta$, $p$).
- **Controllability (partial formalization):** reachability of finite task targets by the gate class on the frozen map — with the crucial caveat that an input-only control $g(x)$ is fixed for the entire pass (open-loop modulation), not an independent per-step control $u_t$. A fuller formalization specifying state space, admissible gate range, transition rule, target class, and joint-realizability requirements remains outstanding.

---

## 3. Final Adjudicated Verdict Table

| # | Claim | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** | Exact preservation forces the gate **inert** on active coordinates: $g_i=\mathbf{1}$ (output gating) or $g_i=0$ (**delta gating**) wherever $F_i(s_t(x))\neq0$ along the preserved trajectory | **TRUE — exact theorem; direct consequence** | Near-tautological globally; real content is *locative*: identifies precisely where gate freedom lives (never-active coordinates; non-$S$ inputs) |
| **T2** | Under **asymmetric preservation** (exact on $S_{\text{old}}$, free on $S_{\text{new}}$), the confinement restricts to old-task active coordinates | **TRUE — exact theorem; the honest CL formalization** | Does not by itself preclude nontrivial isolation when supports are separable and the gate can interpolate |
| **T3** | Deterministic input-only mechanism cannot serve two tasks demanding different outputs at a shared input | **TRUE — exact theorem (identifiability)** | Sharpest limit of "input-dependent only"; practical force depends on context-sufficiency of inputs |
| **T4** | Depth-agnostic gate confined to diagonal of modulation space | Representational lemma **true**; behavioral impossibility **not established** (downgraded after counterexample) | Superseded by open reachability question |
| **T5** | Output projection preserving trajectory requires $\operatorname{rank}P(x)\ge m(x)$ | **TRUE — exact linear algebra** | Capacity corollary withdrawn; input-projection variant requires injectivity of $F$ along the trajectory |
| **T6** | Zero first-order interference *for all smooth losses* ⟺ mutually orthogonal task-Jacobian row spaces ⟹ $\sum_i \dim R_i \le p$ | **TRUE — exact under stated generality** | Worst-case guarantee; likely non-binding for $p\gg d$; standard accounting, not composition-specific |
| **T7** | $e_D \le \delta\sum_{k<D}L_\Phi^k$; finite-$D$ bound exists for every finite $L_\Phi$; **uniform-in-depth** bound iff $L_\Phi<1$ | **TRUE — exact, quantifiers corrected** | "Trained BDH blocks are expansive" reclassified as empirical conjecture requiring measurement |
| **T8** | Finite task sets realizable iff targets reachable by gate class + interpolation capacity + overlap agreement | **CONDITIONAL; crux is a conjecture** | Counterexamples ($F\equiv0$; sign/root obstructions) show reachability fails generically without hypotheses on $F$ |

---

## 4. Final Answer to the Research Question

> *Can input-path interference arising from shared sequential composition be eliminated, bounded, or made controllable by an input-dependent gate/projection mechanism, without per-depth/per-level trainable parameters and without retraining the frozen shared path?*

| Reading | Verdict | Epistemic status |
|---|---|---|
| **Elimination**, global-preservation (mechanism preserves $F$'s behavior on *all* inputs) | Trivially impossible — the only admissible gate is inert everywhere | Proved; degenerate |
| **Elimination**, asymmetric CL (exact preservation on $S_{\text{old}}$, free on $S_{\text{new}}$) | **OPEN.** Necessary conditions established (T1–T3, T5–T7); no general possibility *or* impossibility theorem | Partially characterized |
| **Bounding** | Finite-depth bounds exist for any finite $L_\Phi$; **uniform-in-depth** bounding iff gated dynamics contractive. Bounding is a property of the composed system, not achievable by gate design alone under expansion | Proved, conditional |
| **Controllability** | **Not yet well-posed**: requires formal reachability analysis on the concrete frozen BDH map with the exact gate class | Undefined pending instantiation |

The brief's failure condition is met **only in the degenerate reading**. In the CL-relevant reading the correct outcome is: *not proven impossible, not proven possible; reduced to identifiable necessary conditions plus one decisive open conjecture (reachability).* Reporting anything stronger would violate the novelty discipline.

---

## 5. Obstruction Characterization (Deliverable Under the Failure Clause)

There is no single obstruction theorem in the meaningful setting. The failure surface is a **conjunction of four necessary conditions**, each independently capable of killing the program, none individually fatal:

1. **Identifiability failure (T3)** — conflicting targets on overlapping inputs are unservable by any input-only mechanism. In language-modeling CL, escape requires context-sufficiency of the input or task conditioning.
2. **Preservation-geometry confinement (T1/T2)** — the gate is pinned to identity (output gating) or zero (delta gating) on every (old input, active coordinate) pair. Freedom survives on never-active coordinates — potentially substantial under BDH's ReLU sparsity — and on new-task inputs.
3. **Gradient coupling of shared $\phi$ (T6)** — new-task updates generically move $g_\phi$ off the preserved behavior on old inputs; worst-case immunity costs a rank budget that is restrictive ($p\le d$) or vacuous ($p\gg d$).
4. **Depth amplification (T7)** — under expansive gated dynamics, per-depth modulation error grows geometrically in $D$; bounding then demands architectural contractivity, an assumption on $F$, not a capability of the gate.

Plus **three scope conditionals** silently underlying every result: (a) **statelessness** of the mechanism (any running-statistics component revives channel A); (b) the **$t$-conditioning fork** — a shared-parameter gate $g_\phi(x,t)$ fed by a *fixed, non-trainable* depth encoding adds $O(1)$ parameters and does not violate the constraint as literally worded, and it dissolves the depth-agnostic restrictions entirely, converting the problem into pure reachability (T8); a *learned* depth embedding adds $O(D)$ parameters and violates the constraint outright. Every impossibility-flavored statement applies only on the strict side of this fork; (c) BDH's native Hebbian-style plasticity must be explicitly disabled, else the path is not frozen.

---

## 6. Novelty Ledger (Final)

| Claim | Classification |
|---|---|
| Problem formulation: isolation inside a frozen weight-tied composition via input-only gate, CL-framed, three-channel interference taxonomy | **Apparently novel** (packaging only) |
| T1–T3, T5–T7 | **Direct consequences** (textbook ingredients) |
| T4 representational lemma | Direct consequence; behavioral version false as stated |
| Necessity of state/depth/context information for effective gating | **Plausible adaptation** (consistent with LSTM/FiLM practice; proven only under injectivity assumptions) |
| Reachability of the concrete BDH map under the gate class (T8 crux) | **Conjecture** |
| Expansiveness of trained BDH blocks | **Empirical conjecture** |
| Any observed effect in `cl-h1-report` | Inadmissible as theorem evidence; admissible only as a test of the above conjectures |

**Write-up posture:** claim a new formalization and a clean map of elementary obstructions — not new mathematics.

---

## 7. Constructive Directions Surfaced (all ≤ conjecture)

- **C1 — Orthogonal-gradient protection of $\phi$:** once the protected object is recognized as the *gate parameters*, GEM/GPM-style projection applies compatibly with all constraints: project new-task gradients onto the orthogonal complement of the old-task gradient subspace in $\phi$-space, holding $g_\phi|_{S_{\text{old}}}\approx$ inert to first order. Plausible adaptation; open statistical question about generalization beyond the old-task sample.
- **C2 — Fixed-encoding $t$-conditioning** ($O(1)$ parameters): dissolves depth-agnostic restrictions; legality contingent on the scope ruling.
- **C3 — Delta-gate placement:** gate $g(x)\odot\Delta(s_t)$ inside $s_{t+1}=s_t+\cdot$; never the residual stream itself.
- **C4 — Sparse-coordinate freedom:** exploit never-active coordinates of old-task trajectories (plausible under ReLU sparsity) as legal modification space even on preserved inputs.

---

## 8. Mandatory Follow-Ups Before Any Empirical Reconciliation

1. **Instantiate $F$ from `paper.tex`**: fix the literal update (residual form, LayerNorm placement, Hebbian/multiplicative terms), choose gate placement (C3), verify plasticity disabling and statelessness.
2. **Re-prove every theorem for the actual placement** — especially the zero-forcing variant under delta gating.
3. **Rule in writing on the $t$-conditioning fork** (fixed non-learned encoding permitted?).
4. **Retrieve and map the internal reports** (`plan_v2`, `cl-h1-report`) onto exactly one formal variant: depth-agnostic / $t$-conditioned / state-conditioned.
5. **Define controllability rigorously** as finite-target reachability over the instantiated map and exact gate class, drawing on nonlinear discrete-time reachability theory.
6. **Measurement program:** empirical Lipschitz/expansiveness of the gated BDH block (tests T7's conjecture); empirical cross-task Jacobian row-space overlap in $\phi$-space (tests whether T6's worst case binds).

---

## 9. Discipline Audit

- No experimental observation cited as evidence for any theorem at any point. ✔
- All overclaims identified were withdrawn on the record (capacity corollary; behavioral diagonalization; finite-$D$ "iff"; global-preservation headline) or rescoped. ✔
- Novelty labels consistent post-adjudication: packaging novel, mathematics derivative. ✔

---

## 10. Closing Statement

**Consensus is complete** on the formal framework, the three-channel interference taxonomy, the exact necessary conditions T1–T3/T5–T7 as scoped, the conditional bounding result, the prior-art triage, and the open status of the asymmetric continual-learning question. The single substantive disagreement — whether a general impossibility had been established — was resolved **against** the stronger claim on the strength-of-weakest-proof principle.

The investigation exits the debate not with an impossibility theorem but with something more useful: a precise map of where gate freedom lives (never-active sparse coordinates, new-task inputs), four independent ways the program can fail, one pivotal scope decision ($t$-conditioning), and one decisive open conjecture — **reachability of the instantiated BDH map under the allowed gate class** — whose resolution will determine whether the proposed mechanism is possible, impossible, or merely unproven.
